# CivitasOne — Enterprise Gap Report

**Generated:** 2026-09-07 · **Base:** `origin/main` @ `e43e28d8` (findings re-checked against later same-day merges #1085, #1086) · **Method:** seven read-only audits, one per enterprise dimension, using the repo's own 21 skills (`.claude/skills/`) as rubrics — mechanical scans across every service and route file for breadth, depth reads on representative modules, every finding verified by reading the code. Nothing was modified by the audits.

**Audience:** this is a work register. Each gap has an ID, a priority, the evidence, the governing skill, the fix, and a definition of done. Agents pick gaps in wave order (§3), claim them by editing the Status column, and close them through the process in §5. Do not "fix" a gap by weakening the check that found it.

---

## 1. Verdict

**Release gate: NOT RELEASABLE.** Three reasons, in order of consequence:

1. **The build is not telling the truth.** Service unit tests have not executed on `main` since 2026-09-06 (a stale migration allow-list aborts CI bootstrap, which cascades into Integration, Coverage and the L3 gate), the push-triggered release gate races CI and has failed 5/5, the CI "evidence audit" job is wrapped in `|| true` while the underlying script reports **BLOCKED, 8/8 required lanes unmeasured**, and the weekly DR drill has failed 5/5 weeks before running a single command — with its own alerting crashing, so nobody was paged. Every "green" signal today is downstream of one of these. (REL-001…005)
2. **Some of the product is a facade, and some of it is exploitable.** 31 routes in `admin-service` and `citizen-service` "gap" modules return success without doing anything — including a citizen-facing `POST /v1/citizen/requests` (202, nothing saved) and `POST /v1/admin/roles` (201, random UUID) — and feed real screens (Security Center, Compliance, Citizen Portal). `document-service` is routed and consumed by four pages but has no migrations, database or tests. Any authenticated user can fabricate a successful tax payment, DCB collection and GL event via `POST /v1/revenue/bbps/pay-bill`. (COMP-001…003, SEC-001, DOM-001)
3. **It cannot run safely at scale.** 129 PM2 processes × 10 direct Postgres connections (~1,480 worst case) against `max_connections = 200`, with PgBouncer configured nowhere in `ecosystem.config.js`; 271 tenant-scoped tables have no `tenant_id` index under RLS; 45 more nested-transaction deadlock sites exist across 19 services that were never scanned; 60 of 66 HTTP services exit uncleanly on SIGTERM. (PERF-001…003, TX-001)

What is genuinely strong, and should be protected while the above is fixed: the finance GL core (three-layer debit/credit assertion, period locks, reversal-not-delete with DB triggers, bigint money), the outbox/idempotency discipline (0 `queue.publish` inside a transaction repo-wide; 663/665 consumers use `markProcessed`), the tiered gateway rate limiting, the PII envelope crypto with a versioned keyring, and — corrected from earlier notes — broad design-system adoption in the web app (`ds/` components on 84% of pages).

Important context for anyone reading recent history: PRs #1076–#1086 (merged today) were each verified locally against isolated Postgres containers with 3× full-suite runs, as their descriptions state — but CI itself could not have run those suites on `main`. REL-001 must be fixed before any further "CI green" claim is made.

Priority mapping used throughout: **P0** = exploitable, fabricating, or blocks all other verification; **P1** = correctness/isolation/availability defect on a real path; **P2** = hardening, consistency, or a gap in a control; **P3** = hygiene.

---

## 3. Execution order

Work the waves in order; within a wave, higher priority first. A wave is done when every gap in it is `Fixed` with an independently reviewed PR.

- **Wave 0 — Restore truth (days).** REL-001, REL-002, REL-003, REL-004, REL-005, REL-008, REL-011, REL-019, TX-012. **Status 2026-09-08: done** — all nine closed (REL-001 PR #1095, REL-002/003/004 PR #1091, REL-005 PR #1089, REL-008 PR #1090, REL-011 PR #1088, REL-019 PR #1093, TX-012 investigation PR #1092). TX-013 and TX-014 were discovered as a direct consequence of REL-001 landing (the `Tests` job could finally execute against a real bootstrapped DB) and are queued into Waves 2/3 below rather than reopening Wave 0, since they don't affect whether "CI green" is a trustworthy signal — they're real product defects the signal was finally able to reveal.
- **Wave 1 — Stop the bleeding (P0).** SEC-001, COMP-001, COMP-002, COMP-003, DOM-001, PERF-001. **Status 2026-09-08: done** — all six closed (SEC-001 PR #1096, COMP-001 PR #1099, COMP-002 PR #1097, COMP-003 PR #1101, DOM-001 PR #1100, PERF-001 PR #1098), each through the full fix → independent review → fix-up → re-review cycle; three rounds found and closed real gaps before merge, most notably a live-reproduced cross-tenant data leak in PERF-001's first draft. Four new gaps were discovered as a direct consequence of the fixes and reviews (TX-013, TX-014, COMP-011, DOM-017, PERF-013, PERF-014) and queued into their priority-appropriate waves below rather than reopening Wave 1.
- **Wave 2 — Correctness, isolation, availability (P1).** SEC-002…006, TX-001…003, TX-013, DOM-002…009, COMP-004, COMP-005, PERF-002…005, PERF-010, PERF-015, UX-001…004, REL-006, REL-007, REL-009, REL-010, REL-012. PERF-015 was discovered as a direct consequence of working PERF-002 (the remainder of its backlog after a first tranche) and is queued here rather than reopening Wave 2's scope.
- **Wave 3 — Hardening (P2).** Everything marked P2 (includes TX-014).
- **Wave 4 — Hygiene (P3).** Everything marked P3.

Dependencies worth knowing: COMP-003 resolves REL-007 (the `documents/eoffice` alias contract failure). REL-001 unblocks REL-013 (coverage thresholds are only enforced by the skipped Coverage Gate). SEC-004 and REL-016 are the same upgrade programme seen from two sides. TX-011 and SEC-014 are "commit the scanner and gate on it" and pay for themselves on the first re-run.

Populated all four with realistic rows. The other 2 Procurement E2E failures were real, distinct bugs: the GRN-detail fixture was registered at the singular `/grn/<id>` while the loader fetches the plural `/grns/<id>` (never matched), and the vendor-list test assumed the vendor name was a link when DataTable only links column 0 (`vendorCode`) and names it after that column's value, not the vendor's name (filed as UX-013, renumbered to UX-015 — see that entry). `/stock` removed from `tests/a11y/routes.ts` (redirects to `/inventory`, already a separate entry, per an intentional `next.config.mjs` legacy redirect — not a bug). Web server resilience: `scripts/ci/run-with-watchdog.sh` supervises both the mock gateway and web server for the rest of the Accessibility job, restarting once with a loud diagnostic and failing hard (not looping) on a second death; sabotage-tested against the real `next start` process (see PR), not just a synthetic one. **Residual, found but explicitly not fixed here (see REL-023):** the E2E suite has widespread pre-existing failures across most other spec files, and 2 Accessibility public-route tests (`/auth/login`, `/auth/forgot` → 404) that were previously invisible behind the crash this PR fixes — REL-010's own named symptoms (the hrms-payroll/procurement locators and the `/stock`+crash pair) are the only ones this PR claims, and are green 3 consecutive runs in isolation; the full gate is not green because of REL-023, a separate, much larger problem |

This was initially misdiagnosed by DOM-006's fix as an AWS SDK v3 checksum/LocalStack incompatibility (a `packages/storage` option change was made to work around it); review could not reproduce a checksum-related hang either way against a fresh LocalStack, isolating the real cause to the shared container's resource exhaustion | Find and remove (or raise) the out-of-band memory cap on the shared `civitasone-localstack` container; bring its resource limits under version control (compose/infra config) so they can't silently regress again; consider a periodic restart/health check given the accumulated-I/O pattern observed. **DoD:** the shared LocalStack container serves plain requests promptly under normal concurrent dev/CI load; its resource limits are declared in checked-in config, not set out-of-band | Open |
| REL-023 | P1 | The E2E (Playwright) gate has widespread pre-existing failures across most spec files; mock-gateway-fixture hypothesis tested and REFUTED as the dominant cause — confirmed to be mostly independent per-spec UI/locator drift, plus one distinct, now-fixed missing-fixture cluster and a missing-baseline cluster. Accessibility's 2 auth-route failures this gap named are already resolved by #1189 (separate PR, merged) | Ran the current full suite against `main` exactly as CI does (`pnpm --filter @civitasone/web run e2e --project=chromium`, `CI=true`, production build): **201 of 605 failed** (404 passed), spread across 39 of 46 spec files — materially different from this gap's original from-scratch enumeration (partial run, 111/169), confirming the set drifts as the app changes. Deep-dived 6 representative files end to end (`auth.spec.ts`, `analytics.spec.ts`/`billing.spec.ts`, `audit.spec.ts`, `dashboard.spec.ts`, `crm.spec.ts`, the `workforce.spec.ts`/`specs/workforce.spec.ts` pair) plus every distinct error signature across the full 201 (`grep`'d: 136 strict-mode-violation, 179 element-not-found, 301 toBeVisible failures at the assertion level). Result: the hypothesis is **refuted as the dominant pattern** — most failures are genuine, independent UI drift unrelated to fixture data: (1) `auth.spec.ts` (6/6 tests) was 100% stale against the 2026-08-09 "eliminate interstitial" login redesign (d06f4ead) — bare `/auth/login` now always redirects toward Keycloak, so every test that let a real navigation follow that chain either hit the same class of failure PR #1189 found for the Accessibility gate, or (on this dev box, which happens to have a reachable Keycloak) actually landed on the real IdP page; (2) `analytics.spec.ts`/`billing.spec.ts` hubs each grew a new richer page (`/analytics/dashboards`, `/billing/plans`) that displaced the generic `ModuleListPage` link the tests still expect at the old `/analytics/list`/`/billing/list` — same generic-template-now-obsolete pattern also present in `workflow.spec.ts`; (3) `dashboard.spec.ts`'s `[aria-label="Modules"]` tile nav was fully replaced by `RoleCommandCenter` (previously catalogued by #1188); (4) `audit.spec.ts`/`crm.spec.ts` etc. have renamed headings/labels (e.g. CRM's `/crm/deals` no longer has a "Deal Pipeline" heading) unrelated to any fixture. **One genuine common-cause cluster WAS found and fixed**: `/hr/org-chart`, `/hr/workforce/wfh`, `/hr/workforce/overtime`, `/hr/workforce/staffing-plan` and `/hr/shifts` had **no mock-gateway fixture at all** (not even `[]`) — the exact REL-010 empty-array/duplicate-CTA pattern, just for 5 endpoints REL-010 didn't reach — accounting for all 27 failures across the near-duplicate `workforce.spec.ts` + `specs/workforce.spec.ts` pair (this duplication itself is worth a separate cleanup, flagged out-of-band, not tracked here). A second, unrelated cluster (`hrms-payroll/visual-regression.spec.ts`, 19/19 failures) turned out to be a missing-baseline bug, not app or fixture drift: only the `*-desktop-*` screenshot baselines were ever committed (`d2b0a1eb`); the non-desktop ones never existed in git at all, so those 19 assertions could never pass in a fresh CI checkout | **Fixed and verified (green twice, cold Next.js data-cache, plus one sabotage check reverting the org-chart fixture to confirm the test genuinely depends on it):** `auth.spec.ts` rewritten to assert the app's own first-hop redirect (Location header via `page.request`, not following real navigation into Keycloak) for routes that leave our origin, matching the #1189 precedent (6/6 now pass); added the 5 missing HRMS-workforce fixtures to `global-setup.ts` plus 3 small independent locator fixes surfaced alongside them (a stale `"Work-From-Home"` vs actual `"Work From Home"` aria-label; two `exact:true` strict-mode fixes where a static page element — not fixture data — collided with the assertion) and a missing `/hr/workforce/wfh/new` route (added a one-line redirect to the list page, matching the existing `documents/page.tsx` redirect-stub convention, since the form has always been embedded inline on the list page) — `workforce.spec.ts` 17/17 and `specs/workforce.spec.ts` 20/20 now pass; generated + visually inspected + committed the 19 missing baseline screenshots for `hrms-payroll/visual-regression.spec.ts` (sanity-checked against the pages' actual rendered content, and cross-checked that the already-accepted `-desktop-` baselines share the same environment-level rendering quirks, e.g. an icon font not resolving in this headless Linux Chromium — not something newly introduced). Total: **52 of the 201 originally-failing tests fixed** (auth 6 + workforce.spec.ts 12 + specs/workforce.spec.ts 15 + visual-regression 19). **Confirmed already resolved, not re-touched:** the 2 Accessibility `/auth/login`/`/auth/forgot` failures this gap named are fixed by #1189 (merged) — `apps/web/tests/a11y/routes.ts` now targets `/auth/login?error=unknown` instead of the bare path; that PR's own verification (51/51 `test:a11y`, twice) is the source of truth, not re-run here. **Still open, catalogued but intentionally NOT fixed here (too large/varied for one task per this gap's own scope note):** ~149 failures across ~33 other spec files, overwhelmingly independent per-spec locator/heading drift against intentional redesigns (see patterns 1–4 above for representative examples) — each needs its own small, spec-by-spec pass, not a mechanical bulk fix. Audit whether `apps/web/e2e/workforce.spec.ts` and `apps/web/e2e/specs/workforce.spec.ts` (and similarly `finance.spec.ts`/`specs/finance.spec.ts`) are meant to be near-duplicates and consolidate if not. **DoD (unchanged, not yet met):** `E2E (Playwright)` green 3 consecutive runs across the WHOLE suite — currently 456/605 passing after this fix, not yet the whole suite | In progress (PR #1197) — auth.spec.ts, the workforce/HRMS fixture cluster, and the visual-regression baseline cluster are fixed and verified; ~149 failures across ~33 other files remain open, catalogued above by pattern, not yet fixed |
| REL-024 | P1 | Tests job still fails after PR #1187 restored the 6-service DB-wiring bugs -- separate, unrelated pre-existing failures in identity-service and inspection-service | Confirmed on PR #1187 own post-merge CI run (`main` push): `identity-service/tests/sessions-apikeys-routes.test.ts` and `inspection-service/tests/universe-routes.test.ts` + `inspection-service/tests/routes-integration.test.ts` still fail (plus two other files showing partial failure: "1 failed | 89 passed (90)" and "1 failed | 20 passed (21)" in the same run, service not yet identified). PR #1187 was deliberately scoped to only the 6 services with the wrong-port/missing-DATABASE_URL pattern (building/market/parks/roadcut/shop/trade-service); these are a different root cause not yet investigated | Investigate each failing file independently (do not assume the same DB-wiring root cause -- identity-service and inspection-service were not in PR #1187's affected-service list, so this is likely a different class of bug). **DoD:** the Tests job passes for these files with a real, verified root-cause fix (or, if genuinely out of scope for a single fix, each remaining failure is individually triaged and its own gap filed) | Fixed (PR #1196) -- identity-service and inspection-service: root cause was CI-load contention, not a code bug -- `buildApp()` legitimately takes >10s under `turbo test --continue`'s ~115-package concurrency against one shared Postgres container, exceeding vitest's 10s default `hookTimeout`. Verified: every file in both services passes 100% in isolation (34/34 / 416 tests; 66/66 / 1288 tests); sabotage-confirmed by reverting the fix and reproducing the identical "Hook timed out in 10000ms" failure on all 4 named files under artificial concurrent load, then restoring the fix and confirming they pass under the same load. Fix: `hookTimeout: 30_000` in both services' `vitest.config.ts` (precedent: `tests/quality-program/vitest.config.ts`). Of the two previously-unidentified partial-failure files, one signature ("1 failed \| 89 passed (90)") traced to `workflow-service/tests/quorum-routes.test.ts` (filed as REL-025) and the other ("1 failed \| 20 passed (21)") matched TWO files by coincidence -- `asset-service/tests/gl.test.ts` (a genuine, locally-reproducible bug, filed as REL-026) and `billing-service/tests/gov-rail-gstn.test.ts` (passes 10/10 in isolation, filed as REL-027 since it could not be reproduced outside CI). All four are real, distinct root causes requiring product/domain judgment beyond this gap's CI-timing scope, so each is triaged and filed on its own rather than force-fixed here |
| REL-025 | P2 | workflow-service committee/quorum decisions never persist `status: "decided"` when a majority vote settles the outcome, even though the tally itself computes correctly | `services/workflow-service/tests/quorum-routes.test.ts:78` -- "CAP-026 quorum majority via votes > settles a 3-of-5 majority once the third approval lands": after the deciding (3rd) approve vote, `r3.tally.decided` is `true` and `r3.tally.outcome` is `"approve"` (both assert correctly), but `r3.decision.status` stays `"open"` instead of flipping to `"decided"`. Reproduces 100% locally in isolation (not a CI-timing artifact -- confirmed via `pnpm vitest run tests/quorum-routes.test.ts` against a fresh disposable DB with zero contention). `castVoteTx` (`src/modules/quorum/repo.ts:118-140`) appears correct at a glance -- it inserts the vote, recomputes the tally, and updates `committee_decisions.status` to `"decided"` all inside the SAME `db.transaction()` block as the consumer (`src/modules/quorum/consumer.ts:37-45`), so the update should be atomically visible to the GET route's independent read the instant votes reach 3 -- yet the persisted row shows it never ran (or never committed) for this case | Trace `castVoteTx`'s `UPDATE ... WHERE status='open'` (repo.ts:129-133) with logging/a debugger against the actual 3rd-vote transaction to determine whether the predicate fails to match, the transaction silently no-ops, or the CQRS dispatch (`commands.castCommitteeVote` -> `queue.subscribe(COMMANDS.castCommitteeVote)`) delivers the 3 votes out of the order the test assumes. Needs product/domain judgment on the intended vote-tally-to-decision-persistence contract, not a mechanical fix. **DoD:** `quorum-routes.test.ts` passes; a majority/unanimous/threshold decision's `status` reliably flips to `decided` in the same request cycle its tally settles | Fixed (PR #1241) -- independently reviewed clean (reviewer built a deterministic forced-reproduction of both the race and the fix proving the mechanism beyond any probabilistic doubt, traced the isolation-level fix through every stack layer down to the real SQL it emits) -- turned out to be mechanical, not a product-judgment call. Instrumented `castVoteTx`/the consumer with logging against a real disposable Postgres and traced the actual deciding-vote transaction: all 3 named hypotheses ruled out (predicate matched, `updRows:1`/`updStatus:"decided"` every time; transaction committed cleanly every time; votes processed strictly in order per the test's own await-before-next-post structure) -- `castVoteTx`'s write path is correct. Real root cause was on the READ side: `routes.ts`'s GET route (and the POST vote route's "already voted" pre-check) read the decision and its votes as two independent `scopedRead()` calls -- two independent READ COMMITTED statements -- so a `castVoteTx` commit landing between them let the second statement (votes) observe a later snapshot than the first (decision) already read, pairing a freshly-decided tally with a stale pre-decision decision row. Reproduces reliably under concurrent load (5/9 full-suite runs, always one of the 3 rule-type sub-tests, never a fixed one) but NOT in isolation (0/40+ solo runs of `quorum-routes.test.ts`) -- the opposite of this row's own evidence claim of "100% locally in isolation... not a CI-timing artifact," which PR #1241 flags explicitly as an unreconciled discrepancy rather than silently overwriting it. Matches the shape of the original CI failure that seeded this gap's filing. Fix: new `findDecisionWithVotes()` reads both in one `REPEATABLE READ` transaction (one pinned snapshot for both statements) instead of `scopedRead()`'s default fresh-snapshot-per-statement; both route call sites now use it. Sabotage-checked (revert-fix reproduces on 2/5 full-suite runs; restore-fix is clean); 14/14 clean full-suite runs post-fix; `tsc --noEmit` clean. Added the missing decided-status assertions for the unanimous and threshold rules (previously only majority was covered), so the DoD's "majority/unanimous/threshold" line is now actually proven for all three. Separately documented (not resolved -- flagged for independent review, not decided unilaterally): a vote arriving after a decision has already flipped to "decided" is silently dropped (never persisted, no error) -- is that the intended contract or an audit-trail gap? See PR #1241 for the full trail and a second, unrelated, out-of-scope tenant-filter gap noticed in passing. |
| REL-026 | P2 | asset-service dual-book depreciation schedules (company SLM + statutory WDV) are silently never created on asset acquisition | `services/asset-service/tests/gl.test.ts:249` -- "Dual-book schedules -- company SLM + statutory WDV > one create produces a company(SLM) and a statutory(WDV) schedule" expects 2 rows in `assetDepSchedules`, gets 0; two dependent tests then fail with `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'period')` since no schedule exists to read from. Reproduces 100% locally in isolation (3 failed / 8 passed, zero contention). The enqueue side looks correct -- `enqueueDualDepSchedules` (`src/modules/register/consumer.ts:197-211`) is called from the `assetCreate` handler (`consumer.ts:90`) and writes two `COMMANDS.depSchedule` outbox rows (company/SLM, statutory/WDV) -- but the consumer that should turn those into rows (`src/modules/depreciation/consumer.ts:20-45`) has a bare `if (!asset) return;` silent-skip guard at line 29 right after `registerRepo.findAssetByIdTx(tx, p.assetId, p.tenantId)`, with no logging on the miss. Prime suspect: `tenantScoped(rawQueue)` (consumer.ts:17) -- the RLS-tenant-context wrapper that sets `app.tenant_id` before the handler runs -- may not be establishing that context correctly/in time for this specific lookup, causing `NOBYPASSRLS`+`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` to fail-closed and return zero rows exactly like the identity-service RLS pattern documented elsewhere in this repo | Instrument `findAssetByIdTx` and `tenantScoped`'s GUC-setting path to confirm whether the miss is RLS-related or something else (outbox topic-string mismatch, timing); the silent `if (!asset) return;` should also at minimum log a warning regardless of root cause, since it currently drops a whole depreciation schedule with zero operator-visible signal. Needs domain/product judgment on correct fallback behavior, not a mechanical fix. **DoD:** `gl.test.ts`'s dual-book test passes; a missed asset lookup in this consumer is never silent | Fixed (PR #1236) -- independently reviewed clean (reviewer independently instrumented the original code themselves and confirmed, with their own first-hand evidence, both that this was test-only pollution and that the RLS-timing theory was wrong) -- root cause was NOT the prime suspect (tenantScoped/RLS-GUC context confirmed correct via direct instrumentation: findAssetByIdTx found the asset and the transaction committed cleanly every time) and NOT the `if (!asset) return;` guard firing. Actual cause: a test-only race in `gl.test.ts`'s `relayOutbox()` helper -- it selected every pending outbox row for (tenantId, topic) with no further scoping, so the "Dual-book schedules" test's relay call also swept up 2 stale never-relayed rows left behind by an earlier "Acquisition GL" test (every assetCreate unconditionally enqueues a dual-book pair), quadrupling the concurrent DB work a fixed drain(700) had to absorb. Timing instrumentation showed only 1 of 4 concurrent handler transactions had committed when the assertion ran; the other 3 (including both of this test's own rows) committed 250-450ms later. Fix: relayOutbox() now requires an explicit assetIds scope, plus a poll-until-ready waitFor() helper replacing two fixed-drain-then-assert spots that still raced under the full suite's ambient concurrent load (never when this file ran alone). Sabotage-checked (reverting the scope fix alone reproduces the original failure). Full asset-service suite: 6 consecutive clean runs (315/315) against a disposable Postgres. Per the DoD, both findAssetByIdTx call sites in depreciation/consumer.ts now call captureError() on a genuine miss (logging/metrics only, via this codebase's existing invisible-failure primitive) -- what a genuine FUTURE miss should do (retry/dead-letter/other) is left as an open product question, not decided here. |
| REL-027 | P3 | billing-service `gov-rail-gstn.test.ts` fails intermittently in CI (`Test timed out in 5000ms` + `TypeError: createQueue is not a function` at `src/shared/infra.ts:6:22`) but cannot be reproduced locally | Observed in the same PR #1187 post-merge CI run analyzed for REL-024, matching one of its two unidentified "1 failed \| 20 passed (21)" partial-failure signatures (the other traced to asset-service, filed as REL-026) -- coincidentally identical pass/fail counts made this ambiguous until isolated local runs disambiguated it. `pnpm vitest run tests/gov-rail-gstn.test.ts` against a fresh disposable DB with zero contention passes 10/10 every time, including the specific two tests that failed in CI, so this is not the same CI-load/hookTimeout class of bug fixed by REL-024 (that reproduced 100% under artificial contention; this does not) -- `createQueue is not a function` suggests a monorepo build-artifact race (e.g. `@civitasone/queue`'s `dist/` -- which itself re-exports `@civitasone/queue-service` -- being read mid-rebuild by a concurrent `turbo test --continue` task) rather than a timing-only issue | Needs a CI-side investigation distinct from REL-024's hookTimeout fix -- likely turbo's task graph missing a `^build` dependency ordering for `@civitasone/queue` (the package billing-service actually imports; it is a re-export facade over `@civitasone/queue-service`) before dependents' `test` tasks run concurrently, or a shared-package dist collision under heavy parallelism. Reproduce by deliberately racing a `@civitasone/queue`/`@civitasone/queue-service` rebuild against `billing-service`'s test task, or add build-completion tracing to a real CI run. **DoD:** root cause identified and fixed, or confirmed benign flakiness with a documented retry/mitigation | Fixed (PR #1335, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- the gap's own hypothesis does not hold: turbo.json's `test` task already depends on `^build`, and billing-service's package.json lists `@civitasone/queue` (not `@civitasone/queue-service`, which was this row's original evidence-text error, now corrected above) as a direct dependency, so turbo's `^build` resolution already places `@civitasone/queue#build` ahead of `billing-service#test`. `@civitasone/queue` is itself a pure re-export facade over `@civitasone/queue-service` (`packages/queue/src/index.ts`) and depends on it in its own package.json, so turbo's `^build` closure transitively places `@civitasone/queue-service#build` ahead of it too -- verified in turbo.json directly and in two independent `turbo ... --dry=json` task graphs, including CI's exact `pnpm turbo build --filter='./packages/*'` step (its filter glob excludes `services/*`, but the dry-run confirms `@civitasone/queue-service#build` is still pulled in transitively). Deliberately racing a raw `tsc` rebuild of `packages/queue` against a tight cache-busted import loop (bypassing turbo's scheduler) DID reproduce a module read with zero exports mid-write -- 96/43641 iterations against `packages/queue/dist/index.js`, 1/36964 against `services/queue-service/dist/index.js` -- proving the failure signature's mechanism is real (tsc's dist writes are not atomic), but CI's `build` and `test` steps run strictly sequentially in one ephemeral `ubuntu-latest` job with no cache shared across jobs, so that scenario has no path to occur in the real pipeline. True root cause unconfirmed; leading alternative is CPU-contention-sensitive timing from this file's `vi.resetModules()` forcing a fresh cold import of the whole app graph on every one of its 10 `it()` blocks. Per this gap's own DoD, closed as confirmed-benign with a documented mitigation: narrowly-scoped `{ retry: 2 }` added to just this file's outer `describe` block, full investigation trail recorded inline as a comment. Independent review independently reproduced the race TWICE via different methods (0/53,049 with 1 writer, then 5,248/1,876,570 zero-byte reads plus 131/24,497 undefined-export hits with 3 parallel writers), confirmed the CI-unreachability claim by reading ci.yml directly, and reran the full suite fresh matching 403/405 with the identical 2 pre-existing billing-lifecycle.test.ts failures -- reproducing those DETERMINISTICALLY on a fresh run actually strengthens the case for stale shared-state contamination over pure timing, reinforcing that they need their own isolated investigation (already flagged separately) rather than being folded into this fix. |
| REL-028 | P3 | observability.test.ts's fault-injection dead-letter test is flaky without a broker present | Found during PERF-017's independent review: a test named "fault injection: a throwing handler dead-letters after max attempts" (in the observability package's test suite) failed once in 4 standalone reruns on a fresh checkout with no RabbitMQ broker present -- confirmed unrelated to PERF-017 (the file isn't touched by that PR), reproduced in isolation, not a one-off host-load fluke | Root-cause the flake (likely a timing assumption in the fault-injection harness itself, or an unhandled race between the simulated failures and the retry/backoff timer) and make the test deterministic. **DoD:** the test passes 20/20 consecutive standalone runs with no broker present | Fixed (PR #1278) -- independently reviewed clean (traced drain()'s exact mechanism through deliver()'s single-function retry loop to confirm it genuinely waits out backoffs not just the first attempt, reproduced the sabotage check under real host load -- 27-34 load average, ~7 concurrent vitest workers -- and still could not reproduce the original flake, matching the fix's own honest claim precisely) -- root cause confirmed: the test raced a fixed `setTimeout(r, 200)` against real exponential backoff (`2^attempt*10ms`, ~60ms nominal for 3 attempts) instead of using MemoryQueue's own purpose-built `drain()` method (its docstring: "exists so tests can await async fan-out deterministically instead of racing a fixed sleep") -- under event-loop pressure a setTimeout(...,0)/backoff delay can be pushed past 200ms of real elapsed time even though the nominal total is far below it. Fixed by replacing the fixed sleep with `await q.drain()`. Verified: 20/20 clean on the fix; could NOT reproduce the original flake in 30 standalone reruns of the unfixed code even under moderate concurrent host load (only the qualitative fix -- a deterministic wait instead of a magic-number sleep -- is claimed with full confidence; the specific "1 in 4" original flake rate could not be independently reproduced, honestly noted rather than forced). Full queue-service suite: 87/93 passing (6 expected skips), no regressions. |
| REL-029 | P3 | startOutboxPurge's stuck-outbox WARN can never fire -- a drizzle/postgres-js return-shape bug always reads the count as 0 | Found during REL-026's independent review: packages/outbox/src/index.ts:269-272 (startOutboxPurge) reads (countResult as unknown as {rows?: ...}).rows?.[0]?.cnt ?? 0, but drizzle's postgres-js driver returns a bare array for a raw db.execute() call, not a {rows: [...]} wrapper -- so this always evaluates to the ?? 0 fallback regardless of the real count, and the ">10K stuck outbox" WARN can never fire no matter how backed up the outbox actually gets | Read the count off the bare array result the driver actually returns (matching how other db.execute() call sites in this codebase already handle the return shape), not the {rows} wrapper. **DoD:** seeding a real backlog past the threshold makes the WARN actually fire, verified live against a real Postgres, not just a passing unit test with a mocked return shape | Fixed (PR #1266) — independently reviewed clean (confirmed the bare-array shape both empirically and from driver source, reproduced red-then-green with the unmodified test file, confirmed the old mocks used the same wrong shape that hid this bug) — fixed the count-read to a bare-array cast (`countResult[0]?.cnt ?? 0`), matching the established pattern already used for the same kind of single-row SELECT read in `animal-service/src/modules/registration/repo.ts`. Confirmed the real drizzle-orm + postgres-js return shape empirically first (bare array, no `.rows` property) before fixing. `purge.test.ts`'s mocks encoded the same wrong `{rows: [...]}` shape the buggy code assumed -- exactly why this shipped past unit tests undetected -- updated those 3 fixtures to the real bare-array shape. Added `purge-live-pg.test.ts`: a live-Postgres regression test seeding a real 10,050-row backlog; sabotage-checked with an unmodified test file (red pre-fix -- WARN never fires against a real over-threshold backlog with the original buggy code; green post-fix). Full outbox package suite (5 files/25 tests) passes against a disposable Postgres; 23 passed/2 cleanly skipped with no `DATABASE_URL` set. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Not independently reviewed -- not marking Fixed yet. |
| REL-030 | P3 | tenant-service's tenants_status_check integration test fails deterministically, pre-existing and unrelated to any recent fix | Found during SEC-016's independent review: tenant-service/tests/consumer.integration.test.ts's tenants_status_check test (tied to migration 0024) fails every time, confirmed by running the identical file against a completely unmodified origin/main against a fresh database -- it fails identically there too, so it predates and is unrelated to SEC-016 or any other recent merge | Root-cause and fix the failing assertion. **DoD:** consumer.integration.test.ts's tenants_status_check test passes deterministically on a fresh cluster | could NOT reproduce a failure on a genuinely fresh cluster. Provisioned an isolated disposable Postgres, bootstrapped via `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` against unmodified `origin/main`: migration 0024 applies with 0 errors/notices, and `tenants_status_check` passes 5/5 standalone runs plus cleanly inside the full tenant-service suite (25 files / 361 tests). Root-caused the ORIGINAL report instead: reproduced an identical, deterministic failure -- a bare `PostgresError: new row for relation "tenants" violates check constraint "tenants_status_check"`, indistinguishable from a real regression -- but only by deliberately reverting the constraint to migration 0024's own documented pre-migration definition (i.e. simulating a database that predates 0024). This is the same *class* of false-fresh-reading bug REL-022 fixed for `bootstrap-postgres.sh`'s own scratch files, one layer further out -- in whatever produced the reviewer's test database, not the script itself; see REL-035, filed alongside this fix, for the most likely concrete mechanism (a systemic footgun, not specific to this reviewer). Fix: added a preflight to the `tenants_status_check` test that reads the constraint's real definition via `pg_get_constraintdef()` before asserting, and throws a clear, actionable error naming migration 0024 and `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` when it doesn't match, instead of a bare constraint-violation that reads as a regression. No assertion's expected behavior changed; none were wrong. Sabotage-checked empirically, not just by reverting the diff: with the constraint manually reverted, the fix throws its new diagnostic message; reverting just the test-file fix (`git stash`) against that same stale constraint reproduces the original bare `PostgresError`; both restored, constraint re-validated via migration 0024's real SQL, full suite re-confirmed green. Fixed (PR #1279) -- independently reviewed clean (reverted the constraint via migration 0024's own Rollback SQL, confirmed the original bare error and the new preflight's clearer diagnostic firsthand; ran the fix on two fresh CI-matching clusters -- 25/25 files x2 -- and the unmodified pre-PR code on a fresh cluster too, confirming the assertion was never broken; independently spot-checked REL-035's 64-service claim by grepping all 66 vitest.config.ts files directly). |
| REL-031 | P3 | castVoteTx silently drops a vote cast after a committee decision has already settled -- no error, no log, no DLQ entry | Found during REL-025's own investigation, confirmed accurate during its independent review: services/workflow-service/src/modules/quorum/repo.ts's castVoteTx has no handling at all for a vote arriving after decision.status is already 'decided' -- it's neither recorded, rejected, nor logged. The review found this codebase already has an established convention for exactly this class of problem (an async CQRS consumer processing a command after its target already left the valid window): procurement-service/src/modules/tender/consumer.ts throws NonRetryableError on a bid submitted after a tender's closing date, which the queue driver routes to the DLQ -- visible and auditable, never silent. castVoteTx's current fully-silent behavior is a deviation from that precedent regardless of which substantive answer (should a late vote still count, or just be recorded as an audit note) is eventually chosen for the deeper question | At minimum, stop the silent drop -- log or DLQ-route a late vote the same way tender's bid-after-close is handled, matching this codebase's own established convention. The deeper question (should a late vote ultimately count toward the tally, or only be recorded as a non-counting audit entry) is a genuine committee-governance/product call, not an engineering one -- resolve that separately before deciding final behavior beyond visibility. **DoD:** a vote cast after a decision has settled is never silent -- it's either rejected with a clear reason, recorded as non-counting, or DLQ'd for review, per whichever the product decision settles on | visibility-only fix, per this row's own DoD split. castVoteTx's late-vote branch (a voter who had NOT already voted, arriving after decision.status left "open") now returns `lateVote: true` instead of doing nothing, mirroring castVoteTx's own existing notFound/duplicate sentinel-return convention. The castCommitteeVote consumer surfaces it via captureError() (the same silent-miss-visibility primitive REL-026's depreciation-consumer fix used for the identical bug shape) plus a `rejected_already_decided` audit outcome instead of `success` -- not tender's NonRetryableError/DLQ precedent, since castVoteTx's own local convention (return a flag, don't throw) took priority per this row's fix guidance, and throwing would roll back markProcessed() (forcing redelivery), itself a real behavioral choice. The vote is still NOT inserted into committee_votes -- unchanged. Regression test added (quorum-routes.test.ts, "REL-031 late vote after decision settled"); reproduced pre-fix (waitFor timeout, captured-error count unchanged) and sabotage-checked (disabling captureError() alone reproduces the identical failure; restoring is clean). Full workflow-service suite: 92 files / 1020 tests passing against a freshly bootstrapped, isolated, disposable Postgres. **The deeper question -- whether a late vote should ever COUNT toward the tally if the decision were reopened -- remains explicitly UNRESOLVED and out of scope for this fix**; still needs a separate committee-governance/product decision before any change to counting/tally behavior. Fixed (PR #1269) -- independently reviewed clean (traced the queue driver's NonRetryableError handling directly, correcting one imprecise detail in the PR's own stated reasoning without changing the verdict; reproduced the captureError log line live; sabotage-checked; hand-merged together with REL-032 since both touch castVoteTx, combined result re-verified with the full workflow-service suite -- 93 files / 1024 tests -- before merging). |
| REL-032 | P3 | castVoteTx's internal tally query filters by decisionId only, not tenantId, unlike its own siblings | Found during REL-025's fix, confirmed during its review: castVoteTx's internal recompute-tally query filters only by decisionId, while listVotes/findDecisionWithVotes both also filter by tenantId. Likely already mitigated in practice -- migration 0028_workflow_engine_100.sql shows committee_votes has a real tenant_isolation_policy/RLS policy, and workflow_svc is documented elsewhere in this campaign as NOBYPASSRLS -- so this reads as a defense-in-depth gap rather than a live cross-tenant read today, not yet independently re-verified against a real RLS-enabled cluster | Add the same tenantId filter castVoteTx's siblings already use, for defense-in-depth consistency even if RLS already covers it. **DoD:** all three of castVoteTx's queries filter identically on both decisionId and tenantId | Fixed (PR #1268) -- independently reviewed clean (provisioned a disposable Postgres, confirmed workflow_svc genuinely has no BYPASSRLS attribute so the tenantId filters -- not RLS -- are doing the real work, wrote its own additional test file beyond the PR's own two, reproduced the sabotage check via git checkout of the fix commit's parent, ran the full suite; independently noticed the REL-031 file/function overlap and flagged it for coordinated merge, which is what happened). |
| REL-033 | P3 | committee_vote_unique constraint has no tenant_id, unlike castVoteTx's now-fixed query filters (REL-032) | Found while building REL-032's duplicate-check regression test: services/workflow-service/migrations/0028_workflow_engine_100.sql:82 `CONSTRAINT committee_vote_unique UNIQUE (decision_id, voter_id)` has no tenant_id column, unlike the tenant-scoped uniqueness pattern used elsewhere in this codebase. In practice inert today -- decision_id is 1:1 with a single tenant, and no code path outside a direct DB write bypassing castVoteTx can construct a cross-tenant (decision_id, voter_id) collision -- but if one ever existed (a bug elsewhere, a migration issue), it would surface as a raw, unhandled PostgresError (23505 unique_violation) rather than being cleanly impossible or gracefully handled. Live-reproduced in REL-032's own regression test (tests/rel-032-cast-vote-tenant-filter.test.ts's duplicate-check scenario) via a poisoned row seeded directly (bypassing castVoteTx, the only way to construct this state) | Consider `UNIQUE (tenant_id, decision_id, voter_id)` instead (needs a migration; existing rows already satisfy this) and/or have castVoteTx catch the resulting unique-violation and translate it into a clean, non-crashing response instead of an unhandled exception. **DoD:** the constraint includes tenant_id, or castVoteTx handles the collision gracefully instead of throwing an unhandled PostgresError | Fixed (PR #1331, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- migrations/0043_committee_vote_unique_tenant_scoped.sql widens committee_vote_unique to UNIQUE (tenant_id, decision_id, voter_id) -- existing rows already satisfy it, a pure widening. New regression test tests/rel-033-committee-vote-unique-tenant-scoped.test.ts (4 tests) plus an updated assertion in rel-032-cast-vote-tenant-filter.test.ts's duplicate-check scenario (now asserts the fully-fixed non-throwing outcome instead of the old thrown-PostgresError intermediate state). Verified live that RLS does NOT mitigate this, unlike REL-032: workflow_svc (NOBYPASSRLS) cannot SELECT the poisoned cross-tenant row under committee_votes' FORCE RLS policy, yet its own INSERT for a genuine voter still collided with it pre-fix -- Postgres enforces unique constraints against the physical index unconditionally, regardless of row visibility. Sabotage-checked directly against the live constraint (ALTER TABLE DROP/ADD against the disposable cluster, not a file revert): reverting to the old UNIQUE (decision_id, voter_id) shape reproduces the raw unique-violation PostgresError in exactly the 3 tests that depend on this fix, while the other 4 REL-032/033 tests still pass; restoring the widened constraint returns all 7 to green. Full workflow-service suite green twice (before and after the sabotage check): 95 files / 1031 tests, 0 failures, against a disposable, isolated postgres:16-alpine (never civitasone-postgres:5435). Independent review confirmed REL-032's mechanism directly from its merged code, confirmed the widening is safe by construction (tenant_id already NOT NULL on every row, so the old two-column uniqueness mathematically implies the new three-column one), confirmed the widening-over-exception-handling judgment call is sound (avoids fabricating a 'duplicate' answer to a genuine first-time voter), reproduced the sabotage-check independently, and reran the full suite fresh (95/95 files, 1031/1031 tests). |
| REL-034 | P2 | purgeOutbox's DELETE-count reads use .rowCount (the node-postgres convention) instead of .count (the real postgres-js property) -- always misreports 0 deletions and silently caps real purges at one batch | Found and independently confirmed during REL-029's review: packages/outbox/src/index.ts:222,237 (both purgeOutbox DELETE-result reads) use (result as unknown as {rowCount?: number}).rowCount ?? 0, but postgres-js's driver exposes the affected-row count as .count, never .rowCount -- confirmed by reading the driver source and by calling the real exported purgeOutbox against a seeded disposable Postgres: it returned 0 while the database genuinely deleted 1000 rows, and the do-while batching loop exits after one batch (batchDeleted is always misread as 0, so 0 >= batchSize is always false), silently leaving the rest of any backlog bigger than one batch unpurged. This also directly undermines REL-029's own just-fixed WARN: since purgeOutbox now always returns deleted=0, startOutboxPurge's "deleted === 0" gate is unconditionally true every cycle once backlog exceeds the threshold, so the WARN can no longer distinguish a genuinely stuck purge from one that's actively (correctly) draining -- REL-029 deliberately left this untouched since it was out of that gap's own scope (the SELECT count-check path), but the interaction makes this a real, higher-impact follow-up, not a cosmetic one | Read the count off .count, matching the correct pattern REL-029 just established for the SELECT-side read. **DoD:** seeding a backlog bigger than one batch size and calling purgeOutbox for real results in the batching loop continuing until the backlog is actually cleared, and the returned deleted count matches what was genuinely deleted -- verified live against a real Postgres, not a mocked return shape | Fixed (PR #1307, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- both DELETE-count reads changed from `.rowCount` to `.count` (postgres-js's real property); `totalDeleted` now also includes `inboxBatchDeleted`, previously omitted entirely. `purge.test.ts`'s mocks (same wrong-shape bug class REL-029 already fixed once for the SELECT side of this file) updated to `{ count: N }`; one test's expected total corrected 2300 -> 2800 now that inbox deletions are counted. Full suite including the live-Postgres regression test green (25/25) against a disposable Postgres; sabotage-checked (reverting the source produces exactly 3 failures). First review round found two real issues, both fixed in this follow-up commit: this gap-report row itself was never updated, and a global find-replace while adding the REL-034 doc-comment accidentally rewrote the comment's own explanatory text backwards. Re-review confirmed both round-1 fixes correct: the comment now reads consistently (verified against the code diff), and the gap-report edit is a clean single-row whole-cell change (pipe-field count matches neighboring rows, no duplication). Confirmed the core source fix itself was byte-identical throughout both rounds. Reran the full suite fresh (25/25, live-pg included), tsc --noEmit clean. |
| REL-022 | P1 | `bootstrap-postgres.sh`'s migration-failure detection races across concurrent invocations on a shared host — its scratch file isn't per-process, and reused/stale containers give false-fresh readings | **Two independent findings, now confirmed to be the same root cause, merged from a duplicate pair of entries filed minutes apart by concurrent DOM-003/DOM-005 verification work.** (1) Found verifying DOM-003: two independent, back-to-back `bootstrap-postgres.sh` runs against fresh, isolated Postgres containers, on the SAME unmodified commit, reported completely different, non-overlapping sets of "NEW migration failure(s)" — run 1: `inventory-service/0016_outbox_messages_drop_rls.sql`, `shop-service/0003_permits_public_directory.sql`, `tenant-service/0003_rls_tenant_registry.sql`; run 2: 11 different files — with no `ERROR` text appearing anywhere near the "Applying …" line for any of them in either run's own log. Root cause: `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh:367-374` (service-role loop) and `:456-461` (admin-owned-DB loop) both redirect each migration's psql output to the SAME hardcoded, non-namespaced path, `/tmp/bootstrap-migration-out.txt`, then immediately `grep` that same path to decide pass/fail — a concurrent invocation on this host (5+ observed live via `ps aux`) can overwrite the shared file between this process's `psql` and its own `grep`, reading ANOTHER process's output. (2) Found verifying DOM-005: a run against a throwaway container reported 52 non-allow-listed migration failures across 27 services, e.g. `crm-service/0068_document_types.sql: ERROR: must be owner of function list_document_alert_tenants`. **A dedicated follow-up investigation (closing the open question this second finding originally left) resolved this fully**: three real, completed CI `Tests` job runs from the SAME day all showed `0 novel migration failures` — real CI is unaffected. The local 52-failure reproduction was traced to a REUSED container/database across multiple bootstrap invocations during fix-verification work (confirmed live: sampled failures all showed `NOTICE: relation "X" already exists, skipping` for migration-unique objects, impossible on a genuinely fresh cluster; independently reproducing the exact mechanism by accidentally re-running the script against a not-torn-down container reproduced the identical "already exists" signature). This is the SAME underlying class of bug as finding (1) — shared/reused scratch state (a temp file, or a container/database) across concurrent or sequential local invocations giving a false reading — just manifesting two different ways. Since real CI's bootstrap step is unaffected, none of the ~20+ PRs merged this session on scoped/single-service local verification are undermined by this on the "CI green" axis | Give each invocation a private scratch file (`mktemp`, or suffix the path with `$$`/a run-specific id) and remove it on exit, for both the service-role and admin-owned-DB loops; separately, make the local repro/dev workflow enforce a genuinely fresh container/volume per invocation (document or script a mandatory teardown-and-recreate step) rather than reusing one across multiple bootstrap runs. **DoD:** N `bootstrap-postgres.sh` runs launched concurrently on one host, each against its own isolated Postgres container, report `0 NEW migration failure(s)` on a clean commit, every run, with no cross-run variance; local repro tooling cannot silently give a false-fresh reading from a reused container | Fixed (PR #1193 + follow-up PR) -- PR #1193 closed the migration-output race (private mktemp MIGRATION_OUT, scoped by $$, both loops, removed via an EXIT trap). An independent review of that PR found it incomplete: the reconciliation block further below (novel/stale ratchet that sets this script's exit code) still read/wrote 4 shared hardcoded paths (/tmp/bootstrap-failures-{observed,allowed,conditional,allowed-strict}.txt), the same class of bug one stage later in the pipeline. The follow-up PR moved all 4 (plus the pg_stat_statements best-effort stderr capture) into one PID-scoped mktemp -d directory, cleaned up via the same EXIT trap. Verified 3x with 2 concurrent invocations against fresh disposable Postgres containers giving identical non-contaminated results, plus a 4-way concurrent run; a full grep of the script confirms no other hardcoded /tmp path remains. Sabotage check (reverting only the follow-up fix, keeping PR #1193's MIGRATION_OUT fix): 4 attempts (three 2-way, one 4-way) did not naturally reproduce visible cross-contamination -- the vulnerable window here is a single write-then-immediate-read of a few lines, not spread across ~600 migration files like the original MIGRATION_OUT race, so it is inherently harder to hit empirically even though the code-level TOCTOU is the identical, unconditional pattern. The fix is retained on that code-level basis, not on a reproduced failure |
| REL-035 | P2 | All 64 services' vitest.config.ts silently default DATABASE_URL to the shared, long-lived civitasone-postgres:5435 dev instance, not a fresh/disposable one | Found while investigating REL-030: `grep -rln "localhost:5435" services/*/vitest.config.ts` matches 64 files, e.g. `tenant-service/vitest.config.ts`, `ai-agent-service/vitest.config.ts:10`, `asset-service/vitest.config.ts:8`, `audit-service/vitest.config.ts:7` -- all of the shape `DATABASE_URL: process.env.DATABASE_URL ?? "postgres://<role>_svc:<role>_dev_pw@localhost:5435/civitas_<db>"`. Any developer or reviewer who runs a service's integration test suite (`pnpm test`, `vitest run`) without remembering to export `DATABASE_URL` first silently sends live INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE integration-test traffic (including these suites' own `wipe()`-style cleanup, which deletes rows by fixed UUID) at the SAME shared instance other developers, CI-adjacent tooling, and concurrent agents on this host may be relying on -- not a fresh, disposable database, which is easy to mistake for one. This is the most plausible concrete mechanism behind REL-030's original "fails against a fresh database" report: a database that is long-lived and shared is not fresh merely because the git checkout running against it is, and 5435 (civitas_tenant included) has no guarantee of being migrated to the current HEAD at any given moment. Not yet confirmed which specific instance SEC-016's reviewer actually used -- that would require inspecting the shared instance directly, out of scope for a routine investigation | Pick one (product/infra decision, not prescribed here): (a) remove the silent fallback so these suites fail fast with an explicit, actionable error when DATABASE_URL isn't set, instead of defaulting to the shared instance; (b) point the fallback at a documented, unambiguously-disposable local convention instead of port 5435; (c) at minimum, add a prominent warning next to the `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` docs that integration tests run without DATABASE_URL set will silently target the shared dev instance. **DoD:** running any service's integration test suite with DATABASE_URL unset can no longer silently read/write the shared civitasone-postgres:5435 instance without an explicit, unmissable signal that that's what's about to happen | Fixed (PR #1310, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- picked option (a): all 64 services' vitest.config.ts now throw a clear, actionable error when DATABASE_URL (or DB_URL / *_SCANNER_DATABASE_URL / GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL, whichever a given file actually reads) isn't set, instead of silently defaulting to the shared instance; the fallback still resolves under CI/GITHUB_ACTIONS so CI's own ephemeral per-job Postgres is unaffected. journey-service (previously had no env-driven fallback at all) and gateway-service (sources DATABASE_URL from the differently-named GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL, per SEC-026) needed non-mechanical handling. Verified on 8 services chosen for shape/domain variety; Independent review gave this unusually large (64-file) change commensurately deep scrutiny: read 21 of 64 files directly (every unique special-case shape plus samples), exhaustively swept the full diff programmatically for the remaining 43 (found a perfect 77:77 guard-to-throw match, zero inconsistencies), and dynamically reproduced on 5 services in a fresh worktree including 3 the fixer hadn't sampled. Independently confirmed the CI-detection guard is safe by reading ci.yml directly and running a real CI-path simulation (CI=true, DATABASE_URL unset, 570 tests executed normally). Confirmed both special cases (gateway-service's GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL preservation, journey-service's previously-absent fallback) via git blame and dynamic reproduction. Explained the one outlier-sized diff (visitor-service, 77 lines vs. ~8-20 elsewhere) as incidental whitespace reformatting, confirmed via `git diff -w` and matching before/after failure counts -- zero behavioral regression. |
| REL-037 | P3 | notification-service's findByRecipient hardcodes limit(100), so a delivery-count test assertion silently breaks once a recipient accumulates >100 rows across repeated local runs | Found incidentally during TX-017's independent review: services/notification-service/src/modules/deliveries/repo.ts:13-23's findByRecipient hardcodes .limit(limit).offset(offset) with a default limit of 100 and no cap-awareness in callers. services/trade-service/tests/cross-service-integration.test.ts reuses a single hardcoded ACTOR constant across every local run of the suite (never a fresh random ID), so repeated runs against the same database keep inserting delivery rows for that same recipient; once the count exceeds 100, a before/after "length + 1" assertion style fails with a confusing off-by-a-lot mismatch (observed: expected 100 to be 101) even though a new row genuinely was written -- the real new row is there, just past the fixed page boundary the test's own assertion didn't account for. Confirmed this reproduces identically on code unrelated to and untouched by TX-017's own fix (the query and the test's ACTOR-reuse pattern both predate that PR), so it's a pre-existing, independent test-hygiene gap, not a regression | Either give the affected test(s) a fresh, unique recipient/actor ID per run (matching the tenant/decision-ID randomization convention already used elsewhere in this same test file) instead of a fixed constant, or make the assertion itself robust to pagination (e.g. assert the new row's specific ID appears in the result, not just a raw length delta). **DoD:** the affected delivery-count assertions pass deterministically even after many repeated local runs against the same database, without needing a fresh database each time | Fixed (PR #1308, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- randomized ACTOR to a fresh randomUUID() per run in cross-service-integration.test.ts, matching the applicationId/scrutinyId/licenceId/noticeId/renewalId randomization convention already in that file, instead of the fixed constant; reproduced the exact expected 100 to be 101 failure live against an isolated disposable Postgres (105 synthetic deliveries.deliveries rows inserted for the old fixed ACTOR), confirmed the fix resolves it, then verified 4 consecutive clean runs of the file against the same unreset database plus full trade-service (43/43) and notification-service (1734/1734) suites against a fresh disposable Postgres; Independently reviewed directly (not a dispatched agent) after the assigned review agent stalled on a repeated false wait pattern: confirmed the diff is exactly the claimed one-line change plus comment, confirmed findByRecipient's real implementation genuinely caps at the limit parameter with no cap-awareness, and empirically reproduced the core mechanism directly against a freshly seeded disposable Postgres -- a call with the old fixed ACTOR against 105 real pre-existing rows returned exactly 100 (capped), while a fresh randomUUID()-style ACTOR correctly returned 0. Full end-to-end suite run was blocked by an unrelated, pre-existing TX-017 freshness-guard environment quirk on a from-scratch bootstrap (a GUC-setup issue orthogonal to this fix), not reproduced further given the fix's minimal, test-only, low-risk scope and the mechanism's direct empirical confirmation. |
| REL-038 | P3 | `ux-quality.spec.ts`'s "filter actually narrows results" test times out on its searchbox locator even though the real accessible name appears to textually match | Discovered while verifying REL-036 (hrms-payroll E2E `webServer` readiness fix) -- once the webServer itself starts correctly, `apps/web/e2e/hrms-payroll/ux-quality.spec.ts:262` ("filter actually narrows results (responsive filtering)") still fails 3/3 consecutive runs at line 271 (`await filterInput.fill('zzzzzz_no_match_xyz')`), timing out after 30s waiting for `getByRole('searchbox', { name: /search by/i }).or(locator('input[type="search"]:not([readonly])').first()).first()`. The test's own comment says it was already updated for UX-014 ("the DataTable filter is now a real `type="search"` searchbox... both alternatives below updated to match"). The real placeholder text at `apps/web/src/app/(app)/hr/employees/EmployeesTable.tsx:44` is `filterPlaceholder="Search by name, code or department…"`, which textually contains "search by" and should satisfy the `/search by/i` accessible-name pattern via native placeholder-as-accessible-name fallback -- yet the locator never resolves. Root cause NOT yet diagnosed: possibly an aria-label/aria-labelledby overriding the placeholder-derived accessible name, a mismatched `type` attribute, the input not being mounted/visible when the locator resolves, or a Playwright accessible-name-computation quirk with this component. Confirmed genuinely separate from REL-036's own scope: REL-036's own DoD (webServer starts from cold, 3 consecutive runs) is independently met each of the 3 runs; only this one specific test within the same file fails every time regardless -- **Correction, independent review, 2026-09-14**: reviewing REL-036, once `@civitasone/client-core` and sibling packages were actually built (`pnpm turbo build --filter='./packages/*'` -- absent in both the original filer's and the reviewer's initial worktrees), the SAME searchbox locator claimed above to "never resolve" was observed to resolve and the test passed CLEANLY at a 120s timeout, across repeated runs. The reviewer's own first 3 cold runs instead failed earlier, at `page.goto` itself, under host load average 47-75 on an 8-core box, with the test's own log showing `port 8080 is already in use -- SSR mock will NOT start`. This points to a timeout-budget/host-contention issue with a concrete lead (the port-8080 collision breaking an SSR mock the test may depend on), not a genuine accessible-name-computation bug -- the aria-label/type/mount-timing/Playwright-quirk candidates below are now believed likely wrong, kept only for the historical record of the original filing | **Revised per the above correction:** first determine whether this is a genuine deterministic bug or purely a flaky/timeout-budget symptom of shared-host contention -- rerun the exact failing command on a quiet host with packages fully built and the port-8080 collision resolved (find and fix whatever else on this shared host binds 8080, or make the SSR mock resilient to that port being taken) before assuming the locator/accessible-name theory below. Only if it still fails under those clean conditions: Diagnose why the searchbox locator doesn't resolve despite the textually-matching placeholder (inspect the rendered accessibility tree directly, e.g. via Playwright's trace viewer or `codegen`) and fix the component's accessible name, the test's locator, or both. **DoD:** `npx playwright test --config e2e/hrms-payroll/playwright.config.ts -g "filter actually narrows results"` passes, 3 consecutive runs | Fixed (PR #1322, merged) -- Confirmed environment-only, not a code or locator bug -- closed via verification, no source change needed. Root-caused fully: in a fresh worktree (`~/wt/rel-038`) with `pnpm turbo build --filter='./packages/*'` actually run first (29/29 tasks succeeded, 27 replayed from cache, including `@civitasone/client-core`), the exact DoD command was run 3 consecutive times. On every project able to launch on this host -- desktop-chromium and mobile-chromium -- the searchbox locator (`getByRole('searchbox', { name: /search by/i })`) resolved and the test passed cleanly and deterministically all 3/3 runs (11.6s-13.8s, well inside the 30s test timeout), confirming the 2026-09-14 correction's suspicion that the original "never resolves" finding was fully an artifact of unbuilt workspace packages. Port 8080: confirmed it is permanently held by the real gateway-service (PM2-managed, 2+ day uptime, `services/gateway-service/dist`), not an ephemeral process from a concurrent agent -- this is load-bearing for other agents' live staging verification and must not be freed or killed. `global-setup.ts` already catches this gracefully (`EADDRINUSE` -> logs "SSR mock will NOT start" and continues without crashing), so no code change was needed there either; the 3/3 clean passes confirm this graceful skip does not affect this test's actual behavior -- the DataTable filter's client-side fetch is wired to the port-4001 mock via `CIVITASONE_API_BASE_URL` regardless of what happens on 8080. Separately discovered, confirmed pre-existing, and explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for this gap: Firefox and WebKit binaries were missing entirely on this host; installed them (`npx playwright install firefox webkit`, no code/repo change) but they still cannot launch -- missing OS-level shared libraries (libgtk-3, libcairo-gobject2, etc.), fixable only via `sudo npx playwright install-deps`, a system-level change to a shared host running many concurrent agents that was intentionally not made here, as that falls outside what an agent should do unilaterally. This is not a REL-038 regression: it blocks the other 7 of the suite's 9 browser/viewport projects identically on every run regardless of any fix here, and matches this repo's own `ci.yml`, which has only ever installed and run `--project=chromium`. Flagged separately to the team as a standing host-setup gap rather than worked around or silently ignored. No source or config change made in this PR -- only this Status cell. |

| REL-036 | P1 | hrms-payroll E2E suite's `webServer` never starts from cold -- its `url`-based readiness check against this app always times out, the same root cause the main e2e config already fixed elsewhere | `apps/web/e2e/hrms-payroll/playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer` uses `url: http://localhost:${E2E_PORT}` with `timeout: 300_000`. `apps/web/playwright.config.ts`'s own `webServer` comment (dated 2026-08-27) documents in detail why this exact pattern never works in this environment: every route in this app redirects (307) an unauthenticated request (`middleware.ts`), so Playwright's `url` readiness check never sees a non-redirect response and retries silently until timeout -- even though the server itself is up and responding in milliseconds. The root config was switched to a `port`-based check for exactly this reason; `hrms-payroll/playwright.config.ts` (a separate config for the "HRMS + Payroll E2E test suite", 3 browsers x 3 viewports) was never updated to match. Found and reproduced while verifying UX-014: `npx playwright test --config e2e/hrms-payroll/playwright.config.ts -g "filter actually narrows results"` timed out after the full 300s on `config.webServer`; manually ran the identical server command (`CIVITASONE_API_BASE_URL=... pnpm --filter @civitasone/web dev --port 4100`) and confirmed `curl http://localhost:4100/` returns `307` immediately -- server healthy, readiness check unsatisfiable by design. Only masked when some other suite (e.g. the root e2e config, which correctly uses `port`) already left a compatible server running on the same `E2E_PORT` -- `reuseExistingServer: true` then skips the broken check entirely. From a genuinely cold start (fresh worktree/checkout, CI, or this config run first/standalone) the whole suite cannot run at all | Change `hrms-payroll/playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer` from `url`-based to `port`-based readiness (`port: E2E_PORT` instead of `url: ...`), mirroring the root config's already-fixed pattern. **DoD:** `npx playwright test --config e2e/hrms-payroll/playwright.config.ts` (any single spec) succeeds in starting and reaching its `webServer` from a cold port with no pre-existing server, 3 consecutive runs | Fixed (PR #1303, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- `url` to `port`-based webServer readiness, mirroring the root config's already-fixed pattern exactly (byte-for-byte comparison confirmed by reviewer). Independent review reproduced across 7 total runs (3 cold pre-build, 3 cold post-build, 1 extended-timeout) in a fresh worktree at the PR's exact head commit -- zero runs hit the old symptom, the TCP port consistently ready in seconds. Gap-report edits confirmed clean at the cell level. Noted but not blocking: CI's e2e jobs run `pnpm turbo build --filter='./packages/*'` before starting the server, which matters for reproducing this locally (see REL-038's corrected evidence below for why). |

### B. Security beyond tenancy (skills 19, 05, 03, 11)

| ID | Pri | Title | Evidence | Fix · Definition of done | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-001 | P0 | Any authenticated user can fabricate a successful tax payment, DCB collection and GL event | `revenue-service/src/modules/bbps/routes.ts:33` `POST /v1/revenue/bbps/pay-bill` — client-supplied `assesseeIdentifier`, `amountMinor`, `bbpsTxnId`, no `requireRole`; `bbps/consumer.ts:51-115` inserts receipt + DCB collection, marks txn `success`, enqueues `receiptCaptured`; no gateway verify call exists in the module (gated only by `BBPS_ENABLED`) | Role-gate to collection roles; verify every payment against the BBPS gateway (or accept only signed gateway callbacks); server-derive amount from the demand; reconcile. **DoD:** regression test proves an unverified/unsigned payment is rejected and no ledger rows are written | Fixed (PR #1096) — role-gated to REVENUE_ROLES, per-tenant replay protection on bbpsTxnId; residual limitation honestly documented: no live BBPS gateway to cryptographically verify a payment happened, so a revenue-role holder can still submit a fabricated txn (amount is bounded by real DCB outstanding as defense-in-depth) |
| SEC-002 | P1 | 21 inspection-service tenant tables have no RLS at all (corrected from this row's original count of 23 — see fix notes) | `inspection-service/migrations 0011-0027`: `penalty_orders`, `show_cause_notices`, `prosecution_referrals`, `licences`, `licence_conditions`, `survey_definitions/responses/aggregations`, `sampling_frames`, `devices`, `telemetry_readings/alerts`, `alert_rules`, `penalty_rates`, `encroachment_*` ×4, `illegal_construction_*`, `corrective_actions` | Migration: ENABLE + FORCE + tenant policy per table. **DoD:** L1 lane covers each; cross-tenant read test returns 404/empty | Fixed (PR #1107) — fix agent and reviewer both independently recounted the actual tables from the migrations (not the original audit's list) and found 21, not 23: the original evidence cell omitted `penalty_rates` and miscounted elsewhere; `corrective_actions` is in migration 0011, not the cited 0012-0027 range. Also deliberately overrides an `encroachment.*`/`illegal_construction.*` "no RLS, app-layer only — by design" comment — the reviewer traced that comment to having been mechanically copied from `enforcement.*`, which was itself one of the 21 undefended tables, i.e. not an independent security judgment. `reports.inspection_reports`/`observations` (ENABLE without FORCE) intentionally left to SEC-009 |
