| SEC-008 | P2 | Per-tenant rate limit keyed on client-supplied `x-tenant-id` | `gateway-service/src/app.ts:362-364` `keyGenerator: req.headers["x-tenant-id"] \|\| req.ip` | Key on the verified JWT `tid` (fallback ip only pre-auth). **DoD:** spoofed header cannot shard or burn another tenant's budget | Fixed (PR #1225, independently reviewed — APPROVE, both halves confirmed independently load-bearing via source-level proof of Fastify's hook composition order) — reproduced first: the one thing directly, empirically observable on unmodified `main` is that the tier never fires on `/api/*` at all — 5 requests with `GATEWAY_RATE_LIMIT_TENANT_MAX=2` all returned 200 regardless of the `x-tenant-id` sent, because it was registered `global: false` with no route declaring `config.rateLimit` (`@fastify/rate-limit@9.1.0`'s own `onRoute` logic skips a `global:false` registration for any route that doesn't opt in). The header-spoofing bypass this row's Evidence column describes — burning a victim's budget, or sharding around one's own — is therefore not independently demonstrable on main in isolation; it is what the OLD keyGenerator does once a budget is actually enforced, which is exactly the combined state the sabotage check below reproduces (revert the fix in full → unconditional 200s regardless of header, same as main; restore → correctly limited and un-spoofable) — both halves are correctly diagnosed together, not two unrelated fixes bundled by convenience. Fix: new `verifiedTenantRateLimitKey()` sources the bucket key from `req.jwtPayload.tid` (set by `jwtEdgeVerify` from the verified JWT) first, then `req.headers["x-tenant-id"]` only when `req.apiKeyAuthenticated` is true (a server-set flag `apiKeyPreHandler` assigns only after verifying the `x-api-key` against identity-service and overwriting that header itself), else `req.ip` — never the raw client header directly. Also moved the tier's own registration to `hook: "preHandler"` (default `"onRequest"` fired before `jwtEdgeVerify`/`apiKeyPreHandler` had verified anything) and added `config: { rateLimit: {} }` to the `/api/*` route so the tier actually runs against real traffic instead of being registered but inert. Tests: `services/gateway-service/tests/sec-008-tenant-rate-limit.test.ts`, 7 cases — spoofed-header sharding, spoofed-header budget-burning-a-victim, legitimate distinct-tenant isolation, pre-auth IP fallback, the api-key auth path (against a public route, to isolate the keyGenerator from SEC-023 below), plus two added after independent review: the api-key path against a REAL non-public route (`/api/v1/finance/bills`) confirming SEC-023's separate bug only changes the final response code (401 instead of 200) and does not stop the limiter itself from running or keying correctly — `[401,401,401,429]` at threshold — and a `GATEWAY_JWT_EDGE_VERIFY=off` case proving the fix holds for ALL traffic (not just the pre-auth/public case) when no verified identity is available from either auth path at all. Sabotage-checked twice — once at 5 cases, again at the full 7 after the review round: reverting the `app.ts` fix (keeping the test file) makes every case fail, reproducing the exact spoofing bypass; restoring makes all pass again. Full gateway-service suite (17 files / 167 tests, then 169 after the review round) green against a freshly bootstrapped, isolated, disposable `postgres:16-alpine` on a confirmed-free port (containers torn down after); shared `civitas-postgres` (:5435), Keycloak (:8180), :8080/:3000, and any shared Redis never touched. Note: both my regression tests for the api-key auth path mock the identity-service verify call to succeed, deliberately isolating the rate-limiter's own behavior (does it key correctly on the verified tenant once `req.apiKeyAuthenticated` is true?) from whether that verification can succeed in the real deployed system today — see SEC-024, filed independently during this PR's review, for why it currently cannot. Bonus finds, **not** fixed here: SEC-023 (`proxyHandler`'s own auth check only ever accepts a literal `Authorization: Bearer` header and never consults `req.apiKeyAuthenticated`) and SEC-024 (the verify call itself 404s against a nonexistent identity-service path, an earlier-stage failure of the same feature). Independent review returned APPROVE pending this last round of requested test coverage; not marking Fixed myself — that follows the reviewer's own sign-off on the final diff. |
| SEC-009 | P2 | RLS ENABLE without FORCE on 17 tables (corrected from this row's original count of 16 — see fix notes; service role owns tables → policies bypassed) | crm `appointments`(0075), `canned_responses`(0070), `commission_ledger`(0073), `commission_rules`(0072), `grievances`(0079), `pending_campaigns`(0069), `referrals`(0074), `rti_requests`(0081), `subscriptions`(0077), `service_requests`(0080); estab `items`,`transactions`(0041); hrms `training_plans`,`training_plan_items`(0108); inspection `inspection_reports`,`observations`(0025) — `observations` explicitly deferred here by 0029's own fix notes but missing from this row; workflow `nurture_rules`(0036); precondition stated in `packages/db/src/tenant-scope.ts:20` | `ALTER TABLE … FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` migration per service: crm `0092`, estab `0044`, hrms `0139`, inspection `0030` (admin-run), workflow `0042`. **DoD:** L1 lane green for each | Fixed (PR #1218) — fix agent recounted the evidence cell against the actual migrations (same undercount pattern SEC-002's own fix flagged in itself) and found 17 real ENABLE-without-FORCE tables, not 16/14: `crm.subscriptions`(0077) and `crm.service_requests`(0080) were missing entirely, and `reports.observations` was assigned here by 0029's own fix notes ("intentionally left to SEC-009") but never made it into this row. Verified live on a fresh disposable `postgres:16-alpine` (not the shared dev DB at :5435, via `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh`): all 17 confirmed `relrowsecurity=true, relforcerowsecurity=false` before, `=true,=true` after. inspection-service's two tables are owned by `civitas_admin`, not `inspection_svc` (admin-owned-schema convention — `infra/db/bootstrap/bootstrap_inspection.sql` — schemas stay admin-owned specifically so the service role cannot alter its own tables); migration runs admin-run and the owner-bypass test connects as `civitas_admin`, the real owner, since `inspection_svc` is a non-owner and was never RLS-exempt regardless of FORCE. Added one owner-bypass regression test per table (33 tests, 5 new `tests/sec-009-force-rls.test.ts` files): seed via the correct tenant-scoped path, then prove the owner with no tenant GUC ever set silently saw/touched the row before FORCE and is rejected or sees zero rows after. Sabotage-checked: reverting `crm.appointments`'s FORCE alone failed exactly its own two tests; restored, clean. Bonus find: `workflow-service/nurture-triggers.ts`'s three consumers read `tenantId` off the message but never called `runWithTenant`, so the rule lookup and outbox-enqueue transaction ran unscoped — harmless before FORCE, broke TX-005's redelivery-idempotency tests the instant FORCE landed; fixed by wrapping each subscriber in `runWithTenant(tenantId, ...)`. Full crm/estab/hrms/inspection/workflow suites pass with no other regressions (~9,300 tests); the shared host was under extreme concurrent load from other agents during this work (load average 167) which produced ~15 unrelated flaky failures on the first pass, each individually re-run in isolation and confirmed clean, none touching any of these 17 tables. hrms/workflow migrations renumbered from 0138/0041 after confirming (`gh pr view 1216`) the open SEC-010 PR already claims those numbers. |
| SEC-010 | P2 | Tables created after the isolation sweeps never got RLS | hrms `hrms_wfh_requests`, `hrms_shift_change_requests`(0107), `hrms_audit_log`(0109), `integrations`, `integration_sync_log`(0061); admin `reconciliation_breaks/results`(0020); workflow `dmn_tables`(0027) | Policies + a CI guard: any new `CREATE TABLE` with `tenant_id` must carry RLS in the same migration. **DoD:** guard in Architecture Guard; 0 unpoliced tenant tables | Fixed (PR #1216) — added ENABLE + FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY + a `tenant_isolation_policy` (USING/WITH CHECK) to all 8 tables (5 hrms + 2 admin + 1 workflow — the evidence column above undercounts by one, an error that predates this PR), matching each service's existing convention (hrms: `employee.current_tenant_id()`, see `0135_audit_hr_action_log.sql`; admin: bare `current_tenant_id()`, see `0029_sftp_lead_ingestion.sql`; workflow: `workflow.current_tenant_id()`, see `0031_case_task_mgmt.sql`): `services/hrms-service/migrations/0138_sec010_missing_rls.sql` (5 tables), `services/admin-service/migrations/0032_sec010_reconciliation_rls.sql` (2 tables), `services/workflow-service/migrations/0041_sec010_dmn_tables_rls.sql` (1 table). Verified on a fresh cluster: all 8 are owned by their NOBYPASSRLS service role (hrms_svc/admin_svc/workflow_svc), so FORCE is load-bearing — ENABLE alone would not have applied to the connecting role, same mechanism as SEC-009. A bare INSERT with no `app.tenant_id` GUC now fails closed (WITH CHECK) and a cross-tenant SELECT under the wrong GUC returns zero rows, on every table. **Companion app-layer fix (the migrations alone were not sufficient):** hrms-service `src/modules/attendance/routes.ts`'s `GET /v1/hrms/shift-requests` and `/wfh-requests` used bare `db.select()` (no tenant GUC) — under FORCE RLS this would have silently returned zero rows for every tenant (see `shared/db.ts`'s own `scopedRead` doc comment); wrapped in `scopedRead()`. `src/modules/integration/routes.ts`'s 5 endpoints used `sqlPool.query()` (bare pool client, no GUC) — under FORCE RLS the INSERT/UPDATE would have failed closed and the SELECTs would have silently returned zero rows; converted to `db.transaction()`/`scopedRead()` + `tx.execute(sql...)`, same remedy as TX-002/TX-003/0135. admin-service's `reconciliation-consumer.ts` and workflow-service's `dmn/*` module already wrote through `db.transaction()`/`scopedRead()` — no code change needed, no regression there. **Correction (independent review caught this before merge):** `employee.hrms_audit_log` was first claimed to have no current callers — that was wrong. `shared/audit-log.ts` also exports `createAuditHook()`, registered globally at `app.ts`'s `app.addHook("onResponse", createAuditHook())`, firing on every successful mutating request fleet-wide. Its call site passed the bare `db` client into the insert — under FORCE RLS that failed WITH CHECK on every single mutating request, invisibly (the helper's own try/catch swallows all errors) — exact same failure class as TX-013 before its fix, just caught pre-merge instead of post-merge this time. Fixed: the hook now routes through `runWithTenant()`/`db.transaction()`, same pattern as the rest of this PR. Verified empirically both ways: reverting to the bare-`db` call reproduces `PostgresError: new row violates row-level security policy for table "hrms_audit_log"` (42501) exactly as the reviewer found it; the fix makes a real `app.inject()` mutation produce a real row. New test: `tests/sec-010-hrms-audit-log.integration.test.ts`. Tests: `tests/architecture/tenant-table-rls-guard.test.ts` (9 fixture cases); `services/hrms-service/tests/integration-routes.test.ts` updated for the sqlPool -> tx.execute change (29/29 passing); `services/hrms-service/tests/attendance-routes.test.ts` unaffected by the scopedRead wrap (48/48 passing); full hrms-service/admin-service/workflow-service suites re-run clean. **CI guard (this gap's own DoD):** new `scripts/ci/tenant-table-rls-guard.mjs`, wired into the Architecture Guard job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) as two steps mirroring the fleet's other fixture-tested guards (unit tests, then the real tree) — pure migration-text analysis (no live DB needed), fails on any NEW migration that CREATEs a `tenant_id` table without ENABLE+FORCE+CREATE POLICY in that same file. Ratcheted like `tenant-index-guard.mjs`/`schema-drift-guard.mjs`: 1007 pre-existing fleet-wide instances of the same historical "create now, RLS later" shape — including these 8 tables' own original creating migrations, which will always read this way since a retroactive fix is necessarily a separate, later migration — are grandfathered in `scripts/ci/tenant-table-rls-baseline.json`; the gate is 0 NEW violations going forward, not 0 total. Sabotage-checked three times: (1) the guard itself — planted an unpoliced tenant table, confirmed FAIL; replaced it with a fully-policied one, confirmed PASS; removed it, confirmed clean; (2) a real fix — dropped FORCE on `workflow.dmn_tables` on the live cluster only (never touched the committed migration), confirmed the cross-tenant query test caught the reintroduced bypass (leaked 1 row instead of 0), restored FORCE, confirmed clean again; (3) the audit-hook fix — reverted `audit-log.ts` to the original bare-`db` call, confirmed `tests/sec-010-hrms-audit-log.integration.test.ts` fails with the exact `PostgresError ... 42501` the reviewer reported, restored the fix, confirmed the test passes again. **Independently re-reviewed clean after the audit-log fix (root cause, fix, and sabotage check all confirmed) — Fixed.** |

| SEC-015 | P2 | No code path creates a `sessions` row keyed by a real login's Keycloak `sid` — session revocation has no live session to correlate to | Discovered closing SEC-006: `packages/client-core/src/pkce.ts`'s authorization-code flow talks to Keycloak directly and never calls `POST /identity/sessions`; `git grep` for `commands.createSession` callers outside `sessions/{routes,commands,consumer}.ts` finds none; `sessions.sessions.id` is a server-generated random UUID (`sessions/commands.ts` `createSession`), structurally unrelated to the JWT `sid` claim (`packages/auth/src/index.ts` token-claims doc-comment) | Wire the real login flow (or a Keycloak backchannel-logout/event listener) to call `createSession` with `id` = the token's `sid` on login, so an admin revoking a "session" in the UI/API actually corresponds to a live user session. **DoD:** logging in creates a `sessions` row whose `id` equals the access token's `sid`; revoking it (SEC-006's denylist) then rejects that user's real, live token fleet-wide | Fixed (PR #1292) -- independently reviewed clean, exceptionally thoroughly given the auth-adjacent stakes (traced the session id's full path from JWT verification to createSession with total certainty -- no client-controlled path exists; empirically tested fail-closed on both a missing and a malformed sid; proved at the DB layer that a revoked row survives a same-id re-insert; proved the real revocation mechanism -- the sid-keyed denylist -- rejects even a brand-new validly-signed token carrying an already-revoked sid, a stronger property than the PR itself claimed). One non-blocking recommendation: file a follow-up for the fail-open path's operational-visibility gap -- filed as SEC-027 -- wires apps/web's OAuth callback route (`apps/web/src/app/api/auth/callback/route.ts`, the one real server-side login-completion call site -- `pkce.ts` itself is a pure client-side utility with no other real caller) to the existing-but-never-invoked `POST /identity/sessions`, as a self-service call authenticated with the fresh access token itself (routes.ts already allows `ctx.actorId === body.userId` with no elevated role). `routes.ts`'s self-service branch derives `id` from `ctx.sessionId` (the caller's own independently-verified sid, from `authPlugin` -- never trusted from the request body) and 400s closed if the token has no sid; `commands.ts` made `id` overridable (default unchanged for the admin-creates-for-another-user path); `repo.ts` insert is now `onConflictDoNothing` (the same sid can legitimately recur via Keycloak SSO silent re-auth). New end-to-end test (`sec-015-login-creates-session.test.ts`) drives the real route -> command -> queue -> consumer -> Postgres path and proves the DoD directly: login creates a row with `id === sid`, then SEC-006's existing revoke (DELETE) rejects that SAME live token on the next request (401, was 200) -- not two assertions checked separately. | SEC-016 | P2 | Repo-wide "internal-only" route gate convention includes an inert role string, quietly widening it to any human `super_admin` | Found reviewing SEC-005's fix: `INTERNAL_ROLES`-style arrays (`notification-service/src/modules/stream/routes.ts`, `loyalty-service`'s `/v1/loyalty/accrue`, `tenant-service`'s `/v1/quotas/increment`) all include the literal string `"service_account"`, but `packages/auth/src/context.ts`'s internal-caller path (`x-internal` + service secret) stamps `ctx.roles` with `["super_admin","hr_admin","payroll_admin","finance_admin"]` — the string `"service_account"` never appears in `roles`, only in `ctx.actorType`. `hasAnyRole`/`requireRole` check `ctx.roles` only, so the `"service_account"` entry can never match anything; these routes' real (and only) gate is `super_admin`, a role a human can hold, meaning a human `super_admin` can call routes documented as "internal service-to-service only" | Either check `ctx.actorType === "service_account"` directly (not via the roles array) for these routes, or accept and document that `super_admin` is the intended human escape hatch. **DoD:** an internal-only route rejects a human `super_admin` token unless that's an explicit, documented exception | Fixed (PR #1238) — independently reviewed clean, including the notification-service internal-only reversal (reviewer confirmed zero real callers, a weak/speculative original justification, and existing gated alternative routes for operators -- resolved with confidence, no user escalation needed) — grepped repo-wide and found a 4th site beyond the 3 named here (`tenant-service`'s `/v1/tenant/usage/increment`, same file, same inline pattern). Added `requireInternalOrRoles(ctx, roles)` per service (checks `ctx.actorType === "service_account"` directly, OR-ed with any distinct human role still legitimate on that route). `notification-service`'s `/notifications/publish` fixed strict (no other human role was ever in its array; this knowingly overrides the SEC-005/PR #1106 comment that had deliberately kept `super_admin` as a human escape hatch — flagged for reviewer attention). `loyalty-service`'s `/v1/loyalty/accrue` and `tenant-service`'s two increment routes fixed additively, keeping `loyalty_admin` / `platform_admin` (distinct, separately-legitimate human roles unrelated to the internal-call fingerprint) but no longer accepting bare `super_admin`. Reproduced the bug pre-fix (human `super_admin` got 201/202 on all 4 routes), added regression tests for all 4 (human `super_admin` → 403, genuine internal caller → still succeeds, other legitimate human role → still succeeds), sabotage-checked (revert → bypass returns → restore → rejected again). Full suites green on a disposable Postgres container: notification-service 1728 passed/1 skipped, loyalty-service 192 passed, tenant-service 361 passed, 0 failures; typecheck clean. Not yet independently reviewed — not marking Fixed. |
| SEC-017 | P1 | The deny-list fail-closed pattern SEC-003 fixed for one secret is the dominant pattern across the whole secrets surface, not an isolated twin | Found fixing/reviewing SEC-003: `ecosystem.config.js`'s `IS_PROD = (process.env.NODE_ENV ?? "production") === "production"` gates at least 8 other secrets the same way (`DEVICE_TRUST_SECRET`, `JWT_SECRET`, `VISITOR_TENANT_SIGNING_KEY_PEM`, `PII_ENC_KEY`, `ID_CARD_QR_SECRET`, `MFA_ENC_KEY`, `CITIZEN_PII_KEY`, `CRM_PII_KEY`), and `packages/auth/src/index.ts`'s `isProduction()` uses the identical deny-list check to gate JWT-algorithm downgrade and audience validation. A deny-list (`=== "production"` throws-if-unset) silently falls back to a known dev secret in staging/UAT/QA/preprod/CI — exactly the SEC-003 hole, just not yet independently confirmed exploitable for these other 8+ secrets. SEC-003 fixed only its one call site (`resolveCandSecret`) with an allow-list, deliberately scoped narrow | Audit each of the 8+ deny-list-gated secrets and `isProduction()`'s callers for the same exploitable gap; convert the pattern fleet-wide to an allow-list (`NODE_ENV` must be exactly `development`/`test` to fall back), or centralize `IS_PROD`/`isProduction()` itself into an allow-list so every current and future caller inherits the fix. **DoD:** no secret resolver anywhere falls back to a known value outside an explicitly-declared dev/test environment | Fixed (PR #1191) -- centralized both `IS_PROD` (`ecosystem.config.js`) and `isProduction()` (`packages/auth/src/index.ts`) into an allow-list each: `NODE_ENV` must be exactly `development` or `test` to take the dev-fallback/insecure path; everything else -- unset, `staging`, `uat`, `qa`, `preprod`, a typo -- now fails closed. Both were single choke points, so every current call site now definitely benefits without being individually touched: all 8 named secrets (`DEVICE_TRUST_SECRET`, `JWT_SECRET`, `VISITOR_TENANT_SIGNING_KEY_PEM`, `PII_ENC_KEY`, `ID_CARD_QR_SECRET`, `MFA_ENC_KEY`, `CITIZEN_PII_KEY`, `CRM_PII_KEY`) plus `dbUrl()`/`scannerDbUrl()`/`piiKey()` in `ecosystem.config.js`, and both `isProduction()` callers in `packages/auth/src/index.ts` (`resolveAlgorithm()`'s HS256 forbid, `verifyJwt()`'s JWT_AUDIENCE requirement, SAST-004/CWE-287) -- plus every future caller of either function, with no code change required at the call site. Verified with two new regression suites: `packages/auth/tests/sec-017-env-allowlist.test.ts` (16 tests) and `tests/security/sec-017-ecosystem-is-prod-allowlist.test.ts` (18 tests, spawning real `node -e "require(...)"` subprocesses per `NODE_ENV` so module-level secret consts are genuinely re-evaluated) -- both prove staging/uat/qa/preprod/a typo now fail closed and that `development`/`test` are unchanged; full `packages/auth` suite (52 tests) and `ecosystem-workers.test.ts`/`connection-budget.test.ts` (22 tests) all pass; a sabotage check (revert both fixes, confirm every regression case fails open again, restore) was performed and confirmed. **Flagged, not fixed here:** `context.ts`'s own `NODE_ENV === "production"` check, `index.ts`'s `toRequestContext` inline check, and `plugin.ts`'s two `NODE_ENV !== "production"` checks are the identical bug class but do not call `isProduction()`, so they are untouched -- should migrate to this allow-list in a follow-up. **Flagged, needs a decision, not resolved:** this fix removes the `NODE_ENV=staging` UAT dev-login capability documented in `docs/GOLDEN-PATH-AUDIT.md` / `docs/runbooks/launch-undeployed-services.md` (`RUNTIME_NODE_ENV=staging JWT_ALGORITHM=HS256`) -- that flow now fails closed exactly like production at both layers (secret resolution and JWT algorithm/audience checks) unless real secrets are provisioned for that host; the runbook should either provision real secrets or switch to an explicitly-declared `NODE_ENV=test` instead of overloading `staging` to mean an insecure posture |

| SEC-018 | P2 | Dependency-currency majors + long-tail dev-tooling CVEs surfaced by making the SEC-004 CVE gate real | `scripts/ci/cve-allowlist.json` (added by SEC-004): 3 distinct CRITICAL / 33 distinct HIGH advisories remain allow-listed — `next` 14.2.35 (needs >=15.5.24, 10 GHSAs incl. 2 critical RCEs), `fastify` 4.29.1 (needs >=5.7.2), `find-my-way` 8.2.2 (needs >=9.7.0, dependent on fastify's major), `drizzle-orm` 0.30.10 (needs >=0.45.2) — all fleet-wide major-version bumps, same programme as REL-016; plus zero-runtime-exposure dev-tooling debt: `vitest`/`vite` major bump (coordinate with `@stryker-mutator/*`, pinned to vitest ^2.x), `@stryker-mutator/util`, and a long tail of transitive lint/build-tool CVEs (`fast-uri` ×7, `brace-expansion` ×3, `js-yaml` ×3, `browserslist` ×2, `extract-zip` ×2 — no upstream fix published, needs a replacement dependency — `glob`, `tmp`) needing per-major version-scoped `pnpm.overrides` entries applied one at a time | Staged: (1) drizzle-orm → fastify → next major upgrades per REL-016's existing plan, each with full-suite + contract gates; (2) bump vitest/vite/@stryker-mutator/* together as one coordinated set (dev-only, but the mutation-testing runner must keep working); (3) work through the dev-tooling long tail with version-scoped overrides (`pkg@majorRange`), verifying build/lint/CI tooling after each; replace `extract-zip` since no fixed version exists upstream. **DoD:** `scripts/ci/cve-allowlist.json` has 0 entries (or only entries whose "no fix exists" reason is still true); SEC-004's CVE/SCA gate stays green without needing allow-list renewal | Open |
| SEC-019 | P3 | `audit.test.ts`'s APPEND-ONLY trigger assertion doesn't account for the DB grant that already blocks the write, so it can't verify the trigger it claims to test | `services/audit-service/tests/audit.test.ts:327` expects an UPDATE on `events.events` to reject with `/append-only\|not permitted\|immutable/i` (the `trg_events_immutable` BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE trigger's own message, confirmed present on the parent table and all 8 monthly partitions); actually gets `permission denied for table events`, because `audit_svc`'s GRANT on `events.events` has no UPDATE/DELETE privilege at all (`\dp events.events` → `audit_svc=arx`) and Postgres checks table privileges before running BEFORE triggers. Found while verifying TX-001's audit-service slice (PR #1143) — reproduces identically on unmodified `origin/main`, confirmed unrelated to that fix | Broaden the test's assertion to accept either `permission denied` (the GRANT layer) or the trigger's own message, OR add a migration test that runs as a role WITH UPDATE granted (e.g. `civitas_admin`) so the trigger itself is actually exercised and verified, not just the coarser GRANT. **DoD:** the test genuinely fails if `trg_events_immutable` is ever dropped, independent of `audit_svc`'s grants | Open |
| SEC-020 | P3 | reports.inspection_reports/observations have no real cross-tenant RLS test as the actual inspection_svc role | Found during SEC-009's independent review: these 2 tables are civitas_admin-owned (not inspection_svc-owned, per infra/db/bootstrap/bootstrap_inspection.sql's deliberate design so the service role cannot alter its own tables), so SEC-009's FORCE fix and its owner-bypass test correctly target civitas_admin instead of inspection_svc -- but that leaves the actual inspection_svc role's own cross-tenant access path (a normal non-owner RLS check, unaffected by FORCE either way) with no test proving tenant A cannot read tenant B's rows through it. sec-002-rls-isolation.test.ts's own header comment claims this was "flagged separately" but no such gap-report row or fix exists | Add a real cross-tenant test connecting as inspection_svc (not civitas_admin) against both tables: seed two tenants' rows, confirm tenant A's session cannot see tenant B's rows through the normal RLS policy path. **DoD:** the test fails against a deliberately misconfigured/dropped policy and passes against the current one, run as inspection_svc specifically, not the table owner | Fixed (PR #1265) -- independently reviewed clean (reproduced both directions live as the real inspection_svc role on a disposable Postgres -- confirmed genuine cross-tenant leakage with the policy sabotaged, confirmed isolation held with it restored -- and ran the full 68-file/1295-test suite) -- added services/inspection-service/tests/sec-020-inspection-svc-rls.test.ts: seeds tenant A/B rows in both tables through the real inspection_svc pool (db/sqlClient from shared/db.ts -- confirmed via vitest.config.ts's DATABASE_URL this is the role the app actually connects as, not civitas_admin), proves neither tenant's session can read the other's row through the normal rls_reports/rls_observations policies (migration 0025), with a positive control per tenant. Reproduced first on a disposable cluster (port 47201, since torn down): replacing both policies with USING (true) produced genuine cross-tenant leakage as inspection_svc (tenant B's session read tenant A's row on both tables); restoring migration 0025's exact policy makes the suite pass again -- sabotage-checked both directions per this row's own DoD. Citation correction: the "flagged separately" comment this row cites is actually in sec-009-force-rls.test.ts's header, not sec-002-rls-isolation.test.ts's (which never mentions reports.*/civitas_admin/SEC-009/SEC-020 anywhere, confirmed by full read and git log --follow: one commit, #1107); the substance of the gap was accurate, only the file citation was wrong. Full inspection-service suite green (68 files/1295 tests) against a second, freshly bootstrapped, isolated, disposable postgres:16-alpine (port 47301; ratchet holding, same 6 expected postgis-conditional location-service failures as every run on this image, nothing novel); civitasone-postgres (:5435), Keycloak (:8180), :8080/:3000 never touched; both disposable containers removed after use. tsc --noEmit clean. | SEC-021 | P2 | JWT-authenticated requests never get x-actor-id, silently blanking the HRMS audit trail's actor for the primary auth path | Found during SEC-010's review fix-up: `services/gateway-service/src/api-key-auth.ts:129` sets `x-actor-id` (from `record.ownerId`) only on the API-key auth path; `services/gateway-service/src/jwt-edge.ts` -- the JWT path ordinary browser/session users go through -- only ever sets/deletes `x-tenant-id` (lines 102-106) and never touches `x-actor-id` anywhere in the file (confirmed by grep, zero occurrences). `hrms-service`'s `createAuditHook()` (`shared/audit-log.ts`) reads the actor off this header, so every audit-log row written for a JWT-authenticated request has a missing/null actor regardless of whether the underlying RLS/tenant-scoping is correct -- a real gap in the audit trail's usefulness independent of SEC-010's own RLS fix | Set `x-actor-id` on the JWT path in `jwt-edge.ts` the same way `api-key-auth.ts` does for its own path -- from the verified JWT payload's subject/user-id claim, mirroring the existing `x-tenant-id` injection pattern in the same file. **DoD:** an audit-log row written for an ordinary JWT-authenticated mutating request has a real, non-null actor id, verified end-to-end (not just header-presence) the same way SEC-010's own audit-log regression test does | fixed `jwt-edge.ts` to set/delete `x-actor-id` from the verified token's `sub` claim, mirroring the existing `tid`/`x-tenant-id` pattern in the same file. **Second gap found during investigation, not in this row's original text:** `app.ts`'s `FORWARD_HEADERS` proxy allowlist never included `x-actor-id` at all, so even a correctly-set header (from EITHER auth path) was silently dropped before reaching any upstream service -- this made `api-key-auth.ts`'s own actor-header injection dead code too, not only the JWT path this row was filed against. Both halves fixed and required (verified by reverting each independently -- see PR). New tests: 3 unit (`jwt-edge.test.ts`) + 2 gateway-integration (`jwt-edge-integration.test.ts`, stubbed-fetch header capture) + 1 real cross-process/real-DB end-to-end test (`sec-021-actor-id-audit-log.integration.test.ts`, spawns a real hrms-service child process + real disposable Postgres, sends a bearer-token-only request through the real gateway, polls `employee.hrms_audit_log` for a real row) -- satisfies this row's own DoD directly, not just header-presence. Sabotage-checked: reverted `jwt-edge.ts` alone (all 6 new tests fail, real row absent); restored, reverted `app.ts`'s allowlist line alone (unit tests still pass, both integration-level tests and the real-DB test fail); restored both, all 11 pass. `gateway-service` full suite: 182/182 passing. `hrms-service` full suite: 4468/4478 passing, one pre-existing failure unrelated to this change (`GET /v1/hrms/id-cards/me` 404s; zero hrms-service files touched by this PR; reproduces in isolation on unmodified `origin/main`; tracked separately). Fixed (PR #1267) -- independently reviewed clean (traced verifyJwt()'s single choke point to confirm sub is populated identically on both the RS256/Keycloak and HS256/dev paths, confirmed the FORWARD_HEADERS gap affected both auth paths via shared branch-free proxy code, reproduced both sabotage checks at per-test granularity, ran the full gateway-service and hrms-service suites live). |
| SEC-022 | P3 | tenant-table-rls-guard.mjs can't detect a tenant_id column added via a later ALTER TABLE, only CREATE TABLE | Found during SEC-010's review: scripts/ci/tenant-table-rls-guard.mjs's findTenantTableViolations only inspects a migration's CREATE TABLE column list -- a tenant_id column added via a separate ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN statement (even within the same migration file) is invisible to it, so a table that gains tenant-scoping this way would never be flagged as needing RLS. No such pattern exists anywhere in the fleet's migrations today (confirmed by the reviewer), so this is a latent blind spot, not a live gap | Extend findTenantTableViolations to also scan ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN statements for a tenant_id addition against a table it already knows about, treating it the same as a CREATE TABLE-time tenant_id for RLS-requirement purposes. **DoD:** a synthetic migration that creates a table without tenant_id then ALTERs it to add one is caught by the guard as needing RLS | Fixed (PR #1264) -- independently reviewed clean (wrote its own fixture cases beyond the PR's, confirmed byte-for-byte identical fleet-wide output between sabotaged and fixed states across all 1156 migrations) -- findTenantTableViolations() extended to also scan ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN statements for a tenant_id addition against a table already known to exist (this file's own earlier CREATE TABLEs, or an earlier migration file for the same service, via a knownTables map main() now threads across each service's files in sorted order); the ALTER's own file is held to the same same-file-RLS requirement as a CREATE-TABLE-time tenant_id. **Corrects this gap's original evidence**: it claimed no such pattern exists anywhere in the fleet today -- that is wrong, two real migrations exercise it (admin-service 0004b_missing_module_tables.sql -> 0014_webhook_lifecycle.sql, webhooks.webhook_deliveries; payroll-service 0012_p1_challan_taxcfg_perq_26q.sql -> 0039_tax_slab_config_tenant_scope.sql, payroll.tax_slab_config) -- but both already carry ENABLE + FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY + CREATE POLICY in the same file as their ADD COLUMN, so both pass clean under the fixed guard and the baseline needs no new entries for them; the blind spot was real, it simply had not yet been exercised by a live vulnerability, only by two already-careful migrations. 8 new fixture tests added to tests/architecture/tenant-table-rls-guard.test.ts, confirmed red against the pre-fix guard (4 of the 8 fail without the fix, the other 4 already trivially passed), green after; sabotage-checked (git stash of just the .mjs fix reproduces the exact same 4 failures, restoring it is green again). Fleet-wide run against all 1156 migration files: baseline needs no change from this fix (1007/1007 tracked entries unchanged, 0 stale), aside from one unrelated pre-existing finding -- identity-service scim.scim_tokens (migration 0022_scim_per_tenant_tokens.sql) -- which reproduces identically against the unmodified pre-fix guard (its CREATE TABLE carries tenant_id directly, caught by the original untouched code path), so it predates and is independent of this change; deliberately not absorbed into the baseline here since SEC-025 (Open) already tracks the real fix for it (the table is deliberately RLS-free per its own migration's documented rationale; SEC-025 proposes the hybrid FORCE-RLS design that would close it) -- only the baseline regen is missing, flagged for whoever picks up SEC-025 rather than silently fixed here. Guard's own unit suite 17/17 pass; full tests/architecture/ suite 157/157 pass, no regressions. Not independently reviewed -- do not mark Fixed yet. |
| SEC-023 | P2 | API-key authentication can never reach any non-public proxied route — the gateway's own auth gate ignores `req.apiKeyAuthenticated` | Found while building SEC-008's api-key-path regression test (PR #1225): `gateway-service/src/app.ts`'s `proxyHandler` has its own independent auth check ("Enforce authentication for all non-public routes") that only ever tests for a literal `Authorization: Bearer` header (`if (!auth \|\| !auth.toLowerCase().startsWith("bearer ")) return reply.code(401)...`) and never consults `req.apiKeyAuthenticated` — the flag `api-key-auth.ts`'s `apiKeyPreHandler` sets after successfully verifying a presented `x-api-key` against identity-service and injecting the verified tenant/actor headers. Confirmed directly against `buildApp()` on unmodified `main`: a request with a verified, active, in-scope `x-api-key` and no `Authorization` header gets 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED` ("missing or invalid authorization header") on `/api/v1/finance/bills`; it only succeeds on a `PUBLIC_PREFIXES` route because `proxyHandler` skips this check entirely there. Not a security hole itself (fails closed), but the whole "W1.4 API Key authentication" feature is dead on arrival for every real business route today. Cross-reference: SEC-024, found independently during this same review round, is an earlier-stage bug in the same feature (the verify call itself 404s against a nonexistent identity-service path) — in the real deployed system SEC-024 would prevent ever reaching this check at all, so both must be fixed together for API-key auth to work end-to-end | Make the check also accept an already-authenticated api-key request, e.g. `if (!req.apiKeyAuthenticated && (!auth \|\| !auth.toLowerCase().startsWith("bearer "))) { ...401... }`. **DoD:** an end-to-end `app.inject()` test with a verified, in-scope `x-api-key` and no `Authorization` header successfully reaches a NON-public proxied route (not just a public one) | Fixed (PR #1250) — independently reviewed clean (reproduced the bug/fix/sabotage from scratch on a separate baseline worktree, confirmed the fix logic algebraically, confirmed the negative control still 401s) — reproduced first (new test failed `expected 401 to be 200` against unmodified `main`; the then-unmodified SEC-008 non-public-route test independently confirmed the same broken state by passing its own old `[401,401,401,429]` expectation), then fixed: proxyHandler's gate now also accepts an already-verified api-key request (`req.apiKeyAuthenticated`), matching this row's own suggested fix exactly. Regression test `services/gateway-service/tests/sec-023-apikey-nonpublic-auth.test.ts` meets the DoD above (verified, in-scope `x-api-key`, no `Authorization` header, reaches `/api/v1/finance/bills` — 200) plus a negative-control case (neither api key nor Bearer header — still 401, proving the fix narrows the gate rather than removing it). Sabotage-checked: reverting only the app.ts change (test files unchanged) reproduces both failures exactly; restoring fixes both. Also updated `sec-008-tenant-rate-limit.test.ts`'s own non-public-route api-key test, which had hard-coded the pre-fix `[401,401,401,429]` as its expected result (SEC-008's own PR explicitly flagged this gate as a bonus find, out of scope there) — now `[200,200,200,429]`, since this fix changes what those under-budget requests get from the upstream; the rate limiter's own keying behavior is unchanged. Full gateway-service suite (18 files / 171 tests, up from SEC-008's 17/169) green against a freshly bootstrapped, isolated, disposable `postgres:16-alpine` on a confirmed-free port (25943; gateway-service's own two migrations applied; container removed after) — shared `civitas-postgres` (:5435), Keycloak (:8180), :8080/:3000, and any shared Redis never touched (rate-limit tier left on its in-memory store, no REDIS_URL/GATEWAY_REDIS_URL set, per docs/TEST-INFRA.md). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Does **not** fix SEC-024 (confirmed still Open as of this PR) — both are needed for API-key auth to work end-to-end against the real identity-service; SEC-024 tracked separately. Not marking Fixed myself — that follows independent review, per this campaign's convention. |
| SEC-024 | P2 | Gateway's api-key verify call hits a path that doesn't exist -- api-key auth can never even verify, separate from SEC-023's later-stage bug | Found and independently confirmed while dispatching SEC-008's review: services/gateway-service/src/api-key-auth.ts:48 calls fetch(`${IDENTITY_URL}/internal/apikeys/verify`, ...), but identity-service only registers app.post("/identity/api-keys/verify", ...) (services/identity-service/src/modules/apikeys/routes.ts:70) -- different prefix (/internal vs /identity) AND different hyphenation (apikeys vs api-keys). Confirmed by grep: no route matching /internal/apikeys/verify exists anywhere in identity-service. This means apiKeyPreHandler's verify call 404s for every request, so req.apiKeyAuthenticated can never be set to true regardless of whether the presented key is valid -- a distinct, earlier-stage failure from SEC-023 (proxyHandler ignoring req.apiKeyAuthenticated even when it IS set). Both bugs independently make API-key authentication completely non-functional today; fixing only one leaves the feature still dead | Point api-key-auth.ts's fetch call at the real registered path (/identity/api-keys/verify), or add a route alias at /internal/apikeys/verify if that path is meant to be the stable internal-service-to-service contract and /identity/api-keys/verify is the one that should move -- pick whichever this repo's other internal-service-call conventions favor. **DoD:** apiKeyPreHandler's verify call actually reaches identity-service and returns a real verify result for a valid key, confirmed via a real HTTP call in a test, not a mock of the fetch call | Fixed (PR #1258) -- independently reviewed clean (proved each of the 4 sub-fixes is separately necessary via direct source analysis, verified the new RLS grant is exactly SELECT on one table nothing broader, reproduced the full live end-to-end path with a real child process and unmocked HTTP) -- reproduced first (real, unmocked HTTP against a freshly spawned identity-service: confirmed the 404 on unmodified main). Fixed four independently-real breaks stacked in the same path: (1) registered POST /internal/apikeys/verify on identity-service (modules/apikeys/internal-routes.ts), gated by the existing-but-previously-unused assertGatewayRequest mechanism (x-gateway-request + x-internal-secret) -- deliberately NOT a reuse of the admin route, which requires a caller-asserted tenant and is backwards for a tenant-DISCOVERY call; (2) api-key-auth.ts now sends the matching x-gateway-request header and the raw key (not a pre-hashed one -- identity hashes it itself); (3) api-key-auth.ts now reads the real VerifyResult shape (valid/apiKeyId/tenantId/ownerId/scopes/reason) instead of an invented shape (id/status/expiresAt) that never matched anything identity-service returns and would have rejected every key as "inactive" even with routing fixed; (4) apikeys.api_keys carries FORCE RLS keyed on the tenant GUC, but this lookup's whole purpose is discovering the tenant with none known yet -- verifyApiKey now looks up via the identity_scanner BYPASSRLS role (migration 0023 grants SELECT) and writes under runWithTenant(row.tenantId, ...) once the tenant is known, mirroring sessions/repo.ts#reapExpiredSessions. Sabotage-checked: reverting the fix while keeping the new tests reproduces the original 404, and the gateway's two valid-key assertions flip from 200/403 to 401 -- restoring makes all pass again. Tests: identity-service/tests/sec-024-internal-verify-route.test.ts (in-process, real DB, every verify outcome) + gateway-service/tests/sec-024-verify-real-http.test.ts (spawns identity-service as a REAL child process, drives apiKeyPreHandler's own unmocked fetch() across real HTTP -- matches this row's own DoD exactly). Also fixed two other tests' stale api-key-verify mocks (sec-008-tenant-rate-limit.test.ts, sec-023-apikey-nonpublic-auth.test.ts) to the real contract, surfaced by rebasing onto SEC-023's own merge. Full gateway-service (19 files/176 tests) and identity-service (36 files/438 tests) suites green against a freshly bootstrapped, isolated, disposable postgres:16-alpine (ratchet holding); civitasone-postgres (:5435), Keycloak (:8180), and :8080/:3000 never touched. |
| SEC-025 | P3 | SEC-007 hardening follow-ups: no DB-level backstop on scim_tokens writes; new token-management routes lack a negative-role test | Found during SEC-007's independent review (PR #1227): (1) scim_tokens deliberately has no RLS (necessary for the hash-lookup-before-tenant-is-known path), but write paths (touchLastUsed/insert/revoke) rely solely on application-code tenant_id filtering with no DB-level backstop -- the reviewer empirically verified a hybrid design (FORCE RLS + a permissive SELECT-by-hash policy + tenant-scoped INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies) works and would close this gap, mirroring this project's own SEC-009/SEC-010 lesson about app-code-only tenant filtering. (2) the new POST/GET /identity/scim-tokens and revoke routes have no test confirming a non-admin role gets 403 -- requireRole/hasAnyRole is shared, pre-existing, presumably covered elsewhere, but this PR's own test file doesn't exercise the negative case for its new routes | (1) Add FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY + a permissive SELECT-by-hash-only policy + tenant-scoped write policies to scim_tokens, verified via the same before/after/sabotage pattern this campaign uses elsewhere. (2) Add a test proving a non-admin caller gets 403 from all three token-management routes. **DoD:** a DB-level write outside the token's own bound tenant is rejected even if application code had a bug; a non-admin role cannot reach any of the 3 new routes | Fixed (PR #1271) -- independently reviewed clean (queried live pg_policy to confirm the exact 4-policy design, reproduced the pre-fix cross-tenant write live then sabotage-checked FORCE RLS both ways, ran the full 37-file/448-test suite) -- added migrations/0024_sec025_scim_tokens_rls.sql (FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY + a permissive SELECT-by-hash-only policy + tenant-scoped INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies on scim.scim_tokens) and the companion app-code fix: token-repo.ts's insert/touchLastUsed/revoke now each run inside runWithTenant(tenantId, () => db.transaction(...)) internally (kept out of routes.ts so the f3-b2-mfa-scim-cqrs.test.ts CQRS guard still passes) -- 4 call sites needed it, including two not mentioned in this row (resolveScimTenant's touchLastUsed, bootstrapLegacyScimToken's insert). New tests/sec-025-scim-tokens-rls.test.ts (10 tests): DB-level cross-tenant UPDATE/DELETE/INSERT all rejected, same-tenant write and no-GUC SELECT-by-hash unaffected, and 403-for-non-admin on all 3 token-management routes. Note: SEC-021/SEC-024 (named in this work's task prompt as precedent) turned out not to establish this pattern -- SEC-021 is unrelated and SEC-024 used a different mechanism (a BYPASSRLS scanner role) for a similar problem; the permissive-SELECT-policy design actually used here is this row's own Suggested Fix. Sabotage-checked twice (dropping FORCE RLS on a live disposable cluster flips exactly 5/10 new tests, restoring returns to clean). Full identity-service suite (37 files/448 tests) green against a freshly bootstrapped, isolated, disposable postgres:16-alpine; civitasone-postgres (:5435), Keycloak (:8180), :8080/:3000 never touched. |
| SEC-026 | P3 | SEC-024's real-HTTP test claims it refuses to run without an explicit DB override, but an env-var shadow means it can silently target the shared DB instead | Found during SEC-024's review: gateway-service/tests/sec-024-verify-real-http.test.ts's header comment claims the suite refuses to run without an explicit override and never touches civitasone-postgres (5435) -- but gateway-service's own vitest.config.ts unconditionally sets DATABASE_URL from GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL with a hardcoded 5435 fallback, silently shadowing the two env vars this test's own guard actually checks. The reviewer hit this directly: the test did NOT refuse to run, it proceeded against the shared DB and failed loud (a missing relation) before any write occurred -- no data was written, but the comment's safety claim doesn't hold as written. Not a defect in the shipped fix, purely a test-ergonomics gap in this new file's own self-protection | Have the test assert its actually-resolved DB host:port isn't 5435 (or otherwise verify it's really pointed at a disposable instance) rather than trusting env-var presence, which vitest.config.ts's own fallback can mask. **DoD:** running this test suite with no override set fails immediately with a clear message, not a confusing downstream relation error | Fixed (PR #1299, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- test now asserts the actually-resolved DB port isn't 5435 in beforeAll instead of trusting DATABASE_URL/DB_URL presence; reproduced the original failure (35s, confusing timeout), fixed, sabotage-checked both directions (no override -> immediate clear refusal; real override on a different port -> guard doesn't fire), gateway-service suite 176/182 tests green (one pre-existing unrelated failure in sec-021's test, same guard pattern, flagged separately), tsc clean. | SEC-027 | P3 | SEC-015's fail-open session creation has zero operational signal when it silently fails -- a login's token becomes permanently unrevocable with no metric, alert, or admin-visible indicator | Found during SEC-015's independent review: apps/web/src/app/api/auth/callback/route.ts's best-effort call to create a real sessions row (SEC-015) only logs via a single console.error on failure -- no metric, no alert, no admin-visible indicator anywhere. Confirmed the actual consequence empirically: identity-service's DELETE /identity/sessions/:id handler 404s (via queries.getSession) before ever calling revokeSession when no row exists for that id, so if the best-effort create silently failed for a given login, that user's real, live token is permanently unrevocable via this mechanism for its full lifetime -- with nothing surfacing the failure to anyone who could act on it. Not client-exploitable (an attacker cannot selectively target their own or another user's session this way -- an outage affects all concurrent logins equally, not a chosen one), so this was correctly left non-blocking for SEC-015 itself, but it's a real, silent reliability/security-observability gap worth closing on its own | Add structured logging (not just console.error) and a metric/counter for session-creation failures on the login path, wired into this codebase's existing observability conventions (see packages/observability's captureError() pattern, already used fleet-wide for exactly this kind of "silent but important" failure). Consider also a reconciliation job or admin-visible indicator for logins with no corresponding sessions row. **DoD:** a simulated session-creation failure on login produces a real, queryable signal (metric increment and/or structured log line) that an operator could alert on, not just a line in raw server logs | Fixed (PR #1306, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14, 2 rounds) -- createBackendSession() now reports every failure path (claims decode, missing claims, the identity-service fetch, a non-ok response) via captureError(): an in-memory counter (getCapturedErrorCountByService("web")) and a structured JSON log line, both real today. CORRECTED after independent review: this row previously claimed the counter was "already covered by the existing CapturedErrorsAppearing alert" -- false, not verified before writing it. Actually true: apps/web has no /metrics route (registerOpsRoutes() is Fastify-only) and infra/observability/prometheus.yml has no scrape job for the web app, so the metric is not yet Prometheus-scraped or alertable. Investigated wiring a real /metrics route: found that Next.js self-hosted (next start) does not safely expose the true peer IP the way Fastify does (base-server.js only falls back to the real socket address when x-forwarded-for is absent, so a direct, non-proxied scrape -- the same pattern the other 9 service groups use -- would make an internal-IP guard spoofable); a safe equivalent needs either a custom server or a METRICS_TOKEN with a real secret provisioned in the live deployment, neither reasonable to force into this PR. Recommending the Prometheus-scrape path as its own follow-up gap (next free SEC id) at review-close time. Captured context is userId/tenantId only, never the access token. New regression tests (3, including a claims-decode-throws case added after review) and sabotage-checked twice: each fails against its pre-fix code for the right reason, passes after. Full apps/web suite 3110/3111 (1 pre-existing, unrelated failure); packages/observability 22/22; tsc clean both times. Reconciliation job / admin-visible indicator considered and deliberately deferred (rationale in the PR). Round 1 review found the alert-wiring claim was false as shipped; fixed by honestly correcting the claim after investigating and confirming a real fix isn't safely completable in this PR (see SEC-029, filed as the recommended follow-up). Round 2 independently verified the PR description carries no artifact from an earlier body-edit mistake, verified the spoofability reasoning from source (confirmed the Fastify guard is NOT spoofable today since no trustProxy is configured, sharpening why a naive Next.js port would be worse, not just different), reproduced both sabotage-checks, and reran the full suite fresh (3111/3112, reconciling exactly with the 3rd added test). |

SEC-027's own review investigated wiring a naive scrape route and found it's not safe to do quickly: Next.js's request pipeline (base-server.js) only backfills x-forwarded-for when the header is absent, so a direct scrape-guard modeled on the backend services' isInternalIP() pattern would be trivially spoofable by any caller claiming an internal-looking XFF header -- worse than not having the metric visible at all, since it would look secure while not being. Full gateway-service suite on a fresh disposable Postgres: 172/182 passed (10 skipped) -- sec-021 and sec-024 themselves both pass cleanly (sec-021 completes a full real end-to-end audit-log flow); the 2 failing files (catalogue-consumer/-routes) are unrelated, a pre-existing missing-relation gap on this specific disposable bootstrap, not touched by this change. tsc --noEmit clean. Independent review deterministically confirmed the regression on unfixed code (CI-simulated run against origin/main threw the refusal in 1.5s) and the fix working (same simulated env reached a real, unrelated Postgres error instead), confirmed the dev-host protection is unchanged, and reran the full suite fresh on its own bootstrap: 182/182 passed, 0 failures -- the 2 catalogue failures the fixer saw did NOT reproduce at all, stronger evidence they were environment-specific noise on that specific disposable bootstrap, not a real pre-existing gap. |

| TX-011 | P2 | Deadlock scanner blind spots | `/tmp/scan_nested_tx_v2.py` (not in repo): ignores `export const … = async` repos, `tenantTransaction/scopedRead` as the *outer* block, and >1-level transitive calls (grant `dashboard/queries.ts:71` found only by a deep scan) | Commit an improved scanner to `scripts/ci/`; gate in Architecture Guard. **DoD:** scanner finds the grant case; baseline 0 after TX-001 | Fixed (PR #1237) — independently reviewed clean (reproduced both still-fixed checks, stress-tested cycle-safety with 4 independent recursion cases beyond the fixture suite, confirmed the suppression is narrow via 5 independently-constructed near-miss cases; one narrative-accuracy correction requested and applied, no code changes needed) — `scripts/ci/nested-tx-guard.mjs` committed and gated as two new Architecture Guard steps (unit tests, then the real tree), following tenant-table-rls-guard.mjs's exact convention (keyed-entry baseline ratchet, `--write-baseline`, per-service call-graph scope). Closes all 3 named blind spots: (1) indexes both `export function foo` and `export const foo = async (...) => {...}` as call-graph nodes; (2) treats every `tenantTransaction()`/`scopedRead()`/`<...db>.transaction()` call symmetrically as a possible outer root AND a possible nested violation, no special-casing; (3) does a cycle-safe, unbounded-depth BFS over the per-service call graph (not a fixed depth) — documented rationale: a fixed cutoff would just relocate the blind spot, and TX-001's own install-service finding was already 2 hops deep. Verified against the two real fleet cases this gap and TX-001 cite: reverting grant-service's `getDashboard()` to its pre-fix bare `getOverdueApplicationIds(tenantId)` call is caught (confirmed against the actual file, not just a synthetic fixture); the fixed `dashboard/queries.ts` and install-service's fixed `resolveDag()`/`repo.getStepDefinitionsTx` chain both correctly scan clean. 23 fixture tests (`tests/architecture/nested-tx-guard.test.ts`): the 3 blind spots each proven caught (incl. a synthetic 3-level chain, deeper than either real example, proving no hardcoded cutoff), sabotage-check controls (clean code stays clean, `tx.transaction()` savepoints excluded as a different risk class, comments/strings never count), template-literal safety (this fleet's drizzle `sql\`...\`` tags routinely contain parens inside string text that must not desync brace/paren counting), and a dual-mode-helper refinement (see below) with its own positive/negative fixtures. Run fleet-wide (66 services, ~1.7s): first pass found 11 hits; manual verification against source found 3 false positives (estab `upsertBalance`, grant `sumDisbursedForApplication`, procurement `insertBlacklist`/`insertBlacklistTx` (`vendor-blacklist/consumer.ts:42,149`) — all three a legitimate, recurring "opens its own transaction only as a fallback when the caller didn't supply one" idiom this fleet uses, gated on `if (tx)`/`if (tx === db)`/`if (writer) {...}` shapes) — added a narrow, documented refinement (only suppresses an opener gated behind a bare truthy/equality check on the function's OWN parameter, braced or unbraced early-return form; does not suppress on any other condition, proven by its own control fixtures) that correctly resolved all three without needing real dataflow analysis. Baseline (`scripts/ci/nested-tx-baseline.json`) ends at 7 entries (not 0): 3 distinct, independently source-verified genuine nested-transaction sites TX-001's sweep and its own `scan_nested_tx_v2.py` missed (finance-service, notification-service, visitor-service — all 2+ hops deep, the exact TX-011 blind-spot shape) — filed as **TX-018**, not fixed here (out of this gap's own scope; DoD is the scanner, not the remaining fleet sweep) and not silently absorbed into the ratchet. PR not yet merged — do not mark Fixed until independently reviewed. |
