| TX-013 | P1 | HRMS audit trail has been a 100% silent no-op since it was written; no `audit.hr_action_log` table exists anywhere | `hrms-service/src/shared/audit.ts:52-77` (`writeAuditLog`, doc comment "Fire-and-forget: never throws, never delays a response", catch-and-`log.error` only) inserts into `audit.hr_action_log` on every HRMS mutating action; `git grep` across every `services/*/migrations/*.sql` finds zero migrations creating that table in any service — invisible to skill 16's audit-coverage check (T5), which only greps for the `enqueue(tx, AUDIT)` call existing, not whether its target table exists. Discovered only because REL-001 unblocked the `Tests` job: every hrms-service test that mutates data now logs `[audit] write failed` / `relation "audit.hr_action_log" does not exist`. | Add the missing migration; assert on `writeAuditLog` actually persisting in at least one integration test (not just that it doesn't throw). **DoD:** a real HRMS mutation produces a row in `audit.hr_action_log`; CI fails if that regresses | Fixed (PR #1123) — `audit.hr_action_log` migration added; integration test asserts a real employee-create mutation persists a row (not just no-throw), passing in CI |
| TX-015 | P3 | ~70 hrms-service test files stub `sqlClient` with an incomplete double that omits `.begin`, silently exercising a raw-`.unsafe()` fallback path instead of the real transactional/tenant-GUC-scoped path | Found fixing TX-013: `audit.ts`'s tenant-scoped insert (`withRawTenantGuc`) needs a real `sqlClient.begin()`-capable client; ~70 of `hrms-service`'s `vi.mock("../src/shared/db.js")` test doubles don't expose `.begin`, so those tests fall back to the pre-fix, non-tenant-scoped `.unsafe()` call path and never actually exercise `withRawTenantGuc`. Confirmed harmless in production (the real `sqlClient` always has `.begin` — see `packages/db/src/pool.ts`), but it means this large slice of the test suite provides no coverage of the tenant-scoping behavior it silently bypasses | Extend the shared `sqlClient` test-double factory to include a real (or realistically-shaped) `.begin`, so these ~70 files exercise the same code path production traffic does. **DoD:** the fallback branch in `audit.ts`/`raw-tenant-guc.ts` callers has zero remaining test-double-only callers; removing the fallback doesn't break any test | Fixed (PR #1343, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- re-derived the true scope from source rather than trusting the "~70" estimate: 89 files actually mock `shared/db.js`; classified all 89 against `withRawTenantGuc`'s real contract and found only 24 genuinely silently-broken + reachable (4 more share the broken stub shape but are consumer/repo tests that structurally can't reach `writeAuditLog`, fixed anyway for uniformity; 38 already inherit the real client via `importOriginal` spread; 2 already had a correct hand-built double; 21 are out of scope). Added `tests/fixtures/mock-sql-client.ts` (shared `createMockSqlClient()`, matching the existing `id-cards-routes.test.ts`/`medical-routes.test.ts` precedent for why the mock's base must be a plain arrow function, not `vi.fn(...)`) and wired it into the 28 affected files (3 of which, living under `src/`, inline the same construction locally since `tsconfig.json`'s `rootDir: "./src"` rejects a cross-boundary import). Verified with a temporary env-gated probe instrumenting both branches of the old `if`/`else`: before, 499 FALLBACK / 1047 GUC; after, 0 FALLBACK / 1546 GUC -- exact 499 -> GUC shift. With the fallback proven dead (real `sqlClient` always has `.begin`), removed the `if`/`else` in `audit.ts` entirely. Full `hrms-service` suite (4478 tests) on a freshly-migrated DB is byte-identical before and after this whole change: 1 pre-existing, unrelated failure (`routes-coverage-g.test.ts` id-cards/me 404), 4468 passed, 9 skipped. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Also surfaced (not this gap's bug, noted in the PR): re-running the suite repeatedly against the same disposable Postgres rather than a fresh one intermittently fails a `*-consumer*` test on a queue-processed marker row -- the same pre-existing flake class TX-013's own PR independently hit under a different file name; disappears on a fresh DB every time, orthogonal to TX-015; cross-checked against TX-013's actual PR body and confirmed genuine independent corroboration of the identical flake class under a different filename. Independent review independently re-derived the full 89-file classification from scratch with its own script (reproduced the exact 28/38/2/21 bucket sizes), manually read/diffed 13 individual files spanning every bucket (all correctly classified), empirically reproduced both TypeScript claims by deliberately breaking each one and confirming the exact predicted compiler errors, reproduced the before/after instrumented evidence itself on the pre-fix commit, and reran the full suite fresh on its own disposable Postgres (exact match: 1 failed, 260 passed, 4468/4477 tests). The largest and most rigorously reviewed change of this whole campaign. |
| TX-014 | P2 | Two hrms-service workforce-planning dashboard endpoints 500 on every real call; backing tables never migrated | `hrms-service/src/modules/workforce-planning/routes.ts:151-169` (`GET /v1/hrms/workforce/budget` joins `employee.position_budget`, no error handling), `:186` (`employee.employee_profiles`, same file) — same "discovered only once REL-001 unblocked `Tests`" pattern as TX-013; zero migrations create either table in any service | Add the migrations, or 501 the endpoints with `source:"stub"` until they're built (per DOM-015's pattern) — never a route that only 500s. **DoD:** both endpoints return 200 with real data or an honest 501 in a test | Fixed (PR #1162) — both endpoints now return `501 {code: "NOT_BUILT", source: "stub"}` instead of the raw-query 500; regression test covers the 501 contract plus the reader-role auth gate on both routes. Verified against a fresh, isolated Postgres container (never the shared dev DB): sabotage check confirmed the fix is load-bearing (reverting only `routes.ts` reproduces the original `PostgresError 42P01` → 500); full `hrms-service` suite 255/257 files, 4440/4451 tests passed (2 pre-existing failures, unrelated modules: seniority-consumer coverage, ID-cards route); `tsc --noEmit` clean |
| TX-016 | P2 | knowledge-service version restore has a non-atomic "read latest versionNo, +1, insert" race: concurrent restores/creates on the SAME document can violate the `document_versions_doc_version_uk` unique constraint, silently dropping one command into the DLQ | Found verifying TX-001's knowledge-service fix: `knowledge-service/src/modules/versions/consumer.ts`'s `versionRestore` handler (and equally `versionCreate`, same shape) computes `nextVersionNo = getLatestVersionNo(...) + 1` then inserts — two concurrent handlers for the same `documentId` can both read the same max and then race on the same `versionNo`, one losing to the unique constraint and erroring into the queue's DLQ instead of getting a distinct version number. Reproduced live: 13 concurrent `versionRestore` commands targeting one shared document threw `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "document_versions_doc_version_uk"` on all but one. Not a TX-001 site (no nested transaction/pool exhaustion involved — this is a correctness race within a single, already-well-scoped transaction) | Serialize version-number assignment per document: either a DB sequence/`nextval` per document, an `ON CONFLICT` retry loop, or a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the document's latest-version row before computing `+1`. **DoD:** the same concurrent-restore scenario (N concurrent commands, same document) yields N distinct version numbers and an empty DLQ | Fixed (PR #1159) -- repo.lockVersionSeq(): pg_advisory_xact_lock keyed on (tenantId, documentId), taken before getLatestVersionNoTx() in both versionRestore and versionCreate consumer handlers; regression test at 15 concurrent versionRestore commands on the SAME document (real Postgres, no mocks) asserts 15 distinct contiguous version numbers and an empty DLQ; sabotage-checked (reverting the lock reproduces the race -- 6/15 commands lost to the DLQ on a single run) |
| TX-017 | P2 | `services/trade-service/tests/cross-service-integration.test.ts` reproducibly fails with `PostgresError: invalid input syntax for type uuid: ""` on a fresh, correctly-provisioned Postgres -- root cause NOT receiptHeadCode (that theory is disproven, see below); the exact trigger is still unresolved -- **RESOLVED 2026-09-13 (PR #1282):** the trigger was never the database's data or trade-service's code -- it was the test silently connecting to this project's shared, long-lived dev Postgres instead of a disposable one; see below | **Corrected entry** -- an earlier version of this row (filed while verifying PR #1187's CI DB-wiring fix) blamed a missing `receiptHeadCode` at `trade-service/src/modules/applications/consumer.ts:119`. That mechanism is disproven: `packages/events/src/municipal-cross.ts`'s `buildMunicipalFeeChallanPayload` defaults `receiptHeadCode: opts.receiptHeadCode ?? MUNICIPAL_FEE_RECEIPT_HEAD_CODE` ('0075'), confirmed by reading the source directly -- the code always reaches finance-service regardless of whether the call site passes it, and all 19 municipal `emitMunicipalFeeChallan` call sites (including roadcut-service) rely on this same default, so trade-service is not an outlier. The failure IS real and reproducible, though: confirmed 4 times independently, across 2 disposable Postgres images (`postgres:16-alpine` and the CI-matching `postgis/postgis:16-3.4`, both freshly bootstrapped via `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` with 0 unexpected migration failures) -- so it is not an image/environment artifact. Query-level tracing (`DB_QUERY_DEBUG=true`, which makes postgres-js's thrown-error `.query`/`.parameters` enumerable per `packages/db/src/pool.ts:158`) shows the failing statement is `relayOnce`'s own plain outbox read (`packages/outbox/src/index.ts:116-121`: `select ... from _outbox.messages where published_at is null order by created_at asc limit $1`), invoked from the test as `relayOnce(financeDb, q, 100, "finance-service")` right after `submitApplication`'s cross-service flow enqueues into finance-service's outbox -- the error surfaces while the driver decodes the RESULT rows (a `uuid`-typed column), not the `$1` parameter. A freshly-bootstrapped `civitas_finance` database's `_outbox.messages` table already holds roughly 528,000 rows before this test ever runs (topics observed: `finance.instrument.issued`, `audit.event.record`), of which roughly 1,100 are already unpublished (`published_at IS NULL`) -- i.e. `relayOnce`'s unfiltered, oldest-first, unbounded-by-tenant/topic query returns 100 of these pre-existing rows ahead of the test's own newly-enqueued message, every single run. This backlog's existence and rough size are the concrete, reproducible part of this finding. NOT yet isolated: a raw `::text`-cast read of the same rows (bypassing native uuid decoding, run as the cluster superuser) found zero rows with an empty string in `tenant_id`/`actor_id`/`correlation_id` across the full ~528K backlog -- so the exact row/column that trips the typed decode is still unknown; it may be a different uuid-typed column not covered by that cast, a driver-level artifact specific to the typed query path, or something about the newly-enqueued row itself rather than the backlog. An independent review of PR #1187 reported this same test file passing 18/18 in one run; that could not be reproduced on a fresh container on repeated attempts here, and the discrepancy is unresolved -- flagging rather than guessing further. **Resolved 2026-09-13 (PR #1282):** root-caused. Reproduced fresh in an isolated `postgres:16-alpine` container (port 55432, clear of shared ports 5435/8180/8080/3000), bootstrapped via `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` with 0 unexpected migration failures (ratchet holding) -- the test passed cleanly with ZERO code changes: 3 consecutive runs of this file (7/7 tests each), then trade-service's full suite (3 files / 43 tests), all green. The uuid decode error does not reproduce against genuinely fresh data. Traced the actual cause to the test environment, not the app or the data: this project's shared dev EC2 host runs `civitasone-postgres` (`infra/docker-compose.yml`, `container_name: civitasone-postgres`) as a LONG-LIVED container permanently bound to port 5435 -- the exact port `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` defaults to, trade-service's `vitest.config.ts` DATABASE_URL fallback defaults to, and this test file's own FINANCE_URL/NOTIFICATION_URL fallbacks default to. Confirmed read-only (civitas_admin role, zero writes) that the shared instance's `civitas_finance._outbox.messages` holds 529,438 rows (2,178 unpublished as of 2026-09-13, up from this row's original ~528K/~1,100 a few days earlier), topics and magnitude matching this row's original report almost exactly, dated 2026-09-03 through 2026-09-12 -- real accumulated activity from finance-service/hrms-service/payroll-service worker processes that have run against it continuously for 2+ weeks (confirmed via `ps aux` on the host), NOT seed/demo data and NOT a migration artifact (checked `scripts/ci/bootstrap-postgres.sh` and every migration that touches `_outbox.messages` or creates partitions -- none seed or bulk-insert outbox rows). A byte-level dump (`octet_length(col::text)` grouped by value, across all 529,438 rows and every partition, not just the ~1,100 unpublished ones originally sampled) of EVERY uuid-typed column `_outbox.messages` has -- `id`, `tenant_id`, `actor_id`, confirmed exhaustively via `information_schema.columns` against the live table and all its partitions (messages/messages_default/messages_legacy/messages_y2026m07..m11), not just the 3 columns the Drizzle model declares -- found ZERO malformed values: every row has exactly a clean 36-character uuid in all three columns, no exceptions, no NULLs. Bad/missing data as a cause is now conclusively ruled out, for the entire backlog, not just the originally-sampled unpublished rows. Independent second signal: the shared instance's `_outbox.messages` still has RLS ENABLED+FORCED (`relrowsecurity=t, relforcerowsecurity=t`, via `pg_class`), while a fresh bootstrap of CURRENT migrations always disables it (migration `0051_outbox_messages_drop_rls.sql`) -- proof the shared instance's schema, not just its data, has drifted stale relative to what `main` migrates to today. The exact byte-level mechanism of the original decode error was NOT pinned beyond this: given the data is proven clean, the likeliest explanation is a driver/resource-contention artifact decoding a 100-row jsonb-heavy result set from a table under heavy concurrent load (host load average observed at 6.6/9.6/19.6 from concurrent agents) rather than a deterministic code defect -- but this could not safely be re-provoked to confirm mechanistically, since doing so requires running `relayOnce()` against the shared instance, which marks whatever it reads as published, permanently corrupting real unpublished backlog rows it did not produce (a real hazard each of the 4 original reproduction attempts likely triggered unknowingly, further growing the very backlog under investigation). This also fully explains the previously "unresolved discrepancy" with the independent PR #1187 reviewer's reported 18/18 pass: real CI (GitHub Actions) has nothing else bound to port 5435, so the identical default safely resolves to an actually-fresh container there -- the failure is specific to this shared, persistent dev host, not the code or CI. | Isolate the exact failing row/column with a byte-level dump (not just a `::text` cast) of the ~1,100 unpublished backlog rows, and separately investigate why a freshly-bootstrapped dev/test database ships ~528K outbox rows at all (a seed/demo migration, most likely) and whether `relayOnce`'s test helper should filter by tenant/topic or a time cutoff so a real cross-service test's own message isn't competing with ancient backlog. **DoD:** `cross-service-integration.test.ts` passes reliably and repeatably against a freshly-bootstrapped database, and the root cause of the uuid decode error is identified and fixed, not routed around. **Fix landed (PR #1282):** added a `beforeAll` guard (`assertOutboxIsFresh`) to `cross-service-integration.test.ts` that checks each of the three services' (trade/finance/notification) `_outbox.messages` total row count before any write happens, throwing a clear, actionable error naming this row instead of proceeding -- threshold (5,000) is calibrated empirically: residue from repeated local runs against the same reused disposable container stayed in the low hundreds even after 5+ reruns (333/87/96 rows observed), while the real shared instance sits at 529,438, over 100x higher. Deliberately did NOT add a tenant/topic/time-cutoff filter to `relayOnce()` itself (`packages/outbox/src/index.ts`) as originally suggested above -- `relayOnce` is production code with an at-least-once-delivery contract over ALL unpublished rows regardless of age; filtering it would silently let genuinely stuck production messages never get relayed, trading a test-isolation problem for a production reliability regression -- exactly the unjustified band-aid this row's own DoD warned against. Verified: 3 consecutive clean runs of the target file (7/7) plus trade-service's full suite (3 files / 43 tests) against a freshly-bootstrapped disposable container; `tsc --noEmit` clean. Sabotage-check: pointed the finance leg at the real shared instance (RLS-bypassing role, read-only query only) -- guard fired immediately with its intended message; shared instance's row count independently confirmed unchanged (529,438 before and after) -- zero side effects. Follow-up flagged separately (not in this PR's scope): the identical missing-guard pattern exists in 5 sibling cross-service test files (shop-service, advertisement-service, animal-service, refund-service, parking-service) that share the same DSN-fallback-to-5435 exposure. | Fixed (PR #1282) -- independently reviewed clean (reproduced the original unmodified test clean 3x on a fresh disposable cluster -- proving the code was never broken -- confirmed the shared instance's RLS drift live and read-only, verified relayOnce()'s unfiltered at-least-once contract is genuinely production code not a test-only shortcut; found and fixed a minor afterAll robustness nit in review, filed REL-037 for an unrelated incidental finding). |
| TX-018 | P1 | 3 more nested-transaction pool-deadlock sites TX-001's sweep and its `scan_nested_tx_v2.py` missed — found and source-verified by TX-011's new `scripts/ci/nested-tx-guard.mjs` on its first fleet-wide run, all 2+ hops deep (the exact TX-011 blind-spot shape: a caller-supplied `tx` is threaded through 1-2 safe layers, then dropped by a deeper helper that takes no `tx` param at all and unconditionally opens its own) | **finance-service**: `gl/consumer.ts:296,340,356,429` and `:469` (5 call sites, all `postJournal(tx, msg, {...})`, itself correctly `tx`-scoped) → `postJournal`'s own body (`gl/consumer.ts:65`) calls `validateOrgAssignment(journal.tenantId, {...})` with NO `tx` param at all (`org-structure/domain.ts:81-101`) → unconditionally calls `assertLegalEntityExists(tenantId, legalEntityId)` (`org-structure/domain.ts:16`, confirmed: `db.transaction(...)`, no `tx` param, no guard) and — depending on which of `costCenterId`/`profitCenterId`/`operatingUnitId` the journal carries — `assertCostCenterBelongsToLE`/`assertProfitCenterBelongsToLE`/`assertOperatingUnitBelongsToLE` (`org-structure/domain.ts:29,45,61`, identical unconditional-open shape, confirmed by reading all 4). **notification-service**: `deliveries/consumer.ts:161` (inside the outer `db.transaction` opened at `:134`) calls `resolveChannelWithDefault(msg.tenantId, prefs, p.eventType, ...)` with no `tx` (`deliveries/channel.ts:62-76`) → `channelQueries.getDefaultChannel(tenantId, preferred)` (`channels/queries.ts:22`) → `repo.findDefaultChannel(tenantId, type)` (`channels/repo.ts:16-22`, confirmed: unconditional `scopedRead(...)`, no `tx` param at all). **visitor-service**: `visit-request/consumer.ts:448` (inside the outer `db.transaction`, `markProcessed(tx, ...)` confirms `tx` is live in scope) calls `hasRestrictedArea(msg.tenantId, request.locationId, permittedAreaIds)` with no `tx` (same file, def at `:221-245`, confirmed: unconditional `db.transaction(...)`; its own early-return guard, `if (permittedAreaIds.length === 0)`, is unrelated to tx-presence). Under concurrent load (GL journal posting, notification delivery, and visit approval are all realistic concurrent-traffic paths, not rare edges) each nests a second pool checkout while the outer one is held, the same silent-deadlock-under-`pool.max`-concurrency risk as TX-001's other 84 fixed sites | Same convention as TX-001: extract `...Tx(tx, ...)` siblings threading the caller's `tx` through every hop (`validateOrgAssignmentTx`/`assertLegalEntityExistsTx`/etc. for finance-service — all 4 org-structure asserts, not just the one on the shortest path; `findDefaultChannelTx`→`getDefaultChannelTx`→`resolveChannelWithDefaultTx` for notification-service; `hasRestrictedAreaTx` for visitor-service), route the nested callers, regression test at `pool.max+3` concurrency with state/DLQ assertions, sabotage-check. **DoD:** `scripts/ci/nested-tx-guard.mjs`'s baseline entries for these 3 services go to 0 (stale-entry check will fail CI until `--write-baseline` is re-run after a real fix, per its own ratchet) | Fixed (PR #1248, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- all 3 nested-transaction pool-deadlock sites (finance-service's `validateOrgAssignmentTx` chain covering all 4 org-structure asserts, notification-service's `resolveChannelWithDefaultTx` chain, visitor-service's `hasRestrictedAreaTx`) fixed via the same `*Tx`-sibling convention as TX-001. Independent review verified all 3 sites end-to-end from source (not just the shallowest call site) in a fresh worktree with its own disposable Postgres, reproduced the sabotage-check via `git apply -R` of the actual merge-commit diff (guard immediately flagged the 5 known finance-service sites; regression test reproduced a genuine drain timeout, not a crash), confirmed `nested-tx-guard.mjs` passes fleet-wide (66 services, 0 violations) with its own 23 unit tests green, and independently confirmed the one honestly-disclosed caveat (notification-service's DB-call branch is currently unreachable via production `processSend()` given this fleet's adapter registration, so its regression test necessarily drives the extracted Tx chain directly -- a real, correctly-disclosed limitation, not a discrepancy). Full suites reproducibly green across all 3 services (finance-service 117/117 files 1519/1519 tests, notification-service 74/75+1 skip 1734/1735+1 skip, visitor-service 92/92 1135/1135 after isolating one host-contention-induced flake unrelated to this fix). |

| DOM-018 | P0 | Operational: audit whether any real payroll run used the 100x-inflated `pctOfBasic` calculation REL-009 fixed | REL-009's reviewer traced the bug's introduction to commit `6e14ffa1` (2026-08-18) — live in production payroll code for ~3 weeks before the 2026-09-08 fix (PR #1110 / commit `564fa877`, confirmed by reading PR #1110's own diff). `payroll-service/src/modules/payroll/consumer.ts` maps real per-tenant `structComps` (DB-backed `pctOfBasic` column, via `listComponentsByStructure`) directly into `computeSlip` for every actual payroll run; no upstream ceiling/magnitude clamp exists on computed gross pay (only a floor-clamp for negative-net overpayment recovery). A maker-checker approval gate exists before a run reaches `approved`/`disbursed` (approver ≠ creator) — a mitigating factor, not a guarantee. No seeded/fixture data was found configuring a real tenant structure component with `pctOfBasic` (the only seeded `pct_of_basic` data found lives in a separate, unaffected, correctly-scaled CTC-calculator path) — suggestive but not conclusive that no tenant was actually exposed. **Update (this pass):** built a real audit tool (see Fix column) and ran it read-only against the shared dev Postgres reachable from the campaign host (`civitasone-postgres` container, `civitas_payroll` DB, connected as the BYPASSRLS `civitas_admin` role — the plain `payroll_svc` role silently returns 0 rows from every RLS-forced payroll table with no tenant GUC set, which would have falsely looked like "no data"). That environment has real persisted history spanning the bug window: 1,088 `payroll_slips` across 18 `payroll_runs` (9 approved/disbursed), created 2026-06-20 through 2026-09-03. The tool found 0 slip-component amounts matching the buggy 100x formula, 0 components exceeding their own slip's basic pay, and 0 tenants currently configuring any non-BASIC/DA/HRA `pctOfBasic` component — consistent with the original seed-data finding above, now checked against real run data instead of just fixtures. **This is not confirmed production** — it is this host's shared dev/staging database; whether it IS production or production is a separate, unreached target is a deployment-topology fact only a human here can supply. **Update (independent review + fix pass, this pass):** an independent reviewer found the tool had a real blind spot — its own "survives a structure being edited or deleted" claim was false for the combination of (config edited/deleted after the slip ran) + (pctOfBasic configured low enough, ~≤1%, that even a 100x-inflated amount still doesn't exceed basic pay): that produced zero findings, not even a soft warning, on a planted 100x-buggy slip. Fixed by adding a third, structural `NO_CURRENT_CONFIG` check that flags any non-ad-hoc slip component whose code has no matching `payroll_components` row at all for that tenant/structure, independent of magnitude — this cannot be defeated by a low percentage, since it never evaluates the stored amount. Verified against the reviewer's exact repro (pctOfBasic=0.5%, config removed) in a fresh isolated container: the old script found nothing, the fixed script correctly flags it. Re-ran the full 5-slip suite (EMP-A..E covering OK/CONFIRMED_BUG_SIGNATURE/IMPLAUSIBLE_MAGNITUDE/NO_CURRENT_CONFIG/UNEXPLAINED_MISMATCH) and the original 1,088-slip shared-dev-DB scan: same clean 0-finding result as before (an initial version of the new check produced 266 false positives on `LOP` deduction lines, which are ad-hoc and never sourced from `payroll_components` at all — excluded once identified, see `EXCLUDED_CODES` in the script). Separately, the reviewer found the documented `payroll_scanner` credential does not actually work — it has `BYPASSRLS` but no grant on schema `payroll` (only `_outbox`/`_inbox`, by original design — see migration 0032's own comments — so this was not a missed grant to add, it would have widened a deliberately narrow role). Added a new, purpose-built `payroll_audit_scanner` role (migration 0040) instead: `BYPASSRLS`, read-only `SELECT` on schema `payroll` only, nothing else | This needs a human decision, not an agent action: whoever owns payroll operations/production access should run `services/payroll-service/scripts/audit/dom-018-pctofbasic-check.mjs` (PR #1165) against the real production `civitas_payroll` database (via the `payroll_audit_scanner` role — migration 0040_payroll_audit_scanner_role.sql — or an equivalent BYPASSRLS credential with `SELECT` on schema `payroll`; NOT `payroll_scanner`, which lacks that grant by design; the script refuses to run without BYPASSRLS) and record the result here. If it reports any `CONFIRMED_BUG_SIGNATURE`, `NO_CURRENT_CONFIG`, or `IMPLAUSIBLE_MAGNITUDE` finding, identify the affected employees/runs and recompute/correct their pay. **DoD:** either confirmed no tenant was exposed **in the actual production database**, or affected runs identified and corrected | In progress (PR #1165) — tooling built, independently reviewed, and fixed for the blind spot the review found (structural `NO_CURRENT_CONFIG` check + working `payroll_audit_scanner` credential); proven clean against the one reachable environment and against a planted-bug repro; human action required to run it against confirmed production data before this can close |

Full-suite variance across repeated runs (15 vs 8 vs 49/49 isolated) was noted as a real but pre-existing host-load artifact unrelated to this fix -- see REL-035-adjacent flakiness follow-ups. |
| DOM-033 | P1 | The direct three-way-match endpoint's PO/GRN precondition can never pass for a genuinely-matching pair -- an escaped-caret regex bug makes it throw 409 unconditionally | Found during DOM-027's independent review: services/procurement-service/src/modules/three-way-match/routes.ts:80 does grn.poRef.replace(/\^procurement_po:/, "") -- the backslash-escaped caret makes this regex match a LITERAL '^' character, not anchor to string-start, unlike the correctly-anchored /^procurement_po:/ used consistently everywhere else this same poRef format is parsed (grn/consumer.ts:41, grn/consumer.ts:214, grn/repo.ts:77 -- confirmed by direct comparison, including a live regex test in node). Since a real poRef value never contains a literal '^', this .replace() is always a no-op: grnPoId keeps its "procurement_po:" prefix forever and can never equal the bare body.poId it's compared against, so the endpoint's own PO+GRN-match precondition throws 409 GRN_PO_MISMATCH for every genuinely-matching PO+GRN pair, unconditionally -- the endpoint cannot succeed for its actual intended use case. This pre-dates DOM-027 (byte-identical in the base commit) and is untouched by that fix. No existing test (before or after DOM-027) catches it because every HTTP-layer test uses a nonexistent PO that 404s at line 77, before ever reaching the broken line 80 | Fix the regex to /^procurement_po:/ (remove the stray backslash-escape), matching the 3 other call sites that already parse this exact poRef format correctly. **DoD:** a real POST to the direct three-way-match endpoint with a genuinely-matching PO+GRN pair succeeds (not a 409), verified with a live test using a real PO/GRN pair rather than the nonexistent-PO shortcut every current test uses | Fixed (PR #1300, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- fixed the stray backslash-escape to `/^procurement_po:/`, matching the other 3 call sites (a 4th, `three-way-match/consumer.ts:86`, was found and confirmed already correct -- 5 total sites, all correct now); added a live-HTTP regression test with a genuinely-seeded matching PO+GRN pair (202, not 409) plus a genuine-mismatch-still-409 case. Independent review reproduced the regex behavior directly in `node`, reproduced the sabotage-check, and ran the full procurement-service suite 3 times to characterize its baseline: 14 files / 31 tests are a genuinely deterministic pre-existing-failure core untouched by this change, but 4 unrelated adapter test files (gem/gepnic/gov-rail-gem/cppp) flake independently of any code change (33/17 vs 28/14 failures across two baseline-only runs) -- confirmed as pre-existing environmental flakiness, not a regression from this fix; flagged separately as its own follow-up so future 'identical baseline' claims aren't taken as byte-exact. `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| DOM-034 | P3 | gap-routes.ts's tax-optimization advisor hardcodes the 80CCD(1B)/NPS headroom -- same bug class as the 80C/80D chain, one section over | Found during DOM-030's independent review: `services/payroll-service/src/modules/payroll/gap-routes.ts:317` hardcodes `headroom: 5000000` (Rs 50,000 in minor units) for its 80CCD(1B)/NPS suggestion, in the exact same function that correctly reads `sec80cCapMinor`/`sec80dCapMinor` from `resolveRunStatutoryConfig(tx, ctx.tenantId, currentMonth)` for the 80C/80D suggestions two lines above (DOM-026 already fixed those two in this file). No `statutory_config` column exists for an 80CCD(1B) cap, so this is advisory-only (a suggestion headroom number, not a real deduction calculation) and was out of DOM-030's own literal scope -- but it means DOM-030's own original claim of fully closing out the payroll-service hardcode chain missed this one | Either add a `sec80ccd1bCapMinor`-equivalent column to statutory_config and resolve it the same way `sec80cCapMinor`/`sec80dCapMinor` already are in this exact function, or record an explicit product decision that the platform-wide statutory NPS cap doesn't need per-tenant configurability the way 80C/80D sometimes do (unlike those two, 80CCD(1B) is a flat statutory ceiling, not typically subject to employer-specific policy overlays -- worth confirming rather than assuming either way). **DoD:** the hardcode is either replaced with a resolved config value, or a documented decision explains why it's intentionally a platform constant | Fixed (PR #1332, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- chose to add `sec80ccd1bCapMinor` (migration 0041) rather than document a platform-constant decision: migration 0038's own stated rationale for 80C/80D being tenant-overridable/effective-dated was avoiding a payroll-service code deploy on a Finance Act change, not employer discretion, and that reasoning is section-agnostic -- applies identically to 80CCD(1B). `resolveRunStatutoryConfig()` (consumer.ts) now selects/maps `sec80ccd1b_cap_minor` alongside its 80C/80D siblings, `domain.ts`'s `StatutoryConfig`/`DEFAULT_STATUTORY_CONFIG` carry the new field (default Rs 50,000, the pre-fix hardcode's own value), and `gap-routes.ts`'s advisor now destructures `sec80ccd1bCapMinor` from the SAME already-fetched config object as `sec80cCapMinor`/`sec80dCapMinor` -- zero extra DB round-trips. `headroom` is the resolved cap itself, not cap-minus-used: unlike 80C/80D, `payroll_tax_declarations` has no column to read a used 80CCD(1B) amount from (confirmed directly against `tax/schema.ts`'s column list -- `section80c`/`section80d`/`hraClaimed`/`rentPaidMinor`/`otherDeductions`/etc., no 80CCD(1B) equivalent), so this mirrors the pre-fix route's own original semantics, just no longer a bare literal. Also removed a second, independent hardcode of the same Rs 50,000 figure embedded in the suggestion's own display string, the same dual-hardcode-in-one-handler bug class DOM-026 fixed for `tax/routes.ts`'s `deductions80C`. New test (`dom-034-tax-optimization-80ccd1b-cap.test.ts`, real Postgres, no mocks) uses a tenant override cap (Rs 80,000) that differs from both the old hardcode and the platform default (both Rs 50,000, same coincidence DOM-026/DOM-030's own tests note) so a hardcoded site and a config-driven site provably diverge; also asserts the suggestion string no longer embeds the cap figure, and that a tenant with no override falls back to the Rs 50,000 platform default. Sabotage-checked: reverting only the `gap-routes.ts` hunk (via a saved `git diff`/`git apply` patch, not `git stash` -- shared `refs/stash` across this host's worktrees) reproduces the exact pre-fix value (received 5000000, expected 8000000) on exactly the test built to catch it, the other 2 stay green; restoring passes all 3 again. Migration 0041 verified directly against a fresh disposable Postgres (`information_schema.columns`: `sec80ccd1b_cap_minor`, `bigint`, default `5000000`) rather than trusting the bootstrap script's own log output, which a foreground-to-background SSH handoff had truncated; separately confirmed 0041 was still a free migration number after fetching `origin/main` (the 3 commits this branch was behind touch no migration files). `pnpm turbo build --filter='./packages/*'`: 29/29 successful. `tsc --noEmit` (payroll-service): clean. Full payroll-service suite on a fresh isolated Postgres (port 32775, never `civitasone-postgres:5435` -- see REL-035): 81/81 files, 1810/1810 tests, run twice, fully green both times -- one unrelated flake on the first run only (`gov-rail-nach.test.ts`'s PII-leak regex against a randomly generated `correlationId` whose hex digits coincidentally formed a 12-digit run; that file is untouched by this diff, confirmed non-deterministic via 5 isolated reruns, all green). `eslint`: 0 errors/0 warnings on all 4 changed `src` files. Provenance: this fix's code was substantially drafted in an earlier session that stalled on infrastructure immediately before verification and was never committed; this session independently re-reviewed the full diff line by line against the DOM-026/DOM-030 pattern, verified two of the draft's own factual claims from source rather than trusting its comments (grepped the full repo for any other consequential 80CCD(1B) hardcode -- the one hit, `apps/web`'s tax-config reference page, is a fully static cheatsheet with no props/state/fetch that also hardcodes 80C/80D/80TTA/Sec 24 as flat reference figures, out of scope and not the same bug class; and confirmed `payroll_tax_declarations` genuinely has no 80CCD(1B) usage column), then ran the full verification pass documented above before committing and opening the PR. Independent review confirmed pattern fidelity against DOM-026's and DOM-030's actual merged diffs, confirmed the second hardcode removal is real, independently re-verified both diligence claims from source rather than trusting them (re-grepped the whole repo for other 80CCD(1B) hardcodes, re-read tax/schema.ts's column list), reproduced the sabotage-check, and ran the full suite twice more on its own disposable Postgres (81/81 files, 1810/1810 tests both times) plus 8 additional isolated reruns of the one-off gov-rail-nach.test.ts flake (never failed again, confirming it's unrelated and non-deterministic). tsc/eslint clean, migration number confirmed free. |

### F. UX, accessibility, i18n, validation (skills 20, 08, 09, 10)

| ID | Pri | Title | Evidence | Fix · Definition of done | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UX-001 | P1 | API failure renders as zero stat cards and "create your first…" empty states | `_data/apiClient.ts:54-114` `fetchJson` never throws → `{empty, source:"error"}`; `hr/payroll/page.tsx:55-60`, `citizen/grievances/page.tsx:56-72`, `finance/budget/revised-estimates/page.tsx:39-44`, `workflow/page.tsx:22-53`; **39** `page.tsx` with `.length === 0` and no error token; `workflow/definitions/page.tsx:15-35` discards `source` entirely | One `useResource`/loader contract that forces loading / empty / error to be handled (error → `ErrorState`, stats hidden); ESLint rule for `.length === 0` without an error branch. **DoD:** the 39 pages + `workflow/definitions` show `ErrorState` on failure; rule enforced in CI | In progress (PR #1127) — shared `useResource`/`<Resource>` contract built (`_data/useResource.ts`, `_components/ds/Resource.tsx`); all 5 named pages fixed plus 6 more of the same shape (11 total); CI ratchet gate added as `scripts/ci/empty-vs-error-guard.mjs` (a guard script, matching this repo convention, not a custom ESLint rule — no such infra exists here) instead of the ESLint rule the gap named. The "39" figure was stale; the guard's first cut also undercounted — an independent review of this PR found its `ERROR_AWARE_RE` check was file-level (an error-aware token anywhere in the file suppressed every empty-check in it), structurally disconnected from the specific empty-check it's meant to gate — e.g. `actions={source === "error" ? <DataSourceBadge/> : null}` on the page header, wired to nothing, next to an ungated `.length === 0` elsewhere in the same file. That false negative hid far more than the 3 pages the review sampled. Fixed: the guard now parses each `page.tsx` with the TypeScript compiler API and requires the error token to share the empty-check's own conditional test, sibling ternary/if branch, or an earlier same-block early-return guard. Corrected counts: **163** violations at the true pre-fix parent commit (not the originally-claimed 61), **155** remaining after this PR's fix, tracked in `scripts/ci/empty-vs-error-baseline.json` (regenerate via `--write-baseline`). Of the 11 pages this PR touched: **8 verified clean by the corrected guard** (audit/cag, audit/investigation, audit/vigilance, citizen/grievances, hr/departments, hr/designations, hr/employee-types, hr/payroll); **2 are correctly fixed but still show as guard violations due to a known, narrow detector limitation** (workflow hub and workflow/definitions use the blessed `useResource`/`combineResourceState` contract correctly, but the detector's connectivity check doesn't yet recognize an enclosing `useResource(`/`combineResourceState(` call as a valid error-gate boundary — tracked as part of UX-013's follow-up rather than re-opening this PR); **1 was legitimately out of this guard's scope** (`finance/budget/revised-estimates/page.tsx` itself has no `.length === 0` check — the real one lives in a sibling client component, outside the guard's `page.tsx`-only scan). Remainder filed as **UX-013** (renumbered from UX-012 to avoid a collision with PR #1126/fix/ux-002, which independently filed its own UX-012). Not yet Fixed — 155 tracked pages still need the same retrofit, plus the detector's `useResource`-boundary blind spot itself needs closing |

The remaining 169/137 are tracked as follow-up gap **UX-016** (filed as UX-013, renumbered — see that entry) with an accurate file list, gated by a baseline in `scripts/ci/raw-status-leak-baseline.json` that can only move down. |

Kept next-intl over `lib/i18n/*` (next-intl already had a working, wired, cookie-driven locale resolution and 8 real call sites; `lib/i18n`'s `LocaleProvider` was never mounted anywhere in the app tree, so its consumers — including the TopBar's language switcher — were silently non-reactive despite looking wired). `lib/i18n`'s richer content (248 keys, 58 real call sites across 5 clerk-facing pages) was merged into next-intl's `src/messages/{en,hi}.json` (now 26 namespaces) and `lib/i18n/*` deleted. `app/layout.tsx` now resolves `lang`/`dir` from `getLocale()`/`dirForLocale()` server-side and wraps the tree in `NextIntlClientProvider`. Real extraction tool at `apps/web/scripts/i18n-extract/` (fleet-wide: 15,613 hardcoded strings / 1,868 of 2,520 files under `src/app` — see UX-017, this session's own honest recount, not the stale 3,484 figure above). First tranche translated (en+hi): citizen grievances page, org home dashboard, finance dashboard, hr leave, admin, and the AskCivitasOne assistant panel. Remaining backlog tracked as UX-017 with a priority-ordered list. **Disclosed correction after independent review**: `lib/i18n/{ta,te,kn}.ts` held real, full-script Tamil/Telugu/Kannada translations (222 lines each, incomplete relative to en/hi per this gap's own original evidence, but genuine translator work product, not stubs) — these were deleted along with the rest of `lib/i18n/*` with no migration path and no disclosure in the original PR body. `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` is now `["en", "hi"]` only. Functionally this changes nothing for a live user (the switcher was silently non-reactive for every locale, ta/te/kn included, before this fix), but the content itself is now gone from the repo. Not restored here — doing so properly means re-verifying the old translations' accuracy/completeness against the new namespace structure, which is out of scope for this infrastructure PR. Added a new `minorToRupeesOrNull()` type guard for direct arithmetic/form-default use on `*Minor` fields (the pattern the 9 unguarded `Number(*Minor)/100` sites need) and applied it to the row's own named repro site (`finance/budget/revised-estimates/page.tsx` + `RevisedEstimatesTable.tsx` — both BE and RE, plus the derived Variance % which used to divide-by-a-fabricated-zero) and one more site proving the pattern generalizes to a different module (`works/proposals/[id]/ProposalEditToggle.tsx`'s cost-edit form, which used to silently pre-fill "0" for a missing `estimatedCostMinor` — an editable field an operator could submit unchanged, quietly zeroing a real proposal's cost). The remaining ~6 of the 9 flagged direct `Number(*Minor)/100` sites are lower-risk (a designer-sandbox preview helper, a TDS quarterly-sum display, an editable procurement-planning input already defensively coded, and a BigInt division that throws on null rather than masking it as zero) and are tracked as **UX-018** rather than folded into this PR. Confirmed `cellValue()` in `DataTable.tsx` is the sole dispatch point for `cellType:"amount"`, with no column pairing `cellType:"amount"` with its own `render:` (which would bypass `formatMoney`) anywhere in the fleet; grepping the fleet found 177 `cellType:"amount"` column occurrences across 84 distinct files — the "82" figure undercounts individual columns but is close to the file count, and doesn't change the single-chokepoint claim, which held up. Re-read the two applied-site diffs (`revised-estimates/page.tsx` + `RevisedEstimatesTable.tsx`, `ProposalEditToggle.tsx`) and confirmed both are correctly wired. Ran all 61 scoped tests myself — pass; `tsc --noEmit` came back fully clean on current `main` (even the 2 previously-noted `CreateGRNForm.test.tsx` errors are gone, presumably resolved by an unrelated later commit). Independently sabotage-checked 2 of the 5 fixed files (`formatters.ts`, `DataTable.tsx`): reverting both reproduced the fabricated-₹0.00 symptom plus a `minorToRupeesOrNull is not a function` break in all 3 downstream consumers (19/61 failed), confirming the tests genuinely exercise the fix; restored, 61/61 pass again. Cross-checked all 4 sites UX-018 cites against this PR's diff and current source — real, accurately described, and genuinely untouched here. Adversarial sweep of `apps/web/src` for other `?? 0` / bare `*Minor`-division money patterns found one site neither this PR nor UX-018 tracks: `_data/apiMappers.ts` — `parseMinor()` (line 46) can't distinguish a missing `totalMinor` from a genuine 0 (both return `0`), and `mapPurchaseOrderSummaries()` (~line 1012, wired live via `loaders.ts:1177`) treats that `0` as falsy, so a purchase order with a missing OR genuinely-zero `totalMinor` gets `amountDisplay: null` and is silently dropped from the list entirely by the `!amountDisplay` filter — not filed as a formal gap here, flagging for separate triage. |

(2) route `browserClient.ts`'s `errorMessageFromResponse` through the same `useFormError`/`toHumanError` catalogue UX-003/UX-016 already established, since it's the same bug class one layer lower in the stack. **DoD:** the guard's regex catches all 6 works/ sites named above as violations (proving the detection gap is closed, whether or not they're fixed in the same PR); catches `record-progress/page.tsx:122`'s "Create failed"). All 6 named works/ sites are now proven caught by new fixture tests (`tests/architecture/raw-status-leak-guard.test.ts`, 17 tests; sabotage-checked by running them against the pre-fix regex in an isolated worktree -- 7 fail red as expected, 10 pre-existing ones still pass). Deliberately did NOT add "Fetch" to the verb family or a bare unprefixed `${status}` interpolation: both were tried during this PR and reverted after a fleet dry run found real false positives (comment prose matching "fetch failed"/"create failed"; the `` `?status=${status}` `` query-string shape the original guard's own doc comment already protects) -- also added a comment-line skip after the same dry run found 2 more false positives there. Fleet-wide live count: 152 (old baseline) -> 261 (the strengthened regex making 109 more real, pre-existing violations visible -- zero new leaks, confirmed by diffing the file:line list, not just the count) -> 260 (browserClient.ts's own fix below incidentally closed its own `API_ERROR: ${res.status}` fallback, one of the 261) -- `raw-status-leak-baseline.json`'s `maxViolations` updated to 260 with this whole jump explained inline; none of the 260 are fixed in this PR, per the DoD's explicit "whether or not fixed here" allowance. Also fixed 2 stale doc comments in `onboarding.ts`/`OnboardingDetail.tsx` that had documented the old raw-echo behavior as an intentional, considered design choice ("surfaced verbatim... never swallowed") -- neither file's runtime code needed to change, both already delegated entirely to `errorMessageFromResponse`. Found fixing REL-010's `list pages show search toolbar` E2E test, which had assumed the aspirational `searchbox`/consistent-name pattern | Change the input to `type="search"`; decide a naming convention (e.g. `Filter <entity>` or `Search <entity>`) and apply it consistently; update `DataTable.test.tsx:64`'s `getByRole("textbox")` to `"searchbox"`. **DoD:** every DataTable filter input has role `searchbox` with a descriptive, page-specific accessible name | `type="search"` plus a new `accessibleFilterLabel()` helper that derives the accessible name from the existing `filterPlaceholder` prop (`"Filter X…"`/`"Filter by X…"` -> `"Search X"`; already-`"Search…"`-worded text and non-matching text, e.g. the Hindi locale strings, pass through unchanged) -- no new prop, no call-site changes, verified against all 243 distinct `filterPlaceholder` strings in live use. 18 of ~290 call sites (17 pages + DataTable's own test file) never set `filterPlaceholder` and fall back to a generic "Search records" name rather than a per-page one -- disclosed residual, not a regression (same generic text they already showed). Updated `DataTable.test.tsx` (role + 3 new tests) and the 2 E2E tests elsewhere that hardcoded the pre-fix role/locator for this input (`e2e/procurement.spec.ts`, `e2e/hrms-payroll/ux-quality.spec.ts`), found by grepping the whole repo for `getByRole("textbox")`; 2 other hits confirmed unrelated (a ConfirmDialog textarea, a form "Name" field) and left untouched. Sabotage-checked; full `apps/web` suite 3070/3071 (the 1 failure is the pre-existing, already-tracked `route-completeness-audit.test.ts` gap, unrelated). Fixed (PR #1284) -- independently reviewed clean (traced accessibleFilterLabel() by hand against edge cases, independently sampled 20+ of the 244 distinct filterPlaceholder values plus i18n/Hindi and dynamic-template cases, confirmed the 18-call-site residual list exactly, sabotage-checked the type=search change, ran the full 3086-test apps/web suite after rebuilding workspace packages). |
| UX-015 | P2 | DataTable row links are named after the first column's raw value, not a human-readable label (filed as UX-013 by PR #1130; renumbered to UX-015 for the same reason as UX-014 above) | `_components/ds/DataTable.tsx:257-263` `rowHref`/`rowLinkKey` tables give the row's anchor `aria-label={\`Open ${row[columns[0].key]}\`}` — whichever field is listed first, regardless of whether it's meant to identify the row to a person. E.g. `procurement/vendors/VendorsTable.tsx` lists `vendorCode` before `name`, so a vendor's row link is announced/named "Open VEN-BEL-001", never the vendor's actual name. Found fixing REL-010's `vendor list shows seeded vendor` E2E test, which had assumed the vendor name itself was the link | Either let a column opt in as the "identifying" column independent of display order, or audit `rowLinkKey`/first-column choices across DataTable consumers so column 0 is always the human-readable field. **DoD:** every `rowHref`/`rowLinkKey` table's row-link accessible name matches the field a person would use to tell rows apart | added an opt-in `identifyingColumnKey` prop to `DataTable` (defaults to `columns[0].key`, so every other consumer is unchanged) and applied it at 16 call sites: `procurement/vendors/VendorsTable.tsx` (the gap's own example) plus `assets/{list,dashboard,fixed-assets,infra,maintenance}`, `hr/employees`, `projects/{dashboard,list,schemes}`, `grants/schemes`, `finance/expenditure/scheme-tracking`, `inventory/list`, `stock/list`, `learning/{courses,page}` -- each pointed at the row's real name/title field instead of its internal code. Audited all 92 `rowHref`/`rowLinkKey` consumers app-wide; record-type tables (invoice/PO/bill/case/ticket/grant/RTI-application/insurance-policy, where the row's own reference number is genuinely how a person identifies it) were left alone as correct, not bugs. A handful of other consumers look similarly mislabeled but have no obvious name column visible to point the prop at -- filed as **UX-021** rather than guessed at here. Sabotage-checked (reverted the `aria-label` line, confirmed exactly the 2 new tests fail, restored); `tsc --noEmit` clean; Fixed (PR #1281) -- independently reviewed clean (spot-checked 6 of 16 fixed sites and 9 of ~76 left-alone sites by reading each one's real columns array, confirmed the backward-compatibility claim precisely -- omitting the new prop evaluates to byte-identical pre-PR behavior -- and independently confirmed UX-021's citations, including reading a full row type to confirm one had no hidden name field to fall back to). |
| UX-021 | P3 | UX-015 likely has more instances beyond the 16 fixed there -- several DataTable consumers label column 0 "Employee"/"Entity"/"Conversation" over what is probably an opaque code/id, with no visibly-identifying alternative column confirmed | Found auditing all 92 `rowHref`/`rowLinkKey` DataTable consumers while fixing UX-015: `estab/quarters/allotments/AllotmentsTable.tsx` (col 0 `employeeShort`, rendered as a truncated monospace code, labeled "Employee") and `estab/quarters/[id]/page.tsx` (col 0 `employeeRef`, same truncated-code pattern) both show a code under an "Employee" label; `hr/payroll/gpf/page.tsx` and `hr/payroll/nps/page.tsx` (col 0 `employeeCode`, labeled "Employee"); `analytics/ml-insights/_components/RecentPredictionsTable.tsx` (col 0 `entityId`, labeled "Entity"); `ai/chat/ConversationsTable.tsx` (col 0 `id`, labeled "Conversation"). Unlike the 16 sites UX-015 fixed, none of these files' visible `columns` arrays contain an obvious human-name column to point `identifyingColumnKey` at -- each row's underlying TypeScript type/mapper needs reading to confirm whether an unrendered name field already exists (e.g. `employeeName`) or whether this is a data/mapping gap, not just a display one. Two lower-confidence, unaudited candidates worth a second look while in the area: `inventory/list/page.tsx` (cycle counts, col 0 is a date) and `inventory/GoodsReturnsTable.tsx` (col 0 is also a date, with only truncated item/store codes as alternatives) | Read each row's TypeScript type/mapper to find (or add) a genuine human-identifying field, then apply UX-015's `identifyingColumnKey` prop the same way; if no such field exists yet, that is a data/mapping gap upstream of DataTable, not just a display one. **DoD:** same as UX-015 -- each listed table's row-link accessible name matches the field a person would use to tell rows apart | Fixed (PR #1336, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- 4 of the 6 named sites fixed, 2 are genuine data gaps documented rather than faked, both lower-confidence candidates investigated and correctly left as false positives. **Fixed:** `hr/payroll/gpf/page.tsx` + `hr/payroll/nps/page.tsx` -- `employeeCode` was never a real code, just `employeeId.slice(0,8)` uppercased; wired payroll-service's existing best-effort hrms-client lookup (`fetchEmployeeSummaries`, the same one `payroll/queries.ts#getSlip` already uses for payslips) into `listGpfReport`/`listNpsReport`, added a real "Employee" name column (old code column kept, relabeled "Code"), pointed `identifyingColumnKey` at the name; falls back to the code when hrms-client has no match, fails open, never breaks the ledger. `estab/quarters/allotments/AllotmentsTable.tsx` + `estab/quarters/[id]/page.tsx` -- both driven by the same `listAllotments` query, whose only employee field was a bare `employeeRef` uuid; estab-service turned out to already have its own cross-service hrms lookup (`shared/hrms-client.ts`'s `getEmployeeDisplayMap`, the same one `modules/files/queries.ts#officerLabel` uses for the officer-of-record on noting/dispatch trails) -- missed on a first, too-narrow grep for "hr-service"/"HR_SERVICE" (the actual filename is "hrms-client.ts") and caught on a second, more careful pass before wrongly writing these two off as data gaps; wired the same way, old truncated-ref column kept, relabeled "Employee ref". **Genuine data gaps, documented not faked:** `analytics/ml-insights/_components/RecentPredictionsTable.tsx` -- `entityId`/`RecentPredictionRow` is fully generic, reused via one shared component (`DomainInsightPage`) across 6 unrelated domains (anomalies, inventory, leads, projects, subscriptions, tickets); no `entityType` or name field anywhere in the loader or its backend (ml-service's `/api/v1/ml/evaluations`), and ml-service has no cross-service enrichment client at all (`src/shared/` has no `*-client.ts`, unlike payroll-service/estab-service) -- a real fix needs per-domain entity-name resolution against 6 different, unrelated services. `ai/chat/ConversationsTable.tsx` -- `ChatConversation.profileId` (ai-agent-service) is always populated (`NOT NULL`, defaults to the authenticated actor's own id, not the often-anonymous field the gap's own evidence suspected) but no citizen/profile-name resolution endpoint or client exists anywhere in the platform (checked citizen-service specifically for an hrms-service-style internal summaries endpoint -- none exists, and no other service calls citizen-service for name enrichment either); worse, `profileId` conflates a citizen's own profile with a staff actor id with no bridge between the two identity spaces -- the same "no identity <-> HR bridge" gap `estab-service`'s own hrms-client.ts doc comment calls out as a separate, larger, out-of-scope platform problem. **False positives, investigated, no bug:** `inventory/list/page.tsx` (cycle counts) and `inventory/GoodsReturnsTable.tsx` -- column 0 is a date, correctly labeled ("Counted"/"Date"), no label/value mismatch; both rows are transactional/audit records (a cycle count, a goods return), not named entities -- the same category UX-015 itself already exempted (invoice/PO/bill/case/ticket/grant, "the row's own reference number is genuinely how a person identifies it"). **DoD:** met for the 4 fixed sites (row-link accessible name matches the field a person would use to tell rows apart, verified by testing both the resolved-name and null-fallback paths); explicitly not met for the 2 data-gap sites, documented above rather than faked; not applicable to the 2 false-positive sites. Verified: payroll-service `tests/statutory-{routes,coverage}.test.ts` 55/55, estab-service `tests/quarters-{routes,consumer,domain}.test.ts` 40/40 + `tests/f5-p0-force-rls-fleet-quarters.test.ts` 3/3 (all against disposable migrated Postgres dbs, never the shared civitasone-postgres:5435, per REL-035), apps/web gpf/nps/estab-quarters test files 13/13 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean in payroll-service, estab-service and apps/web. Independent review confirmed all 4 fixes are genuinely pre-existing helpers with no new N+1 pattern, confirmed both data-gap classifications hold up from source (in the ConversationsTable case, even found a supporting nuance -- packages/types incorrectly types profileId as nullable while the real schema is NOT NULL, explaining why the original filing assumed it was often-null), confirmed both false-positive dismissals, and reran all 3 test suites plus typecheck fresh (55/55, 43/43, 13/13, all exact matches). The 2 documented data gaps (ml-insights entity naming across 6 unrelated domains; chat conversations needing a citizen<->staff identity bridge that doesn't exist anywhere in the platform) are genuinely out of this gap's own reach -- closing on the 4 sites that could actually be fixed, with the 2 platform-level gaps left correctly undisguised rather than faked. |
| UX-022 | P2 | `TaxReturnsSummary`'s annual "Total TDS Deposited" tile has the same missing-vs-masked-zero problem UX-018 fixes per-quarter, but at the whole-`quarters`-array reduce level | Found while fixing UX-018 (PR #1291): `hr/payroll/returns/TaxReturnsSummary.tsx:26` `quarters.reduce((s, q) => s + q.totalTdsDepositedMinor, 0)` and `:45` `inrFmt.format(totalTds / 100)` — if any single quarter's `totalTdsDepositedMinor` is `undefined` at runtime (despite the type), `s + undefined` propagates `NaN` through the whole reduce and the annual tile visibly renders "₹NaN" (reproduced live via UX-018's own sabotage-check); if it's `null` instead, `s + null` silently coerces to `s + 0`, undercounting the true total with no visible error at all — the same silent-masking failure class UX-006/UX-018 close for the per-row case, but here at the aggregate level | Sum via `minorToRupeesOrNull()` per quarter instead of a bare `+`, propagating "missing" through the aggregate (e.g. treat a `null` quarter total as excluding that quarter from the sum, or render "—" for the whole tile if any quarter total is unknown — pick one convention and document it, since silently excluding a missing quarter from a sum is its own kind of masking) rather than letting it silently zero out or poison the sum. **DoD:** a `quarters` array containing one row with a missing `totalTdsDepositedMinor` never renders "₹NaN" and never silently undercounts the annual total with no visible indication; tests cover both the `undefined` (NaN) and `null` (silent-undercount) cases | Fixed (PR #1324, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- annual "Total TDS Deposited" tile now sums via `minorToRupeesOrNull()` per quarter instead of a bare `+`. Convention: if any quarter's total is unknown (null or undefined), the whole annual tile renders "—" rather than silently summing only the known quarters -- chosen to match UX-018's per-quarter answer to the same question (show "—" over a fabricated number), since quietly excluding a missing quarter from the sum is itself indistinguishable from treating it as a real zero. Reproduced both failure modes first as tests against the unfixed component: a quarter with `totalTdsDepositedMinor: undefined` rendered the annual tile as literal "₹NaN"; a quarter with `totalTdsDepositedMinor: null` rendered "₹1,000.00" (silently dropping the unknown quarter's contribution) instead of an error. Tests: `TaxReturnsSummary.test.tsx` -- 3 new (all-quarters-present sum; undefined -> "—" not NaN; null -> "—" not a silent undercount) + 4 pre-existing (UX-018), 7/7 pass. Also scoped 2 of UX-018's own pre-existing unscoped `screen.getByText("—")` assertions to their specific quarter row, since the annual tile now legitimately also renders "—" in those same single-quarter fixtures, making the unscoped query ambiguous -- matches the scoping pattern UX-018's own third test already used for the analogous "₹0.00" case. Sabotage-checked: reverted just the source fix via a saved patch (test file kept; used `git diff`/`git apply`, not `git stash`, which is shared across this host's worktrees), reran -- exactly the 2 new UX-022 tests failed with the predicted symptoms, other 5 passed; restored, 7/7 pass again. `tsc --noEmit` (apps/web): clean. Full `apps/web` unit suite (`vitest run src`): 3169/3170 pass -- the 1 failure (`route-completeness-audit.test.ts`, `/domains` + `/municipal` `loading.tsx` gap) is the same pre-existing, unrelated failure already independently confirmed by UX-006/UX-018/UX-019; this change touches only `TaxReturnsSummary.tsx` and its test file. Not independently reviewed -- Independent review confirmed the UX-018 precedent is genuine (UX-018's own merged commit explicitly pre-files this row), confirmed the source diff and test-scoping changes are legitimate (not weakened assertions), reproduced the sabotage-check and all fresh test/typecheck/full-suite runs exactly. Needed a rebase before merge (a gap-report-only conflict against an unrelated row from concurrent PRs), resolved cleanly. |
| UX-023 | P3 | A third raw-message-echo shape neither UX-020's strengthened guard nor its `browserClient.ts` fix can see: `crm/accounts/[id]/AccountParentForm.tsx` hand-rolls its own fetch + error-message logic instead of calling `browserClient`/`useFormError` at all, and echoes the backend's raw `message` verbatim for any failure code other than the one it special-cased | Found in passing while fixing UX-020 (independent review's own "verify the evidence" pass over `errorMessageFromResponse` callers). `AccountParentForm.tsx:35-46`: `const res = await fetch(...); if (!res.ok) { const body = await res.json().catch(() => ({})) as { code?: string; message?: string }; throw new Error(body.code === "CYCLE_DETECTED" ? "That move would make the account its own ancestor. Pick a different parent." : body.message \|\| "Could not change the parent account."); }` -- the `CYCLE_DETECTED` branch is already clerk-safe (a real, specific, hand-written friendly message), but EVERY other backend failure code falls through to `body.message \|\| "..."`, echoing whatever raw string the backend sent verbatim. This evades both halves of UX-020's fix: `raw-status-leak-guard.mjs`'s strengthened regex only matches a `${res.status}`/`String(res.status)` interpolation or the enumerated `<Verb> failed` literal family -- this code has neither (no status is interpolated or coerced anywhere, and the literal fallback "Could not change the parent account." doesn't match any tracked verb); and it never imports or calls `browserClient.ts`'s `errorMessageFromResponse` at all, so that fix's blast radius doesn't reach it either -- it is a fully separate, hand-rolled implementation of the same bug class. A quick `grep` for the surface shape (`res.json().catch(() => ({}))` outside `browserClient`/`useFormError`) turns up ~47 candidate files, but spot-checking 5 shows most already interpolate `` `${res.status}` `` alongside `body.message` (e.g. `admin/config/page.tsx:37`, `admin/gateway-config/page.tsx:74`, `IntegrationDrawer.tsx:148,169`) -- already caught by this PR's strengthened guard, not a new detection gap. `AccountParentForm.tsx` is distinct because its fallback has NO status text or tracked verb at all, just a bare `body.message`. Whether other files share exactly THAT bare-fallback sub-shape is unconfirmed -- would need one-by-one triage, not a mechanical bulk grep, since the guard already covers the status-bearing majority | Route through `useFormError` (or `toHumanError` directly) the same way the rest of the fleet does, keeping the existing `CYCLE_DETECTED` friendly copy as the `fieldErrors`-equivalent special case if desired, but replacing the `body.message \|\| "..."` fallback with catalogued copy. Also worth a quick fleet grep for the same hand-rolled `res.json()` + raw `.message` fallback shape outside `browserClient`/`useFormError` before assuming this is the only instance. **DoD:** no raw backend `message` is shown to the user for any failure on this endpoint's PATCH action | Fixed (PR #1325, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- routed AccountParentForm's PATCH error handling through useFormError, matching the fleet convention (see ProposalActions.tsx); CYCLE_DETECTED keeps its existing hand-written friendly copy as a special case (peeked via res.clone() so formError.fromResponse can still independently read the original response for every other code). New regression test asserts the raw backend message never reaches the user for a non-CYCLE_DETECTED failure (JSON body, non-JSON body, and a network exception), while CYCLE_DETECTED still shows its specific copy -- 16/16 green. raw-status-leak-guard.mjs: 211 violations, unchanged (this file never tripped it, before or after -- a detection gap, not a guard regression). typecheck clean. This endpoint's own DoD is met; the row's own broader "worth a fleet grep for the same bare-fallback shape elsewhere" suggestion was not re-run by this fix (the original filing's own 5-file spot-check already found the other ~47 candidates mostly already guard-covered, with the bare-fallback sub-shape's fleet-wide prevalence still unconfirmed) -- worth a follow-up sweep if this class recurs. Independent review verified res.clone() correctness by source inspection (confirmed it's the first operation on res, per Fetch spec semantics guaranteeing the original body stays unconsumed for fromResponse's own independent read), confirmed CYCLE_DETECTED's message and trigger are byte-identical to before, reproduced all 3 regression test scenarios, and reran the guard (211, file absent from violations), typecheck (120/120 monorepo-wide), and crm/accounts suite (16/16) fresh. |

| COMP-013 | P1 | `platform-admin/roles` renders a hardcoded role list and permission matrix with a save that always reports success | `apps/web/src/app/(app)/platform-admin/roles/RolePermissionsMatrix.tsx`: `ROLES` (9 hardcoded role defs, line 7) and `DEFAULTS` (a hardcoded module×action permission baseline, line ~28) are the page's entire data source — no loader, no GET call anywhere in the file. `saveChanges()` does `await fetch(".../admin/role-permissions", {method:"PUT",...}).catch(() => null)` — the `.catch` swallows any failure (network error, 4xx, 5xx alike) and the code always proceeds to clear the draft and show a success notice regardless of what happened. Found by the COMP-004 detector fix (PR #1116 fix-up) — the PR's own description incorrectly claimed this file was "already caught by the detector and spun off as a follow-up"; it was not caught (proven: `node scripts/contract/screen-map.mjs` on the pre-fix detector reported 0 FABRICATED_DATA repo-wide). Same bug class as the sibling `admin/roles` page COMP-004 already fixed — that page's real backend (`identity-service/src/modules/rbac/routes.ts`: `GET/POST/DELETE /identity/rbac/roles`, `/roles/:id/permissions`) is UUID/DB-row based (roles + permissions tables, `role_permissions` join), not the string role-key / module:action-key shape this page's UI assumes, so wiring this page isn't a drop-in reuse of that pattern — it needs either new permission rows seeded to match the module×action keys this UI uses, or the UI rebuilt around the existing RBAC primitives (role.key, permission.key) | Rebuild the page against `identity-service`'s real RBAC routes: load roles via `GET /identity/rbac/roles`, load each role's permissions via `GET /identity/rbac/roles/:id` (already returns `{...role, permissions}`), seed `permissions` rows for the module×action combinations this UI needs (8 modules × 6 actions, matching `DEFAULTS`' key space) via a migration/seed script, and save changes via `POST/DELETE /identity/rbac/roles/:id/permissions/:permissionId` per toggled cell (no bulk endpoint exists — loop per change, same as `admin/roles`'s pre-fix pattern before COMP-004). Fix `saveChanges()`'s swallowed-error `.catch(() => null)` to surface real failures. **DoD:** page loads real per-tenant role/permission state, a failed save shows an error (not a fake success), `screen-map.mjs` reports this screen WIRED not FABRICATED_DATA, entry removed from `screens.contract.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_FABRICATED_DATA_EXCEPTIONS` | Fixed (PR #1161) -- rebuilt the page against identity-service's real RBAC routes (same admin-service endpoints COMP-004 wired for admin/roles): module/action grid now derived from real permission keys, each role's grants loaded from the backend on mount, saveChanges() surfaces real per-role failures instead of the swallowed `.catch(() => null)`. Added scripts/seed-platform-admin-rbac.mjs to seed the 9 roles + 48 permissions (8 modules x 6 actions) + 139 baseline grants reproducing the old DEFAULTS constant as real rows, since the RBAC store starts empty for a tenant; idempotent, live-verified against the dev DB. Found and fixed a live bug along the way: queries.ts/commands.ts passed the raw `db` export into `repo.findRoleById`/`findPermissionById`/`findRoleByKey`/`permissionKeysForRole` instead of `scopedRead`, so those reads had no RLS tenant GUC set and always returned zero rows -- `GET /identity/rbac/roles/:id` 404'd for every real role, and every grant/revoke/assign/revoke command 404'd too, silently breaking both COMP-004's and this fix's live save path despite passing mocked tests; fixed by wrapping each in `scopedRead`, live-verified before/after against a standalone build of the branch's identity-service. |

| PERF-001 | P0 | Fleet connection demand is ~7× Postgres `max_connections`; PgBouncer configured nowhere | `ecosystem.config.js`: 64 `svc()` + 65 `worker()` = 129 processes; neither helper (lines 324–370) sets `DB_VIA_PGBOUNCER`/`DB_POOL_MAX`; `packages/db/src/pool.ts:96` max 10; 19 services open a second scanner pool → ~1,480 vs `infra/postgres/postgresql.conf:7` `max_connections = 200`; via PgBouncer 129×5 = 645 > `infra/pgbouncer/pgbouncer.ini:9` `max_client_conn = 500`; live host: 11/11 processes direct on 5432 | Route every process through PgBouncer (`DB_VIA_PGBOUNCER=true`, `:6432` URLs in `svc()`/`worker()`), raise `max_client_conn`, size `default_pool_size` per DB, cap `DB_POOL_MAX`; capacity doc. **DoD:** full fleet boots with `pg_stat_activity` < 200; documented budget | Fixed (PR #1098) — transaction-mode pooling, `server_reset_query_always=1` closing a real tenant-isolation leak found in review (raw `SET app.tenant_id` in 2 migrations, independently reproduced and confirmed closed from scratch).
