| PERF-002 | P1 | 271 tenant tables have no `tenant_id` index under RLS (2026-09-07 audit, from migration text); a fresh live-cluster scan on 2026-09-08 (`scripts/ci/tenant-index-guard.mjs`, this fix) measured 263 before this PR and 254 after — the fleet grew (SEC-002 alone added RLS to 21 tables) faster than the backlog was worked | First tranche fixed 11 tables: finance ledgers `finance_ap_ledger`, `finance_ar_ledger`, `finance_bank_reconciliation`, `finance_debt` (all 4 named in the original evidence, all PK-only); revenue `assessees`, `assessments`, `dcb_entries`, `rate_heads`, `rate_slabs`, `remissions` (all 6 named, all PK-only); works `work_proposals` (named, 42-col PK-only). Also added the `(tenant_id, created_at)` composite the evidence called for on the 12 widest tenant tables by column count (0/12 had one before): `work_proposals` plus 9 hrms tables (`hrms_employees`, `hrms_assessment_attempts`, `hrms_applications`, `hrms_job_openings`, `hrms_offers`, `hrms_candidates`, `hrms_requisitions`, `hrms_contractor_bills`, `hrms_leave_policy_rules`), `crm.contacts`, `citizen.service_definitions` — all already had a leading tenant_id index, so this part was additive-only | `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` migrations, ledgers and revenue first; CI guard: new tenant table without a tenant index fails. **DoD:** 0 tenant tables unindexed; guard in place | Fixed (PR #1122, reviewed 2026-09-12) — first tranche only, deliberately scoped (271→254-table backlog too large for one reviewable PR of index migrations on live-shaped tables); the 254-table remainder was always PERF-016's separately-tracked scope, not a remaining part of PERF-002 itself. `scripts/ci/tenant-index-guard.mjs` added, ratcheting against `scripts/ci/tenant-index-baseline.json` (254 tracked entries at merge time, now 200 after PERF-016 tranche 1) so CI fails on any *new* unindexed tenant table without blocking on the existing backlog. Remaining tables tracked in **PERF-016** (renumbered from an earlier draft's PERF-015 — that ID collided with PERF-003's own, independently-filed PERF-015 follow-up, which merged first). **Status-closure review (2026-09-12):** re-verified from scratch on an isolated, disposable cluster (not the PR's own claims) — all 11 named migrations confirmed present with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` and `tenant_id` as the leading column, all 12 `(tenant_id, created_at)` composites confirmed; bootstrapped a fresh cluster end-to-end (0 migration failures fleet-wide) and ran `tenant-index-guard.mjs --report`: all 11 tables show 0 missing, all 12 composite tables show `created_at composite=true`, PASS with 173 pre-existing (unrelated) baseline entries remaining, 0 new. Ratchet mechanism sabotage-verified both directions on that same cluster — an injected unindexed RLS tenant table is caught and fails the build (exit 1, flagged NEW), and a baselined entry given a real index but left listed is also caught and fails (exit 1, flagged stale) — both cleanly revert to PASS once corrected, confirming the gate is real, not a no-op. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `integration-tests` job genuinely invokes the guard (grepped, not decorative); note it has not actually executed on `main` recently (including on this PR's and PR #1192's own runs) because upstream `Tests`-job failures cause `needs: [test]` to skip `integration-tests` — an unrelated, already-tracked flakiness (REL-022 family), not a defect in this guard or its migrations, and independently worked around here by verifying directly against a live cluster rather than trusting a CI badge. Confirmed no double-counting: none of these 11 tables appear in PERF-016's baseline (old 254-entry or current 200-entry). PERF-016 tranche 1 (PR #1192, works+hrms, 54 tables) independently confirmed real. Adversarially checked the guard script for scan gaps (schema-qualification, partitioned-table handling) — declarative partitioning is handled correctly: the guard's `relkind='r'` filter skips only the partitioned parent, and each partition (itself `relkind='r'`) is still individually checked, so no partitioned tenant table is silently missed |
| PERF-003 | P1 | 60/66 HTTP services exit uncleanly on SIGTERM; compose prod probes a static `/health` | Only court, finance, inspection, meeting, visitor, works handle SIGTERM in `src/index.ts`; `infra/docker-compose.prod.yml` 33 healthchecks on `/health` (static `ok`, `packages/observability/src/index.ts:516`), 0 on `/ready` | Shared graceful-shutdown helper (`app.close()`, `sql.end()`, drain) in `packages/observability`; `kill_timeout`/`wait_ready` in PM2 (REL-012); compose → `/ready`. **DoD:** rolling restart with 0 severed requests; DB-down → probe fails | Fixed (PR #1121 + follow-up PR #1205, both reviewed) — first tranche of 6 (gateway, identity, hrms, payroll, revenue, procurement — traffic-critical/state-sensitive: auth path, payroll/HR, procurement) wired to the shared `registerGracefulShutdown()`/`signalReady()` helper (REL-012's `packages/observability/src/lifecycle.ts`), `{graceful:true}` set in `ecosystem.config.js`, compose healthchecks → `/ready` for the 5 of those 6 with a compose block. Live SIGTERM-during-in-flight-request verified (not just `pm2 status`, which fails open per REL-012's own finding) on 2 services + a sabotage check proving the verification catches a broken wiring. **Correction (independent review + PR `fix/perf-003-followup`, 2026-09-12): the verification above did not exercise a genuine keep-alive hang, and the underlying claim did not hold** — a realistic keep-alive-client test reproduces an 8s hang, not a clean exit. Root cause: Fastify 4.29.1's `forceCloseConnections` defaults to `'idle'` on Node ≥19 (this fleet runs Node 22), which sweeps idle keep-alive sockets exactly ONCE, at the instant `app.close()` runs; a socket genuinely mid-request at that instant that goes idle a moment later (ordinary keep-alive — the normal condition for any persistent client: another service, a monitoring probe) is never revisited, so shutdown hangs for the full 8s `forceExitMs` and force-exits (exit 1). Verified `forceCloseConnections: true` is NOT a safe fix: on Node ≥18.2 (checked against the installed Fastify 4.29.1 / Node 22 sources) it maps to `server.closeAllConnections()`, which destroys every tracked socket unconditionally — including ones actively sending a request or awaiting a response — trading the hang for severed in-flight responses. **Actually fixed (PR `fix/perf-003-followup`):** `packages/observability/src/lifecycle.ts`'s `registerGracefulShutdown()` gained an optional `server` option (Fastify's `app.server`) that, when supplied, re-runs Node's own in-flight-safe `closeIdleConnections()` on an interval (default 200ms) for the duration of shutdown — closing the one-shot gap without ever touching a socket mid-response. Wired via a one-line `server: app.server` addition into all 12 services now using the shared helper: this row's original 6 (gateway, identity, hrms, payroll, revenue, procurement — `revenue-service` was missed in an earlier pass and has now been included) plus court/finance (REL-012) and PERF-015 tranche 2's inspection/meeting/visitor/works. Re-verified with a real `http.Agent({keepAlive:true})` client (not one-shot connections) against a real DB-backed authenticated endpoint on identity-service, hrms-service and procurement-service — ~40 concurrent requests each, all completing with clean 200s across SIGTERM, process exit in 39–990ms (vs. the 8s timeout). Sabotage check (hrms-service, using an external Postgres `LOCK TABLE ... ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` to force a deterministic in-flight window immune to timing jitter): with the fix reverted, the identical test reproduced an 8038ms hang (matching `forceExitMs`) while the same in-flight requests still completed correctly; restoring the fix brought it back to 3476ms (bounded by the test's artificial lock, not the bug). **58/66 services still have no shutdown handling at all — unchanged, still tracked in PERF-015 (that gap's own next tranches should apply the same `server: app.server` wiring from the start, not just `registerGracefulShutdown()`/`signalReady()`, to avoid repeating this same keep-alive gap).** Independent review of `fix/perf-003-followup` (2026-09-12) found nothing new -- confirmed the Node/Fastify semantics directly from installed source, reproduced the live keep-alive test on 2 services, built its own independent deterministic sabotage harness (8033ms hang reverted / 2466ms fixed), tested 5 adversarial `server`-argument edge cases (all graceful), and confirmed clean typecheck/build/lint across all 13 touched packages. This row's last blocking issue is resolved; row closed |
| PERF-004 | P1 | SQS consumers have no retry backoff; DLQ arrivals are silent | `services/queue-service/src/bus.ts:457` fixed redelivery until `SQS_MAX_RECEIVE_COUNT`; 0 `ChangeMessageVisibility`/`DelaySeconds`; `routeToDlq` (`:722-765`) emits no counter/alert | Exponential backoff via `ChangeMessageVisibility` (1s→60s per skill 07); `dlq_total` metric + alert rule. **DoD:** poison-message test shows backoff and an alert | Fixed (PR #1125, reviewed 2026-09-12) — re-verified the evidence against current `main` before fixing: `routeToDlq` already called `incrementDlqMessage(topic)` (added by an earlier, unrelated OPS-1 PR after this gap was filed), so the "0 counter" half of the evidence was stale; the "0 `ChangeMessageVisibility`" half was still accurate (confirmed 0 hits repo-wide). Fixed: `pollTopic`'s failure branch now calls `ChangeMessageVisibility` with an exponential backoff (`computeBackoffSeconds()` — base 1s, factor 2, capped 60s per skill 07, keyed off `ApproximateReceiveCount`, overridable via `SQS_BACKOFF_BASE_SECONDS`/`SQS_BACKOFF_MAX_SECONDS`); the existing DLQ counter was renamed `dlq_messages_total{topic}` → `dlq_total{topic,reason}` (grepped first — nothing else in-repo referenced the old name) to match skill 07's metric spec and add the missing `reason` breakdown; added the `DlqMessagesAppearing` alert on `increase(dlq_total[5m]) > 0` in `infra/observability/alert.rules.yml`. New regression suite `services/queue-service/tests/perf-004-dlq-backoff.test.ts` (6 tests, fully-mocked SQS client per the existing 05-T4 pattern) asserts the actual `ChangeMessageVisibility` calls/timeouts (`[1,2,4]`, strictly increasing), the eventual DLQ with `dlq_total` incremented, that a message succeeding after backoff is still processed exactly once (1 delete, no re-delivery), and that the alert rule's own threshold is satisfied by a recorded arrival. Sabotage-checked both the backoff loop and the metric increment (removed each in turn — 2 and 1 tests failed respectively — then restored, all 6 green). Full `queue-service` suite green 3× (84/87, 3 pre-existing LocalStack-gated skips when unset) plus green with `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL` unset; `tsc --noEmit` clean for `queue-service` and `observability`. **Not independently verified against live LocalStack** — `civitasone-localstack` was pinned near its 1GiB memory cap by other concurrent host activity during this work (confirmed via `docker stats` and a timed-out health check) and the pre-existing `sqs.localstack.test.ts` timed out for unrelated infra reasons; the new suite's fully-mocked client exercises the identical `pollTopic`/`routeToDlq` code path without that dependency. Filed **PERF-017** for the identical missing-backoff pattern in the RabbitMQ adapter (`services/queue-service/src/adapters/rabbitmq.ts:161`), out of scope for this gap's own evidence (which cites only the `SqsQueue` class). **Status-closure review (2026-09-12):** independently re-verified the above against current `main` (PR #1125 already merged) in an isolated worktree. Confirmed `computeBackoffSeconds()` (`bus.ts:457`) is genuinely exponential (base x factor^(attempt-1), capped, `attempt = max(1, floor(receiveCount))`) and correctly keyed off `ApproximateReceiveCount` rather than a fixed/never-reset counter; confirmed the `SQS_MAX_RECEIVE_COUNT` boundary is handled correctly -- `pollTopic` checks `receiveCount >= maxReceiveCount` and routes to DLQ *before* the backoff branch, with `continue` short-circuiting it, so a message cannot back off forever past the threshold. Ran `perf-004-dlq-backoff.test.ts` independently: 6/6 pass. Independently sabotage-checked both claimed points rather than trusting the PR's own sabotage run: neutralizing the exponential-growth formula failed 3 tests (the pure-curve unit test plus both integration tests -- a superset of the claimed 2, since it also breaks the direct `computeBackoffSeconds` assertions); neutralizing `incrementDlqMessage` failed 2 tests (poison-message-DLQ test + alert-threshold test) once the dependent `@civitasone/observability` package was rebuilt so the source edit actually propagated to its compiled `dist/` (which is what resolves at runtime, not `src/` directly) -- an initial attempt without rebuilding gave a false "0 tests affected" result, caught and corrected before relying on it. Ran the full `queue-service` suite: 84/87 passed, 3 skipped (`sqs.localstack.test.ts`), matching the claim exactly; `tsc --noEmit` clean for both `queue-service` and `observability`. Confirmed PERF-017 and PERF-018 both exist as their own tracked rows in this report. **Real LocalStack verification: partially obtained, and stronger than the mocked suite alone on the boundary check.** The shared `civitasone-localstack` container is Docker-healthy but still pinned at 1022MiB/1GiB (99.7%) memory -- the same root-cause condition PERF-018 documents -- so, consistent with that gap's own restraint around shared infrastructure, it was left untouched; a fresh, isolated `localstack/localstack:3` container was started instead (its own port, `--memory=3g`/`--memory-reservation=768m`, healthy within 2s). The literal pre-existing `sqs.localstack.test.ts` still failed against it (0 messages received across all 3 of its tests) -- root-caused to a genuine but **pre-existing and unrelated** race, not a PERF-004 regression: `resolveSubscriberQueues()` (`bus.ts:611-623`, authored 2026-08-08, over a month before this PR, untouched by its diff) negatively-caches an empty `ListQueues` subscriber list for 15s, and because `start()` is deliberately non-blocking (`BOOT-NONBLOCK`), a `publish()` called immediately after `start()` on a cold (never-before-created) topic -- exactly this test file's own pattern -- reliably loses that race regardless of LocalStack's speed. Confirmed by reproducing the failure, then confirming a several-second delay between `start()` and `publish()` avoids it. Out of scope for PERF-004 itself (untouched code predating it) and not fixed by this review; flagged here since it is currently untracked elsewhere in this report and a follow-up gap may be warranted. Working around it with two small standalone probe scripts (not committed; run against the isolated LocalStack and deleted afterward), a real end-to-end backoff-to-DLQ cycle was captured against genuine SQS semantics: 3 delivery attempts at `SQS_MAX_RECEIVE_COUNT=3` with increasing inter-attempt gaps (1615ms, then 3000ms -- consistent with the 1s to 2s curve plus real long-poll/network overhead), correct DLQ routing exactly on the 3rd attempt using the broker's own authoritative `ApproximateReceiveCount` (not a mock's scripted value -- a strictly stronger check of the boundary condition than the fully-mocked suite alone can provide), and `dlq_total` correctly incremented. The isolated container was removed afterward; the shared `civitasone-localstack` container was never touched. **One residual, non-blocking finding:** the repo-wide grep claimed before the metric rename ("nothing else referenced the old name") missed `infra/observability/grafana/dashboards/civitasone-overview.json:2540` (panel "DLQ Messages Total (rate/5m)"), which still queries `rate(dlq_messages_total[5m])` and will silently show no data post-deploy. The alerting path is unaffected -- `infra/observability/alert.rules.yml`'s `DlqMessagesAppearing` alert correctly targets `dlq_total`, so the paging safety net this gap's DoD actually depends on is intact -- so this is a one-line dashboard-query fix left for a follow-up rather than made here (out of scope for a docs-only status update). No other CRITICAL/HIGH issues found; closing this gap. |

| PERF-012 | P2 | On-prem/Helm PgBouncer deployment cannot start — wrong env var names | Found while fixing PERF-001: `infra/onprem/helm/civitasone/templates/pgbouncer.yaml`'s pgbouncer container sets `DATABASE_HOST`/`DATABASE_PORT`/`DATABASE_USER`/`DATABASE_PASSWORD`, but the `edoburu/pgbouncer` image's entrypoint only recognizes `DB_HOST`/`DB_PORT`/`DB_USER`/`DB_PASSWORD` (or `DATABASE_URL`) — none of those names match, so `DB_HOST` is unset and the container fails its own `${DB_HOST:?...}` startup check; `tests/infra/helm-pgbouncer.test.ts` asserts the (wrong) env var names are rendered, so it doesn't catch this. This is the on-prem/Kubernetes deployment path, not the EC2 PM2 fleet PERF-001 fixed — also, that chart uses a single shared `civitasone` DB role fleet-wide (`values.yaml`'s `config.DATABASE_USER`), a materially different auth model than the per-service-role EC2 fleet, so this isn't a one-line rename; needs its own investigation | Rename the container's env vars to match the edoburu image's actual contract; decide the auth model (single shared role vs. per-service, and how PgBouncer authenticates upstream — see `docs/architecture/CONNECTION-BUDGET.md` §3b for the pattern PERF-001 used for the EC2 fleet). **DoD:** `helm template` output boots a real pgbouncer pod against a real Postgres; a `helm-pgbouncer.test.ts` update that would have caught this | Fixed (PR #1326, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14, 2 rounds) -- three real bugs found and fixed, only one the gap's own literal ask: (1) env var names renamed to the edoburu image's real contract (DB_HOST/DB_PORT/DB_USER/DB_PASSWORD, not DATABASE_*), sourced from the same ConfigMap/Secret keys every other service reads; DB_NAME deliberately left unset so the entrypoint defaults to the wildcard database entry, since this one shared role must reach every per-service database. (2) the pinned image tag `edoburu/pgbouncer:1.21.0` doesn't exist on Docker Hub (never published without a `-pN` suffix) -- ImagePullBackOff regardless of the env var fix; repinned to `v1.23.1-p2`, matching PERF-001's already-fixed docker-compose pgbouncer. (3) AUTH_TYPE `md5` can't authenticate to a scram-sha-256-only Postgres (14+'s own default) -- fixed to `scram-sha-256`. Auth-model decision: kept the existing single-shared-role model as-is rather than adopting PERF-001's per-service-role+AUTH_QUERY pattern, since that pattern exists specifically to avoid a hand-maintained credential per role across 65+ distinct roles -- a problem this one-role chart doesn't have; moving on-prem to per-service roles would be a materially larger, unrequested change with no corresponding problem to justify it. `helm-pgbouncer.test.ts` updated (2 corrected assertions -- the original 6 tests all passed against the buggy template -- plus 3 new ones: full DB_* env contract with negative assertions the old DATABASE_* names never return, the image tag, and AUTH_TYPE), sabotage-checked (reverting each fix in turn fails the corresponding new assertion, 4 unrelated tests stay green). Verified beyond static rendering: booted a real, isolated edoburu/pgbouncer:v1.23.1-p2 container and proxied real queries through it against two different database names via the wildcard route, confirming DB_NAME-unset routing and scram-sha-256 auth both work against a real, unmodified Postgres. helm lint and a full default-values helm template both clean. Round 1 review confirmed the 3 originally-claimed fixes from first principles (pulled the real edoburu/pgbouncer entrypoint.sh, confirmed the image tag 404s on Docker Hub, corroborated AUTH_TYPE against this repo's own PERF-001 writeup) but found a 4th, unaddressed bug via real container testing: the container's actual listening port defaults to 5432 (LISTEN_PORT was never set), while containerPort/Service targetPort/both probes all target 6432 -- the pod would never pass its liveness probe or receive traffic in a real cluster. Fixed in a follow-up commit (LISTEN_PORT: "6432" added, 10th test added). Round 2 independently reproduced the fix live (container logs and netstat confirm listening on 6432 post-fix, 5432 pre-fix; caught and worked around a Docker userland-proxy false-positive on the pre-fix host-mapped-port check by verifying via direct container-IP connect instead), reproduced the sabotage-check, confirmed the 3 round-1 fixes remain untouched, and swept the whole chart for any other port mismatch (none found). All 4 bugs now confirmed resolved. 10/10 tests pass. |
| PERF-013 | P2 | New tenant-isolation CI guard (`raw-session-guc-guard.mjs`, added by PERF-001) has real false-negative gaps | Independently confirmed by hand-crafting adversarial inputs: the guard misses `SET app.tenant_id TO '...'` (the standard alternate Postgres syntax), a `SET` split across lines, raw SQL built via string concatenation, and `set_config(..., someVariable)` where the third arg isn't a literal `true`/`false`. Does NOT reopen the actual tenant-isolation leak PERF-001 fixed — `server_reset_query_always=1` protects at the connection-reset level regardless of which SQL syntax produced the leaked state — but weakens the guard's ability to catch a *new* instance of the pattern before merge | Broaden the guard's detection beyond the one literal `SET x =` token it currently matches — cover the syntax variants above. **DoD:** guard catches all four variants in a new adversarial test | Open |
| PERF-014 | P2 | PgBouncer's `auth_query` (added by PERF-001) fails closed on a genuinely fresh bootstrap — undocumented manual grant required | Independently discovered setting up an isolated repro: every database hits `ERROR: permission denied for table pg_authid` under PgBouncer's `auth_query = SELECT rolname, rolpassword FROM pg_authid WHERE rolname=$1` until `GRANT SELECT ON pg_authid TO civitas_admin` is run manually per-database — nothing in this repo's bootstrap SQL grants this anywhere, the same "worked on the dev host from an undocumented manual step" pattern this repo has caught and fixed for itself before (see `bootstrap_admin_role.sql`'s own header). Fails loud (connection refused), not a silent leak, but means PERF-001's live verification almost certainly ran against a host with this grant from earlier undocumented setup, not a clean bootstrap | Add the `GRANT SELECT ON pg_authid TO civitas_admin` (or the correct minimal grantee) to the bootstrap SQL. **DoD:** a genuinely fresh `bootstrap-postgres.sh` run produces working PgBouncer auth with no manual step | Fixed (PR #1342, merged; independently reviewed 2026-09-14) -- added `GRANT SELECT ON pg_authid TO civitas_admin` to `bootstrap_admin_role.sql`, matching PERF-001's actual `auth_user`/`auth_query` config verbatim. Non-obvious gotcha found and fixed along the way: `pg_authid` is a shared catalog (rows are cluster-wide) but `GRANT`/`REVOKE` on it writes to the *connected database's* own ACL, confirmed empirically — granting while connected to `civitas_test` left the grant invisible from `postgres` on the identical cluster. PgBouncer's `auth_dbname` is `postgres`, so the fix explicitly `\c postgres` before the `GRANT`, independent of whatever `$PGDATABASE` the invoking bootstrap run uses (this script's own documented throwaway-container example overrides it to `civitas_test`, which would otherwise silently miss the grant). Verified end-to-end, genuinely fresh containers each time: pre-fix, a real PgBouncer instance running the actual `auth_query` config failed with the exact `permission denied for table pg_authid` error for every role with no static `userlist.txt` entry (i.e. every one of the 65+ service roles -- `civitas_admin` itself has a static entry and never invokes `auth_query` at all, so its own login was never actually affected, a narrative detail the independent review corrected); post-fix, `civitas_admin`, `finance_svc` and `hrms_svc` (neither in PgBouncer's `userlist.txt`) all authenticated through the real PgBouncer with no manual grant step. Independent review independently reproduced the per-database-ACL claim from scratch (own test, two databases on one cluster, confirmed pg_authid's rows are byte-identical shared-catalog data while pg_class.relacl differs per database) and reproduced the bug/fix end-to-end with real PgBouncer against the real merged auth_query config. |

Sabotage check (inspection-service): reverted to the old ad-hoc process.on(SIGTERM) handler, rebuilt dist/, forked dist/index.js exactly as PM2 does in fork mode with an IPC channel -- the ad-hoc handler never sent the PM2 wait_ready 'ready' IPC message (5s timeout, nothing received), which is precisely the operational bug this fix closes: under real {graceful:true}+wait_ready, PM2 would eventually hit listen_timeout and mark the process errored even though it is actually serving traffic. Restored the fix, rebuilt, reran the identical fork test -- ready received within milliseconds of listen() resolving. **54 zero-handler services remain untouched and out of scope for this PR, tracked as the next PERF-015 tranche.** **Note (PR `fix/perf-003-followup`, 2026-09-12):** the shared `registerGracefulShutdown()` helper these 4 services were ported onto here had its own separate bug (see PERF-003's row) — it hung for 8s under ordinary keep-alive traffic instead of exiting promptly. | PERF-017 | P2 | RabbitMQ adapter has the identical missing-retry-backoff pattern PERF-004 fixed for SQS | Found while working PERF-004 (out of scope there — that gap's evidence cites only the `SqsQueue` class): `services/queue-service/src/adapters/rabbitmq.ts:161` requeues a failed message via `ch.nack(sqsMsg, false, true)` with no delay — immediate, fixed-rate redelivery until `deliveryCount >= this.maxRetries`, same as the pre-fix SQS behavior. RabbitMQ has no native visibility-timeout equivalent to extend; the standard fix is a delayed-exchange/dead-letter-with-TTL requeue pattern (republish to a per-attempt TTL queue that dead-letters back to the live queue after the backoff interval) rather than a single API call like `ChangeMessageVisibility` | Route the retry requeue through a delayed re-publish (TTL-based dead-letter-exchange hop, or the `rabbitmq_delayed_message_exchange` plugin if available) keyed off `deliveryCount`, same 1s→60s exponential curve as PERF-004/skill 07. **DoD:** poison-message test on the RabbitMQ adapter shows increasing delay between redeliveries, not a fixed rate | Fixed (PR #1212) — independently reviewed clean (reproduced the TTL+DLX topology via the RabbitMQ management API, independently reproduced the x-delivery-count bug against the real old adapter code — 1,870 delivery attempts, deliveryCount stuck at 1 throughout — and ran an independent sabotage check) — checked for `rabbitmq_delayed_message_exchange` first (`rabbitmq-plugins list` against a disposable `rabbitmq:3-management` container): not present in the standard image, and this repo provisions no RabbitMQ deployment anywhere (`infra/docker-compose*.yml`, `infra/onprem/` — zero hits), so used the TTL + dead-letter-exchange "parking lot" pattern instead (already this file's own pattern for the permanent-failure DLQ) — no non-standard plugin required. Failed-but-retryable messages are now acked off the main queue and republished into a per-delay retry queue (`{queue}.retry.{seconds}s`, one per distinct bucket for attempts 1..maxRetries-1) whose `x-message-ttl` is the backoff delay and whose dead-letter-exchange routes directly back to the originating queue (not the topic's fanout exchange, which would incorrectly re-fan the retry to every other subscribed service) once the TTL elapses; same exponential curve as PERF-004 (`computeBackoffSeconds`, reused from `bus.ts` — base 1s, factor 2, capped 60s), resolved via new `RABBITMQ_BACKOFF_BASE_SECONDS`/`RABBITMQ_BACKOFF_MAX_SECONDS` env vars. `dlq_total{topic,reason}` now gets a real reason (`unparseable_body`, `invalid_envelope`, `non_retryable_error`, `max_retries_exceeded`) from every RabbitMQ DLQ path instead of defaulting to "unknown"; the existing `DlqMessagesAppearing` alert already covers this adapter (the metric is shared with `SqsQueue`), so no new alert was needed. Also found and fixed, out of the gap's own evidence: the `x-delivery-count` header this adapter read to decide DLQ-vs-retry was never written anywhere — classic RabbitMQ queues don't auto-populate a delivery count the way SQS maintains `ApproximateReceiveCount` — so `deliveryCount` was permanently stuck at 1 and the retry path could never actually reach the DLQ via retries; fixed as a side effect of owning the republish (`requeueWithBackoff` stamps the header itself on every hop). New `services/queue-service/tests/perf-017-rabbitmq-backoff.test.ts`: 3 pure env-var-resolver tests plus 2 tests against a REAL, disposable RabbitMQ instance (gated on `RABBITMQ_URL`, same skip-cleanly convention as `sqs.localstack.test.ts`/`AWS_ENDPOINT_URL`) — a poison-message test asserting strictly-increasing wall-clock gaps across the actual 4 redeliveries (not fixed-rate) and the eventual DLQ with `dlq_total` incremented, and a fails-twice-then-succeeds test proving exactly-once processing across the backoff hops. Sabotage-checked: reverting the retry branch to the original `ch.nack(sqsMsg, false, true)` produced 134 near-instant redeliveries instead of 4 with increasing delay, correctly failing both new integration tests; restored, both green. Verified against a real, disposable, isolated `rabbitmq:3-management` container on host-confirmed-free ports (this host runs no shared RabbitMQ at all) — new suite green 2x with a live broker (89/92), full `queue-service` suite green 3x without one (87/92, the 2 live tests skip cleanly). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `eslint` clean (one pre-existing, unrelated `isFifoTopic`-unused warning). **Not yet independently reviewed** — per this section's own rule, a different agent/session review is the exit criterion before this is marked Fixed |
| PERF-018 | P1 | Shared dev fleet's LocalStack (SQS backing for `QUEUE_DRIVER=sqs`) is memory-capped and has been reporting `unhealthy` for its entire uptime; any queue-publish-dependent request hangs indefinitely under load | Discovered building PERF-010's k6 suite: `docker ps` showed `civitasone-localstack` as `Up 11 days (unhealthy)`, `docker stats` showed it pinned at 1021MiB/1GiB (99.66%) memory; `POST /identity/sessions` (which calls `queue.publish` -> SQS `SendMessage`) went from a normal ~1.2s response to a 100%-hang at k6's 30s HTTP timeout under as little as 2 concurrent requests, reproduced twice. Not a code bug in identity-service or `@civitasone/queue` — the dependency itself is degraded. Affects every gap-closure session on this shared host that exercises a real async publish path (silently, since a hang looks like slowness, not a clean error), not just PERF-010's own k6 run | Raise LocalStack's container memory limit (`infra/docker-compose.yml`'s `localstack` service currently has none set in the version inspected — confirm and add one sized for the SQS queue count/volume this fleet actually produces) and/or restart it once sized correctly; add a health-check alert so a future recurrence pages instead of silently hanging every dependent request. Restarting shared infrastructure on a host other concurrent sessions depend on is an ops call, not something to do unilaterally mid-gap-fix — left running (degraded) rather than restarted by this PR. **DoD:** `docker ps` reports `civitasone-localstack` healthy under real fleet-wide SQS traffic for a sustained period; a repeat of this gap's own repro (N concurrent `POST /identity/sessions`) completes within a reasonable latency, not a hang | Fixed (worktree fix/perf-018-localstack-memory) — root cause: `infra/docker-compose.yml`'s `localstack` service had no `mem_limit` defined, yet the live shared container was running under an undocumented, ad-hoc `docker update`-applied 1GiB cap (host-side drift, not IaC) pinned at 1021Mi/1Gi (99.7%) RSS. `civitasone_localstackdata` volume is <1MB on disk, ruling out disk-backed message/data buildup -- the memory pressure is in-process bookkeeping for the fleet's 1,483 SQS queues across 31 services, not a leak or growing backlog. Under that pressure, `docker exec ... awslocal sqs list-queues` itself hung indefinitely when tried live during this investigation, reproducing the gap's own queue.publish-hang symptom outside k6. Fix: added explicit `mem_limit: 3g` / `mem_reservation: 768m` to the `localstack` service in both `infra/docker-compose.yml` (dev fleet, the gap's target) and `infra/docker-compose.prod.yml` (proactively, same undocumented-cap risk class). Verification: `docker compose config` confirms the fields resolve correctly (3221225472 / 805306368 bytes); rather than restart the live shared container (other concurrent gap-closure sessions depend on it -- same restraint this gap's own notes call for), validated against a disposable standalone `localstack/localstack:3` container with the identical `--memory=3g --memory-reservation=768m` limits: created 300 synthetic SQS queues (20% of the fleet's real count) under parallel load, memory peaked at ~1.05GiB (35% of cap) during the creation burst and settled at 442MiB idle, `/_localstack/health` responded 200 in 3-8ms throughout (vs. the live container's 5s healthcheck timeouts), then torn down. **Not fixed / left as follow-up**: the live shared `civitasone-localstack` container itself still runs under the old ad-hoc 1GiB cap until an operator recreates it from the updated compose file (`docker compose up -d localstack`) or applies `docker update --memory=3g --memory-reservation=768m civitasone-localstack` -- deliberately not done unilaterally by this fix, since it is shared infrastructure other concurrent sessions on this host depend on right now. |

## 5. How to work a gap

1. **Load the governing skill(s)** named in the section header before touching code, and skill 16's "How to run this audit" for the verification bar.
2. **Claim it** by editing the Status cell (`In progress (PR #…)`) in a docs-only PR, or in the same PR as the fix.
3. **Work in an isolated worktree** (`git worktree add /home/ec2-user/wt/<gap-id> -b fix/<gap-id> origin/main`). Never edit the primary checkout.
4. **Fix, then prove it:** regression test that fails before and passes after (sabotage-check it — break the fix, watch the test fail, restore), full service suite 3× on a fresh isolated Postgres container, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
5. **Open a PR** whose body cites the gap ID and this report; **never self-merge a code fix** — an independent review from a different agent/session is required, and a review that finds nothing new is the exit criterion.
6. **Close it** by setting Status to `Fixed (PR #…)`. If a fix reveals a new gap, add it with the next free ID in its section rather than widening the existing one.
7. **Do not weaken a control to make a gap disappear** (e.g. deleting a failing gate, lowering a threshold, marking a test skipped). REL-001's allow-list and REL-003's `|| true` are the cautionary examples.

---

## 6. Coverage and limits

What the audits did **not** examine, so a clean cell above must not be read as coverage: runtime behaviour on the live host (no servers started; no `pg_stat_activity`, no `pg_policies` — RLS findings are from migration text), Flutter app internals beyond route/feature counts, `packages/*` and `infra/` beyond the files cited, the ~50 smaller services' module bodies beyond grep-flagged files, screen content for the 572 `NO_LOADER` screens, runtime contrast/focus/keyboard behaviour, the 24 env-gated tests' behaviour under nightly, SQS per-topic `VisibilityTimeout`, RabbitMQ/Kafka adapters, and actual bundle sizes (no build run). Scanner limits are recorded in TX-011 and SEC-014.

Sources: seven audit transcripts (security; transactional/data integrity; domain/functional; product completeness; UX/a11y/i18n/validation; performance/reliability; release/QA), each run in its own read-only worktree at `e43e28d8` and removed afterwards. Prior context: `docs/QUALITY-SCORECARD.md` (2026-07-27), `docs/FINAL-UAT-GAP-REPORT.md`, `docs/QA-FINDINGS-BACKLOG.md`, `.claude/skills/16–21`.
